**Viewing a carousel reuses the identical scroll-snap idiom a second time, in `PrispevekProhlizec.tsx`, plus a small dot indicator that tracks the active slide via a plain `onScroll` handler (`Math.round(scrollLeft / clientWidth)`) — not an `IntersectionObserver` like the feed's own active-post tracking, because this is a small, bounded, local carousel rather than a virtualized scrolling list, so the simpler mechanism is the right one here, not a reused-for-its-own-sake copy of the feed's.** A single-photo post renders exactly the same JSX it always did (`vsechnaMedia.length > 1` gates the whole carousel branch), so there's no visual or behavioral change for the overwhelming majority of existing posts. The double-tap-to-like handler stays on the outer wrapping `div`, firing regardless of which slide is showing — a touch-driven horizontal scroll never fires a synthetic `click`, so swiping between slides and double-tapping to like don't fight over the same gesture.

**A new `layers` icon (two overlapping filled squares, matching Instagram's own carousel badge) marks a multi-item post wherever a single static thumbnail is shown and could otherwise look like the whole post — a profile grid tile, and the feed.** The grid badge sits in the same top-right corner `.social-prispevek-video-znacka` already used, and takes precedence over it (a carousel's first item being a video doesn't also show the plain video ▶ badge, matching Instagram's own choice to prioritize "this is a carousel" as the more useful piece of information). The feed, unlike the grid, never lets a carousel actually swipe in place — vertical swipe between *posts* already owns that gesture there, and layering a second, horizontal swipe *within* one post would fight it — so `FeedPrispevek.tsx` only shows the same badge (styled as a small glass circle matching its other floating action buttons) as a hint that opening the full detail (`PrispevekProhlizec.tsx`, via the existing comment-icon tap) shows the rest.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation**: a post's own owner adds two more items to their carousel and `nacti_prispevky()`/`nacti_feed()` both return all three in the correct `position` order; an ordinary single-photo post still returns `dalsi_media: []` (no behavior change for existing posts); a different account is rejected outright when trying to add media to someone else's post. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): a post seeded with two extra `dalsi_media` items shows the carousel badge on its grid tile (and a plain post next to it does not); opening it renders all three slides and three dot indicators. **A second spec** confirmed the upload dialog itself: picking two files opens a real multi-file chooser (`isMultiple() === true`) and shows two thumbnails in the scroll-snap preview strip before publishing. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**Sharing a post into a chat, the last item this pass, reuses `messages.story_id`'s exact shape — one more nullable reference column, `shared_post_id`, no new table.** Unlike a story reply, though, the bubble genuinely resolves and shows the shared post's thumbnail rather than a generic label — sharing a post is specifically about the recipient seeing it, so the label-only restraint documented for story replies (the story usually vanishes before the reply is read) doesn't carry over here: a shared post persists, so a real preview is worth the extra query. `nacti_jeden_prispevek(post_id)` is a small `SECURITY DEFINER` function, the single-post sibling of `nacti_prispevky`/`nacti_feed`/`nacti_ulozene_prispevky` (same `dalsi_media` shape, so a shared *carousel* post's thumbnail is just its first item, consistent with every other single-thumbnail context in this session), gated by the same `smim_videt_prispevek()` every other post read already uses.

**The re-check happens on every read, not just at share time, the same non-retroactive-vs-ongoing distinction this file keeps re-deriving (Secret Chat's blocking enforcement, Uloženo's saved-post visibility): a recipient who could never see the sharer's private account, or who gets blocked by the post's author after the share already happened, sees "Příspěvek už není dostupný" instead of a preview — the share itself isn't undone, but it stops working the moment visibility no longer holds.** `ZpravaSdilenyPrispevek` in `ChatView.tsx` follows the exact fetch-and-render shape `ZpravaMedium`/`ZpravaHlasovka` already established in the same file (a small self-contained component owning its own `useEffect`, not inline logic in the message-map loop) — and, like a media message, its preview is hidden behind the same "message-request media stays locked until accepted" gate documented above, since an unsolicited shared post from a stranger is the same category of thing an unsolicited photo already is.

**`SdiletPrispevekDialog.tsx` only offers already-accepted friends (`stav.pratele`), not the full follow graph or search — sharing is meant for people you already talk to, the same scope `PratelePanel.tsx`'s own "Napsat" button already has.** It follows `NahlasitDialog.tsx`'s small-dialog shape (`.social-overlay` + `.social-dialog`, no `createPortal`) rather than `PrispevekProhlizec.tsx`'s own full-screen shape, consistent with `SledujiciDialog.tsx` before it — a short, dismissible list is not a screen-filling surface, and doesn't risk the stacking-context trap documented for the actual full-screen viewers. Tapping "Poslat" calls `sdiletPrispevekDoChatu()` (thin wrapper: `otevritChat()` then `poslatZpravu(..., sharedPostId)`, the identical two-step shape `reagovatNaStory()` already established for stories) and flips that one row to "Odesláno ✓" rather than closing the dialog — sharing the same post with more than one friend in one sitting is a real use case a close-on-first-send would have blocked.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation**: a genuine share lands a message with `shared_post_id` set in the recipient's chat; the recipient (a public post) resolves the real preview via `nacti_jeden_prispevek()`; blocking the recipient after the share makes that same function return nothing for them, without touching the message row itself; `anon` is rejected outright (a permission error, not an empty result, since the function has no `anon` grant at all) and can't call it via RPC either. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): opening the share dialog from a real post and tapping "Poslat" on a real friend fires the expected `POST /rest/v1/messages` with `shared_post_id`/`chat_id` set correctly and flips the row to "Odesláno ✓"; a separately seeded message with `shared_post_id` renders a real thumbnail and "Zobrazit příspěvek" in the chat view. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**This closes every item from the second Social-improvement round** (comment replies plus the visibility bug it caught, comment likes, post carousels, and sharing a post into a chat) — the same verification discipline as the first round throughout: real production Supabase impersonation for anything touching RLS, a temporary Playwright spec deleted after passing, `npm test`/build kept clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected.

**Editing a post's caption after publishing surfaced a genuinely different class of RLS bug than anything caught so far this session — not a missing `SELECT` policy this time, but Postgres's own documented requirement that an `UPDATE`/`DELETE` target row must *also* satisfy some applicable `SELECT` policy on the table, if the table has any `SELECT` policy at all.** `posts` already had exactly one, narrow `SELECT` policy (`jsem_moderator() and exists (select 1 from reports ...)`, added for the report-a-post feature) — so the moment a real `UPDATE` policy (`upravit popisek vlastního příspěvku`, `user_id = auth.uid()` on both `USING` and `WITH CHECK`) was added, every `UPDATE` — including the post's own owner editing their own caption — silently matched zero rows, because the only `SELECT` policy that existed was the moderator one, and an ordinary owner editing their own unreported post never satisfies it. `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, VERBOSE)` on the failing `UPDATE` showed the real combined filter (`user_id = auth.uid() AND jsem_moderator() AND ...`) — Postgres was AND-ing the new `UPDATE` policy with the pre-existing `SELECT` policy, not OR-ing them, because internally an `UPDATE` first has to "see" its target rows the same way a `SELECT` would. The fix, once understood, was a genuinely new (not previously needed) `SELECT` policy — `"vidět vlastní příspěvek"`, `user_id = auth.uid()` — which reveals nothing new (an owner can already read their own posts through `nacti_prispevky(own_id)`, this only makes the same fact reachable via a raw `PostgREST` query too) but satisfies Postgres's requirement that *some* `SELECT` policy cover the row before `UPDATE` can touch it.

**This is worth recording as its own lesson, distinct from the "subquery against `posts` needs a `SECURITY DEFINER` wrapper" lesson repeated many times above: that one is about a *different* table's RLS reaching into `posts` without elevation; this one is about `posts`' own `UPDATE` policy being silently vetoed by `posts`' own, unrelated, narrow `SELECT` policy — a trap that specifically only appears once a table that has *no* general `SELECT` policy gains its *first* `UPDATE` policy.** Caught by testing immediately after adding the policy, the same discipline that already caught the `post_media`/`post_likes`/`post_comments` versions of the sibling mistake — not discovered by `get_advisors`, which has no way to flag "this policy will conflict with that one," only genuine impersonation testing does.

**`posts.edited_at` mirrors `messages.edited_at` exactly — no version history, just a timestamp `ChatView.tsx`'s own precedent already established the display convention for.** The four `SECURITY DEFINER` functions that already read `posts` (`nacti_prispevky`, `nacti_feed`, `nacti_ulozene_prispevky`, `nacti_jeden_prispevek`) all needed another `drop`+`create` round to add the one output column — the fourth time this session a `posts`-reading function's return shape changed, each time for the same reason (`CREATE OR REPLACE` can't change output columns). `upravitPopisekPrispevku()` only ever sends `{ caption, edited_at }` in its `UPDATE` payload, never `media_path`/`media_type`/`user_id` — RLS itself can't restrict *which* columns an `UPDATE` touches (Postgres has no column-level RLS), so the real boundary here is client discipline, the same "trust the owner with their own row's other columns, RLS just keeps out everyone else" reasoning `messages`' own broad self-row `UPDATE` policy already relies on.

**On the client, `PrispevekProhlizec.tsx` holds its own local `popisek`/`editovanoAt` state seeded from the `prispevek` prop, rather than waiting for the parent list (grid, feed, saved posts) to refetch** — the prop itself is an immutable snapshot handed down by whichever screen opened the viewer, and none of those screens re-fetch just because the viewer is still open, so a save has to update what's on screen immediately rather than depend on a round trip back up through props. The pencil-icon edit affordance only renders for `jeMoje`, next to the caption text, matching where Instagram's own caption-edit entry point sits; "(upraveno)" appears the same way `ChatView.tsx`'s own edited-message tag does, right after the text rather than as a separate line.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation**: a post's own owner successfully edits their caption and sees it (plus a set `edited_at`) through `nacti_prispevky()`; a different account's attempted edit leaves the row completely untouched; `anon` is rejected outright (a permission error surfaces from the RLS-required `jsem_moderator()` check inside the combined policy evaluation, confirming `anon` can't reach the row via any path, not just an empty result). **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): editing a real post's caption fires the expected `PATCH /rest/v1/posts` with the new `caption`, and the viewer immediately shows the new text and "(upraveno)" with no reload. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**"Vypnutí kdo tě vidí online" turned out to need no new privacy plumbing at all — just a self-toggleable `profiles.skryt_online` column and a check inside the existing heartbeat, both following patterns this file had already fully worked out.** `skryt_online` needed no new RLS policy the same way `soukromy` didn't — it's one more plain column on a row the owner already has a working self-row `UPDATE` policy for, from the same profile-editing family as name/motto/bio. `zaznamenejPritomnost()` (the heartbeat `presence.ts` calls every 25 s) now checks the flag before every upsert, not just once at `startPresence()`'s own startup — the same "check what's current, not what was true at launch" instinct `resolveActiveRoleId`/`resolveActiveThemeId` already apply to expiring role/VIP state, applied here to a setting a user can flip mid-session.

**Flipping the toggle on doesn't just stop *future* heartbeats — it deletes the *current* `presence` row immediately, because otherwise "online" would keep showing for up to `JE_ONLINE_PRAH_MS` (two missed heartbeats) after the user asked to hide it.** `presence` had never needed a `DELETE` policy before (nothing ever removed a row — a stale timestamp aging past the freshness threshold was always how the whole feature signals "gone," by design); adding one for `smazatVlastniPritomnost()` didn't repeat the `posts`-style "`UPDATE` needs a satisfying `SELECT` policy" trap documented above, because `presence` already has a working self-row `SELECT` policy (`user_id = auth.uid() or …`) from when online/offline first shipped — the new `DELETE` policy simply had something to combine with, unlike `posts`, which had only the moderator-scoped one at the time its `UPDATE` policy was added.

**The toggle lives in the existing "Soukromí" card in `/nastaveni`, a second row below "Soukromý účet," not a new card** — both answer the same underlying question ("what do other people get to see about me"), and the card's own subtitle already frames it that way.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation**: the account's own `skryt_online` update succeeds and reads back correctly; a genuine self-row `presence` delete succeeds; a different account's attempted delete of someone else's `presence` row leaves it untouched. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): flipping the switch fires the expected `PATCH /rest/v1/profiles` with `skryt_online: true`, a `DELETE /rest/v1/presence` fires in the same action, and the switch/toast reflect the new state immediately. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**Zvýrazněná story ("Highlights") give a story a second, permanent life on the profile — a story still expires in 24 h and gets cleaned up exactly as before, but adding it to a highlight COPIES its `media_path`/`media_type`/`caption` into a brand-new, independent row rather than referencing the original.** Two new tables, `story_highlights` (id/user/name) and `story_highlight_polozky` (the copied media, one row per item, ordered by `position`) — a copy, not a reference, on purpose: coupling a highlight to the live `stories` row would mean `smaz_expirovane_stories()`'s existing, already-trusted cleanup would have to somehow know not to break a highlight, or a highlight would need its own exemption logic threaded through a mechanism that was never designed to have exceptions. A copy needs none of that — the two features simply never interact again after the copy happens.

**Highlight visibility deliberately copies POST visibility (`smi_videt_prispevky_uzivatele`), not story visibility (mutual friends only) — a real broadening, matching what Instagram itself actually does.** A highlight is part of the permanent profile, seen by whoever can see the profile's posts (a public account: anyone; a private one: mutual friends), not scoped to "whoever could see the story while it was live." The new `story_highlight_polozky` SELECT policy leans on this directly and safely: `exists (select 1 from story_highlights h where h.id = story_highlight_polozky.highlight_id)` — a bare, unguarded existence check that looks dangerously permissive in isolation, but isn't, because that subquery runs with the *querying user's own* RLS applied to `story_highlights` too (the referenced table already has a correct, matching policy) — the safe half of the RLS-subquery-elevation lesson this file has now hit both ways: a subquery against a table with **no** applicable policy for the querying role needs a `SECURITY DEFINER` wrapper (as `posts`' own policies keep needing), but a subquery against a table that **already has** a correct policy just inherits it for free, exploited here deliberately from the start rather than discovered as a bug afterward.

**Adding a story to a highlight is one function, not two separate client calls — the same orphan-row avoidance shape `zaloz_chat()`/`pridatStory()` already established.** `vytvoritZvyrazneniZeStory()` inserts the highlight row, then the first item; if the second insert fails, it deletes the highlight it just created rather than leaving an empty, photo-less circle sitting in the profile's highlight row forever. `autor_zvyrazneni(highlight_id)`, a small `SECURITY DEFINER` helper, backs the INSERT/DELETE policies on `story_highlight_polozky` the same way `autor_prispevku()` already backs `post_media`'s — checked against `anon` immediately after creating it (both the RLS-triggered permission error on `story_highlights` and the helper's own revoked grant), the discipline this file has needed to apply by habit after catching the same missing-revoke mistake several times before.

**The client reuses the exact visual language `StoryProhlizec.tsx` already established rather than inventing a second full-screen viewer style — `ZvyrazneniProhlizec.tsx` renders the identical `.social-story-*` classes** (progress dots, tap-left/right regions, header/close/delete buttons), just simplified: a highlight name instead of an author, no auto-advance timer (browsing a permanent collection isn't a race against a 24 h clock the way a live story is), no "who viewed," no reply/reaction row. Deleting a highlight's last remaining item deletes the whole highlight along with it, rather than leaving an empty circle with nothing inside it to open.

**A real click-through test caught a genuine, previously-latent stacking bug shared with two already-shipped features, not just something new to this one.** `PridatDoZvyrazneniDialog.tsx` (opened from `StoryProhlizec.tsx`'s own new ✨ button, `jeMoje`-only) uses the same small `.social-overlay`/`.social-dialog` shape as `NahlasitDialog.tsx`/`SdiletPrispevekDialog.tsx` — but this was the first time that dialog shape was opened from *inside* an already-portaled `.social-story-prohlizec` (z-index 60, opaque black background), and `.social-overlay`/`.social-dialog`'s own z-index (40/41) sat well below it. Both portals attach as siblings directly under `document.body`, so stacking is decided purely by z-index regardless of DOM order — the dialog rendered fully hidden behind the opaque viewer, unclickable and invisible to a real user, while still passing a naive `toBeVisible()` check (which doesn't test actual pixel-level occlusion by a sibling element, only CSS visibility/size). Caught only because this feature's own Playwright spec tried to actually click a button inside the nested dialog and the click was reported as intercepted by the story viewer underneath. The exact same latent bug already existed for `NahlasitDialog`/`SdiletPrispevekDialog` opened from inside `PrispevekProhlizec.tsx` (which reuses the identical `.social-story-prohlizec` class) — never caught before because no earlier test had tried clicking through that specific nesting. Fixed at the general level, not with a one-off override: `.social-overlay`/`.social-dialog` moved to z-index 65/66, above every `.social-story-prohlizec`-shaped full-screen viewer in the app, fixing the latent case along with the new one.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation, the same three-shape check this file applies to every new relationship surface**: the owner creates a highlight plus its first item in one flow; a non-friend viewing a *public* account can see both the highlight and its item (confirming the deliberate "post visibility, not story visibility" broadening); a stranger cannot insert an item into someone else's highlight; once the owner sets their account private, that same non-friend viewer's visibility drops to zero; `anon` is rejected with a permission error (not silent emptiness) reading `story_highlights`, and `autor_zvyrazneni` isn't callable via anon RPC. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): a seeded highlight renders a real photo obálka on the profile page and opens correctly in the viewer with a non-zero rendered size (the same rendering discipline that has caught real bugs before, this time also the z-index one above); creating a new highlight from an open own-story fires the expected `story_highlights`/`story_highlight_polozky` inserts and shows the "Zvýraznění vytvořeno." toast, with the dialog now genuinely clickable, not merely present in the DOM. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**Hashtags — the last item on the "postupně vše" backlog, chosen over a general discovery feed because it slots into the existing search screen instead of inventing a whole second ranking/discovery surface, and needed no schema change to `posts` at all.** `post_hashtags` (`post_id`, `hashtag`, composite PK) is a new, genuinely small table populated automatically, never written to by a client at all — `extrahuj_hashtagy()`, an `AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE OF caption` trigger on `posts`, parses `#word` tokens out of the caption with a plain `regexp_matches(..., 'g')` loop (capped at 30 tags per post, defensive against a caption someone deliberately stuffs with hundreds of `#`s), lowercases each, and re-derives the full set on every caption edit (`delete` then re-`insert`, not a diff) — the same "current text only, no history" simplicity this session already applied to edited messages and post captions themselves. Czech diacritics survive intact (`#škola`, `#čeština`) because `[[:alnum:]]` in a regex follows the database's own UTF8 collation, not a hardcoded ASCII-only character class — confirmed directly, not assumed.

**`post_hashtags` carries RLS enabled with *zero* policies, the same "default deny, only reachable through a narrow function" posture `audit_log` already established — nothing, not even the post's own author, can read the table directly, only through the trigger (writes) or `hledej_podle_hashtagu()` (reads).** This was verified the interesting way, not just asserted: an impersonation test's own *direct* `select count(*) from post_hashtags` as the post's own owner came back `0` immediately after an insert that had genuinely worked (confirmed moments later by the search function finding the row, and by a superuser-context read outside any impersonated role showing the real rows) — not a bug in the trigger, proof the "no policy = truly nobody, not even the author" boundary holds exactly as designed, including against the person who wrote the row.

**`hledej_podle_hashtagu(dotaz, pocet)` is a `SECURITY DEFINER` function shaped like every other post-reading function this session has built (`nacti_prispevky`/`nacti_feed`/`nacti_ulozene_prispevky`) — same `dalsi_media` carousel-thumbnail shape via the identical `left join lateral` pattern, same `smi_videt_prispevky_uzivatele(p.user_id)` visibility check per row (not per-caller-once, since results can span many different authors), same explicit `auth.uid() is not null` belt-and-suspenders guard alongside the visibility helper's own internal one.** It accepts the query with or without a leading `#` (`regexp_replace(trim(dotaz), '^#', '')`) so the client never has to decide whether to strip it. Checked against `anon` immediately, the discipline this file now applies by habit rather than by accident after catching the missing-revoke mistake several times before: rejected outright with a permission error, and `has_function_privilege('anon', ...)` confirmed `false` before anything client-side was built on top of it.

**On the client, `VyhledavacPanel.tsx`'s one search box now does double duty — a leading `#` switches it from name search to hashtag search, no separate toggle or second input.** `hledanoHashtag` is a small piece of state frozen at the moment a search actually completes, not derived live from the current `dotaz` text — typing over the query after seeing results (before submitting again) must not silently reinterpret what's already on screen. A hashtag match renders as the same `.social-prispevek-dlazdice` grid tile every other post grid in this app already uses (profile, feed, Uloženo), opened through the same `PrispevekProhlizec.tsx` — one more entry point into an already-shared component, not a new viewer.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation**: the trigger correctly extracts multiple hashtags (including Czech diacritics) from a real insert and re-derives them on a caption edit; a stranger finds a public account's post by hashtag; the same search returns nothing once that account goes private and the searcher isn't a mutual friend; `anon` is rejected outright on the search function and isn't itself able to call it via RPC; direct table reads of `post_hashtags` return nothing for anyone, author included, confirming the zero-policy design. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): searching `#vylet` renders a real, correctly-sized grid tile and opens it to show the matching caption; a hashtag with no matches shows the empty-state message rather than the name-search one. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

### Social nav rework, Fáze 1 of 5 — Blokovaní/Hlášení moved to the app's own /nastaveni

**Agreed as a five-phase plan, discussed phase-by-phase before any code per this session's own standing process for large redesigns — Fáze 1 (this one, done), Fáze 2 (a Hub→Social/Chat transition animation, picked from a preview before being built as a reusable hook), Fáze 3 (reframing `DomuPanel.tsx` toward Reels/Stories — its own project, planned separately when reached), Fáze 4 (`Hub.tsx`'s own bottom nav pulled into a shared, route-aware component reused across pages), Fáze 5 (swipe/arrow navigation between modules, client-only, no library, building on Fáze 4).** Only Fáze 1 is built so far; 2–5 wait on an explicit go-ahead each, same discipline as every other multi-phase build this session (Souboj, Secret Chat, the IG-style profile redesign).

**Social's own `NastaveniPanel.tsx` — the two-row Blokovaní/Hlášení menu that used to live behind Social's fourth bottom-nav tab — moved into the app's real `/nastaveni` as a new "Sociální nastavení" card, and the bottom nav lost that tab entirely, down to three (Chaty/Domů/Vyhledávač) plus the existing Profil link-out.** The motivation was naming, not function: the app already has one real "Nastavení" (`SettingsModule.tsx`), and Social having its own separate screen under the identical name was the kind of duplication this session's own Hub/Profil reworks have repeatedly closed elsewhere (Cloud backup out of the Hub, the friends list off the profile page and back, logout relocated) — one "Nastavení," not two.

**`NastaveniPanel.tsx` itself didn't change — what changed is who renders it, and that surfaced a real bundle-weight question this session had already solved once before for the identical shape.** `SettingsModule.tsx`, unlike `SocialModule.tsx`/`GameModule.tsx`, is not behind `React.lazy` — `App.tsx` imports it eagerly, the same as `ProfilModule.tsx`. A plain top-level import of `NastaveniPanel` (which needs the full `useSocial()` hook — friends, chats, requests, not just the couple of small calls `SettingsModule.tsx` already made for the Soukromí card) would have pulled that weight into the app's main bundle for every visitor, whether or not they ever open Social — exactly the mistake `PratelSekce.tsx`/`ProfilSocialniSekce.tsx` were built to avoid earlier this session. The fix is the identical shape: a new `pages/setting/components/SocialniNastaveniSekce.tsx`, loaded via `React.lazy` from `SettingsModule.tsx`, holding its own independent `useSocial()` instance (the same accepted "second round trip if a user visits both screens in one session" cost already paid there) and — because `NastaveniPanel`/`BlokovaniPanel`/`ModeracePanel` all draw from `.social-*` classes defined in `SocialModule.css`, which `/nastaveni` never otherwise loads — an explicit `import '@/social/SocialModule.css'`, the exact fix this file already documents once for the same class of bug (`ProfilSocialniSekce.tsx`'s stat/tab/grid CSS move). Confirmed via the actual build output, not assumed: `SocialniNastaveniSekce` lands in its own small chunk (under 2 KB gzip), not folded into the eager `index-*.js`.

**The new card sits in `SettingsModule.tsx` right after "Soukromí" and before "Zálohování dat"** — same topical family (what Social-adjacent state the account carries), reusing the page's own `settings-card` header shell (icon/title/subtitle) around the lazy-loaded content, the same wrapping shape "Zálohování dat"/"Administrace" already use for their own async or conditional bodies. Inside the card, `NastaveniPanel`'s own menu-then-full-screen-section behavior (its "← Zpět do nastavení" button, `BlokovaniPanel`/`ModeracePanel`) is completely unchanged — it just now nests one level deeper, inside a `settings-card` instead of being the whole screen.

**Verified with `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` (all clean — confirmed via the build output that the new chunk stayed separate from the main bundle) and a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): Social's bottom nav renders exactly 4 items (Profil, Chaty, Domů, Vyhledávač) with no "Nastavení" anywhere in it; `/nastaveni` shows the new "Sociální nastavení" card, its lazy content actually resolves past the loading fallback to show "Blokovaní"/"Hlášení," clicking "Blokovaní" opens the section with a working "← Zpět do nastavení" button. `auth.spec.ts`'s four cases unaffected (no auth-gate routing touched).

### Social nav rework, Fáze 2 of 5 — Modulový přechod (Hub → Social/Chat)

**Picked from a live preview, not described in text — a temporary Artifact showed all six candidate transitions (Posun/Prolnutí a zvětšení/Najetí zezdola/Zvětšení z bodu/Prolnutí s rozostřením/Vrstvený posun) driving an actual phone-mockup replica of the Hub and Social screens, replayable back and forth before committing to one.** "Posun" (plain slide-push: the entering screen slides fully in from the right, the leaving screen only partly slides left and dims) won. Building the throwaway comparison first, rather than just picking from a written description, is what let the choice be about how it actually *feels* to tap through, not a guess from adjectives.

**`useModulovyPrechod()` (`core/navigation/`) is the reusable hook the plan asked for, not code glued into one button — built on the browser's own View Transitions API (`document.startViewTransition`), zero library, matching this session's own "zdarma, žádná knihovna navíc, appka to umí napsat ručně" instinct documented for Fáze 5 of this same plan.** The hook wraps `useNavigate()`: call `prejit('/social')` instead of `navigate('/social')` and the browser snapshots the current page, runs the navigation, snapshots the new page, and cross-fades/animates between the two snapshots per CSS — the hook itself never touches what the transition looks like, only that one runs.

**`flushSync()` inside the `startViewTransition()` callback is the one non-obvious piece, and it's load-bearing, not decorative.** `startViewTransition()` snapshots the "before" state synchronously, runs its callback, then snapshots "after" the callback's own DOM mutations are actually painted — but React normally batches state updates asynchronously, so without `flushSync()` wrapping `navigate()`, the browser would snapshot "after" before React had actually re-rendered to the new route, capturing two identical frames and no visible transition at all. `react-dom`'s `flushSync` forces that one `navigate()` call to commit synchronously, which is exactly what the View Transitions contract expects.

**The visual "Posun" itself is pure CSS in `styles/global.css`, not JS — `::view-transition-old(root)`/`::view-transition-new(root)` are UA-generated pseudo-elements holding the two full-page snapshots, keyframed independently** (`buddy-prechod-stara`: `translateX(-26%)` + `filter: brightness(0.55)`; `buddy-prechod-nova`: `translateX(100%)` → `translateX(0)`). These pseudo-elements live in a separate overlay tree outside the normal document — confirmed directly, not assumed, because it's exactly why the file's own pre-existing global `*, *::before, *::after { animation-duration: 0.001ms !important }` reduced-motion kill-switch does **not** reach them, and why `useModulovyPrechod()` has to guard `prefers-reduced-motion` itself in JS (skip calling `startViewTransition()` at all, fall back to a plain `navigate()`) rather than relying on that existing CSS block.

**Wired at exactly the three places the plan named — Hub's bottom-nav "Social," bottom-nav "Chat" (`?zalozka=chaty`), and the header's search icon (`?zalozka=vyhledavac`), all of which are "Hub → Social" trips** — not the reverse. Going back (`SocialModule.tsx`'s own "← Zpět do Hubu") is deliberately left as a plain, unanimated `navigate('/hub')` for now: the CSS above only defines one direction's motion (new-from-right, old-partially-left), and applying it to a *backward* nav would slide the wrong way — a direction-aware version (new-from-left when going back) is exactly the kind of thing Fáze 4's shared, route-aware nav component is scoped to solve properly, not something to bolt onto a one-directional Fáze 2 hook prematurely.

**Verified against the real API, not a mock of it** — a temporary Playwright spec (deleted after passing) wrapped `document.startViewTransition` in `page.addInitScript` before the app loaded and confirmed it's genuinely invoked (this sandbox's Chromium supports the API) when tapping "Social" from `/hub`, that "Chat" lands on `/social?zalozka=chaty`, and — with `page.emulateMedia({ reducedMotion: 'reduce' })` — that the wrapped function is called **zero** times while navigation to `/social` still completes normally through the plain-`navigate()` fallback. `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` clean (the eager main bundle grew by under 0.5 KB — the hook itself is tiny, no new dependency), `auth.spec.ts`'s four cases unaffected (no auth-gate routing touched).

### Social nav rework, Fáze 3 of 5 — Reels (sub-phase 3a: video as first-class content)

**Fáze 3 of the original plan was named only "Sociál → Reels/Stories přerámování" and flagged by the user, at plan time, as its own project by scope — "až se k tomu dostaneme, rozplánujeme ho zvlášť, stejným způsobem" — so it got the same treatment every other large redesign in this session gets: asked what it concretely meant (a `DomuPanel.tsx` that already had Stories and a feed could mean several different things), the user picked all three of the offered pillars — real short-form video Reels, unifying Stories and the feed into one experience, and algorithmic/"for you" ranking — and this session then proposed its own three-sub-phase breakdown (3a/3b/3c) from those pillars, confirmed before any code, per the same discuss-then-build discipline.** 3a (this section) ships first because it's the lowest-risk of the three and needed no data-model discussion; 3b (merging Stories into the same swipeable feed) and 3c (ranking beyond strict chronological, and whether it ever surfaces non-followed content) both wait on their own go-ahead.

**3a is deliberately a branding-and-behavior change to the *existing* video post, not a new content type — no new table, no new `media_type`, no fork of `posts`.** A video post was already fully renderable in the feed (full-height, autoplay-when-active, same likes/comments/double-tap as a photo) since the IG-redesign/feed work earlier in this session; what it was missing to actually read as "Reels" was a duration cap (any video, however long, could be posted) and a way to *make* one in the app rather than only picking an existing file from the device gallery. Both gaps close without touching the schema — video stays `posts.media_type = 'video'`, just constrained and given a second creation path.

**`MAX_REEL_TRVANI_S = 60` (`social/api.ts`) is enforced client-side only, on every video post regardless of how it was produced — recorded in-app or picked from the device — because there is no server-side transcoding step in this project to enforce it centrally, the same "best-effort on the client" posture `MAX_POST_VIDEO_BYTES`'s size cap right next to it already has.** `ziskejDelkuVidea(file)` reads a file's duration off an off-DOM `<video>` element's `loadedmetadata` event (the browser can read a media file's duration from its header without downloading/decoding the whole thing) with a 4 s fallback timeout for a codec the browser can't parse promptly at all — same shape as `promluv()`'s speech-synthesis backup timer in `useBuddyVoice.ts`. When duration genuinely can't be measured, the check **fails open** (allows the upload) rather than blocking on an unmeasurable file — consistent with this being a UX guardrail, not a security boundary; nothing about "Reels" needed a real invariant enforced anywhere the client can't be trusted, unlike money-backed state or moderation.

**Recording happens in `NahratReelDialog.tsx`, a second, equal entry point next to the existing file picker — not a replacement for it, and not a second copy of the caption/publish UI.** Feature-detected exactly like `ChatView.tsx`'s `PODPORUJE_NAHRAVANI_HLASU` (voice messages) and `SkenovatKodDialog.tsx`'s `BarcodeDetector` check — `PODPORUJE_NAHRAVANI_REELU` gates whether the "Nahrát Reel" tile even renders in `ProfilSocialniSekce.tsx`, next to the pre-existing "+" tile, rather than offering a button that would just fail. `getUserMedia({ video: { facingMode: 'user' }, audio: true })` + `MediaRecorder` follows the exact camera/recorder ownership discipline this codebase already has twice over — `SkenovatKodDialog.tsx`'s camera-in-the-component-not-a-hook shape (simple enough not to need `usePoseEngine.ts`-style indirection) and `ChatView.tsx`'s voice-recording chunk/blob/cleanup pattern — stop every track and any still-running recorder on unmount, whether that's a normal close or the user navigating away mid-recording. Auto-stops at `MAX_REEL_TRVANI_S` (a live mm:ss counter, same `formatDelku` shape as the voice-message recording row) so nobody has to watch the clock themselves; a manual "Zastavit natáčení" stops early. Once recorded, the dialog **hands the resulting `Blob`-as-`File` off to the very same `PridatPrispevekDialog.tsx`** that the file-picker path already uses for caption + publish — building a second caption/upload UI for a file that arrived a different way would have been pure duplication, the same reasoning `reagovatNaStory()`/`sdiletPrispevekDoChatu()` already apply elsewhere in this file ("thin wrapper, not a new pipeline").

**Camera preview is mirrored (`transform: scaleX(-1)`) because it's the front (selfie) camera** — an unmirrored front-camera preview reads as visibly wrong to anyone who's ever used a phone's own camera app, the same expectation any front-facing recording UI has to meet. "Znovu natočit" re-runs the full `getUserMedia`/`MediaRecorder` acquisition from scratch rather than trying to reuse or pause the previous stream — the stream is already stopped by the time a preview exists (recording stopped is what produces the preview), so there's nothing left to resume; a second acquisition is simpler and no more costly than trying to keep one alive across a preview-then-retry cycle.

**Sound on feed video moved from permanently `muted` to a per-viewer preference the feed remembers across posts, not a per-post toggle that resets on every swipe.** `DomuPanel.tsx` holds one `zvukZapnuty` boolean (session-only React state, not persisted — matches the throwaway nature of a feed session, nothing here claims to be a durable setting) passed down to every `FeedPrispevek`; tapping the new speaker icon (only rendered for a video post, next to the existing like/comment column, same `.social-feed-akce-kruh` glass-circle language) flips it once and it stays flipped for the next video too — the same "turn sound on once, stays on" convention TikTok/Instagram's own Reels surfaces already use, not a fresh mute state to fight with on every new video. Default is muted: autoplay-with-sound the instant Domů opens would be unrequested noise, the same reasoning that already made the video element `muted` unconditionally before this pass.

**Two new `SocialIcon` cases (`video`, `volume`/`volume-off`) follow the set's existing plain-stroke style** — `video` (a camera-with-viewfinder shape, distinct from `play`'s triangle and `attach`'s paperclip) marks the record entry point specifically as "capture new video," not "pick a file" or "play."

**Building the duration-cap test surfaced that a garbage byte buffer never fires the browser's own `loadedmetadata` at all (it isn't decodable media, so the real pipeline errors out instead) — which is itself the *correct*, already-covered fail-open path, not the thing the test was trying to isolate.** To test the cap itself rather than the fail-open fallback, the temporary spec patched `HTMLMediaElement.prototype`'s `duration` getter *and* its `src` setter (dispatching a synthetic `loadedmetadata` the instant a `blob:` URL is assigned) — proving the cap logic fires correctly given a real metadata event, independent of whether a specific browser/codec combination can actually decode a given file fast enough to produce one.

**Verifying the recording flow itself found a real capability this sandbox didn't previously have, worth recording since it changes a documented limitation elsewhere in this file: Chromium's built-in `--use-fake-device-for-media-stream`/`--use-fake-ui-for-media-stream` flags produce a synthetic camera+microphone with no real hardware and no permission prompt, and they work in this environment.** This is different from — and does not retroactively fix — `SkenovatKodDialog.tsx`'s own "no camera in this sandbox" note above (a genuine hardware/environment gap for a plain, un-flagged launch) or Souboj's WebSocket wall (a proxy limitation, unrelated to devices at all): the fake-device flags are a Chromium *testing* feature, deliberately for exactly this situation, not a workaround for either of those. Confirmed by an actual, real (if synthetic) end-to-end pass — not just checked by code review, which was the honest ceiling for the camera-dependent parts of every earlier feature in this file: launch Chromium with those two flags plus a matching `executablePath` override (Playwright's `test.use({ launchOptions })` replaces the whole object rather than merging it with the project config, so the sandbox's own Chromium path has to be repeated in the test file, not just the new args — caught by a first failed run before fixing it), grant camera/microphone permissions on the context, tap "Nahrát Reel," confirm the recording indicator and live counter appear, stop, confirm the preview stage offers "Znovu natočit"/"Použít," and confirm "Použít" actually lands in `PridatPrispevekDialog.tsx`'s "Zveřejnit" step. A future feature needing a real camera round-trip (a video-message-recording flow, an in-app photo booth) can reuse this exact recipe instead of falling back to code-review-only verification by default.

`npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` clean (`NahratReelDialog.tsx` lands inside `ProfilSocialniSekce`'s existing lazy chunk — confirmed via the build output, no eager-bundle growth), `auth.spec.ts`'s four cases unaffected (no routing touched).

### Social nav rework, Fáze 3 of 5 — Reels (sub-phase 3b: Stories vplynuly do feedu)

**3b closes the open question 3a's own write-up left standing — does the celoobrazovkový Story viewer stay separate, or does it merge into the vertical feed — with an explicit `AskUserQuestion` before any code, per this session's standing discuss-then-build process.** The answer was the more thorough of the two offered options: Stories vplují přímo do svislého scroll-snap feedu jako jeho vlastní položky, žádný samostatný vodorovný pruh koleček nahoře zůstat nemá.

**Every unseen story becomes its own full-height feed "page," positioned before the ordinary post pages — one story per person's group, not one page per group.** `DomuPanel.tsx` flattens `useStories()`'s already-correctly-ordered `StorySkupina[]` (own group first, per `nactiStories()`'s own comment: "Vlastní pruh vždycky první") into a flat list of `{skupina, story, indexVeSkupine}` entries via `useMemo`, then renders them ahead of `prispevky` inside the exact same `.social-feed` scroll-snap container — one continuous swipe from story to story to the first post, not two mechanisms glued together. The old `StoriesBar.tsx` (the horizontal ring-avatar row) and `StoryProhlizec.tsx` (the portaled full-screen viewer with its own tap-left/right and 5 s auto-advance timer) are both deleted outright, not left half-wired — their entire function moved into the new `FeedStory.tsx`, the same "delete, don't leave dead code for a feature whose function moved wholesale" call this file already made for `MujProfilPanel.tsx`.

**Dropping the auto-advance timer is a deliberate simplification, not an oversight — once a story is just one more page in a feed everything else already swipes through, a *second* way to move to the next page (a countdown) would be redundant with the *first* (scroll), not complementary.** `FeedStory.tsx` therefore has no timer, no tap-left/right regions, no `pozastaveno`-while-typing guard against a timer that no longer exists — the user swipes down, same gesture as leaving a post behind. The per-author progress dots stay (`.social-story-progres-radek`, reused, not rebuilt) because they still answer a real question ("kolikátá z kolika"), just filled by position (`i <= indexVeSkupine`) rather than by elapsed time — the identical, already-battle-tested computation `ZvyrazneniProhlizec.tsx`'s own highlight viewer already uses for the same reason (browsing a fixed collection isn't a race against a clock either).

**`FeedStory.tsx` deliberately reuses `FeedPrispevek.tsx`'s own visual skeleton — `.social-feed-info` bottom-left (avatar/name/caption), `.social-feed-akce` bottom-right (action column) — rather than keeping the old viewer's separate top-header-row layout, because that's what actually makes a story and a post read as two flavors of the same thing instead of two different screens stitched together.** Own-story actions (star = add to Highlights, trash = delete) sit in the same right-hand glass-circle column `FeedPrispevek`'s like/comment buttons already occupy; the "N zhlédnutí" button and the reply/quick-reaction row (unchanged content, `reagovatNaStory()`/`EMOJI_REAKCI` — a reaction is still just a one-tap reply, no new mechanism) sit in the same left info column a caption would. No `createPortal`/z-index concern here at all, unlike the old viewer — `FeedStory` is a plain sibling of `FeedPrispevek` inside the normal `.social-feed` DOM tree, not a portaled full-screen overlay competing with `.social-bottom-nav` for stacking order, so the class of bug that made `StoryProhlizec.tsx` need a portal in the first place simply doesn't exist here.

**View tracking moved from "record on every slide shown" to the same `aktivni`-gated one-shot pattern `FeedPrispevek.tsx`'s video autoplay already established** — `oznacitZhlednuti()` fires once, guarded by a ref, the first time a story's `IntersectionObserver` entry crosses the same 0.6 threshold that already decides which post's video plays; the old viewer's own "on every mount, since a mount only ever meant deliberately opening that one slide" trigger doesn't fit a feed where several items can exist in the DOM as neighbors before actually being scrolled to. `DomuPanel.tsx`'s existing observer (previously post-only) now watches both story and post DOM nodes in one pass — `data-post-id` gets a `story:` prefix for story pages specifically so the pre-existing "is this id near the end of `prispevky`, fetch the next page" check (which does a plain `prispevky.findIndex`) can't accidentally match a story id and mis-trigger pagination.

**"+ Story" survives as one small button, not a whole affordance-shaped hole where the ring row used to be** — a plain pill in a slim, non-scrolling row above `.social-feed` (`.social-panel--domu` became `display:flex;flex-direction:column` to host it as a sibling of the feed, not inside it), opening the exact same file-picker → `PridatStoryDialog.tsx` flow the old bar's own "+" badge used, unchanged. This is deliberately not a second avatar tile or a mini version of the old bar — the confirmed answer was "no separate horizontal bar," and one button satisfies "still reachable from Domů" without reintroducing the thing that was asked to go away.

**CSS-wise, nothing shared with the still-unrelated Highlights feature (`ZvyrazneniProhlizec.tsx`) was touched — `.social-story-progres-radek`/`-bg`/`-fill`/`.je-hotovo`, `.social-story-plocha`/`-obrazek`/`-tap*`, `.social-story-prohlizec*`, `.social-story-akce-btn`/`-zavirit` are all still shared infrastructure with the Highlights viewer and `PrispevekProhlizec.tsx`, confirmed by grep before deleting anything, not assumed safe.** Only the classes StoriesBar.tsx alone ever owned (`.social-stories-pruh`/`-radek`, `.social-story-tile*`, `.social-story-plus-btn`, `.social-story-krouzek*`) were deleted, along with `.social-story-progres-fill.bezi`/`@keyframes social-story-plni` and their `prefers-reduced-motion` overrides — the one piece of the shared progress-dot styling that genuinely was StoryProhlizec-only, since Highlights never used the timed "bezi" state either (confirmed by reading its own render code, not assumed from the name). The base `.social-story-progres-radek` rule itself stays exactly as it was (a static flow child, correct for `ZvyrazneniProhlizec.tsx`'s own `.social-story-prohlizec` flex-column shell) — `FeedStory.tsx` needs it `position: absolute` instead (`.social-feed-post` is a flex row where a non-positioned extra child would just compete with the image for space), so that one override is scoped with a `.social-feed-post .social-story-progres-radek` descendant selector rather than touched at the source.

**Verified against the real API, not a mock of `reagovatNaStory` itself** — a temporary Playwright spec (deleted after passing) confirmed no `.social-stories-radek`/`.social-story-tile` renders anywhere on Domů any more; a mocked friend's unseen story renders as its own full-height feed page with the right author/caption and a reply form (not a "N zhlédnutí" button, since it isn't the viewer's own); tapping a quick-reaction emoji fires the expected `POST /rest/v1/messages` with `story_id`/`chat_id`/`body` all correctly set, through the real `otevritChat()` → `poslatZpravu()` path unchanged from before 3b; a mocked own story shows "0 zhlédnutí" plus the star/trash action column and no reply form. A full-page screenshot (checked by eye, not just asserted) confirmed the visual unification actually reads as intended — progress bar overlaying the top of the media, author/caption/reactions anchored bottom-left in the same spot a post's own caption sits. `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` clean (the `SocialModule` chunk shrank slightly, net code removed outweighing what `FeedStory.tsx` added), `auth.spec.ts`'s four cases unaffected (no routing touched).

3c (algorithmic/"for you" ranking, and whether the feed ever surfaces non-followed content) still waits on its own go-ahead, per the same phase plan 3a's write-up already recorded.

### Social nav rework, Fáze 3 of 5 — Reels (sub-phase 3c: algoritmický "Pro tebe" feed)

**3c closes the last open question from the original Reels plan — whether the ranked feed ever surfaces content from accounts the viewer doesn't follow — asked explicitly via two `AskUserQuestion` rounds before any code, per this session's standing process.** The answer to the first (discovery vs. followed-only) was the bigger of the two options: the feed should genuinely mix in public posts from non-followed accounts, not just re-sort the existing followed-only set. The answer to the second (one ranking for everyone vs. a switchable mode) confirmed a real toggle — "Nejnovější" (unchanged, chronological, followed-only) stays available side by side with a new "Pro tebe" (ranked, with discovery), not a silent behavior change nobody can opt out of.

**`nacti_doporuceny_feed()` is a new, separate `SECURITY DEFINER` function — not a parameter bolted onto `nacti_feed()` — because the two have genuinely different shapes, not just different `WHERE` clauses.** `nacti_feed()` keyset-paginates on a single monotonic column (`created_at`); a ranked feed's score isn't monotonic with time at all, so pagination has to key off the pair `(skore, id)` instead, a different cursor contract a caller has to know about. Candidates are drawn from `posts` within the last 14 days (own + followed + everyone else `smi_videt_prispevky_uzivatele()` already says the caller may see — the same visibility gate `nacti_feed`/`nacti_prispevky`/`nacti_ulozene_prispevky` all already share, so discovery can never show a blocked or private-and-not-mutual account's post; it only widens *whose* posts are candidates, never the underlying visibility rule itself). The 14-day window is a deliberate, stated restraint — an unbounded candidate set would mean rescoring the whole table's history on every page load, and a "recommended" post from months ago isn't what "for you, right now" is supposed to mean.

**Score is `(likes + 2×comments) / (age_in_hours + 2)^1.5`, plus a flat +4 for a followed account and +4 for the viewer's own post** — a simple, explainable formula over anything that would need tuning data this project doesn't have. The exponent-1.5 decay means a post's rank fades within roughly a day or two regardless of engagement, so old high-engagement content can't camp at the top forever; the followed/own boosts mean discovery *supplements* the people-you-actually-follow feed rather than competing evenly with it — a stranger's post needs real engagement to outrank a followed account's fresh, unliked one, matching what "for you" means in every reference feed this was modeled after (followed content still comes first by default, algorithmic ranking mostly reorders it and occasionally interleaves something good from outside it).

**Keyset pagination on `(skore, id)` tolerates the score being a live, changing number between page loads — deliberately, not a bug to fix.** A strictly reproducible ranked feed would need to freeze scores at first-load time (a snapshot table, an extra round trip) for zero real benefit at this project's scale; the client just carries `{skore, id}` of the last row it received (`api.ts`'s `nactiDoporucenyFeed()` returns it alongside the posts) and asks for anything ranked lower than that pair on the next page. A post's rank shifting slightly between two page fetches (someone liked it in between) is the same accepted tolerance every reference "for you" feed has — nothing in this app promises frame-perfect ranking stability.

**On the client, `DomuPanel.tsx` gained one small pill toggle (`.social-domu-rezim-prepinac`) in the existing non-scrolling top row, next to "+ Story" — not a second screen.** Switching modes resets the feed and its pagination state (`prispevky`, `vseNacteno`, and a `doporucenyKurzor` ref that only "Pro tebe" ever populates) and re-fetches from scratch; `nacistDalsiStranku()` branches on `rezim` to call the right RPC with the right cursor shape, but everything downstream — rendering, video autoplay, the shared `IntersectionObserver`, `FeedPrispevek`/`FeedStory` themselves — stays completely unaware which mode produced the posts it's showing. The toggle is deliberately session-only state, not a persisted preference — same restraint as `zvukZapnuty` right next to it in the same file: Domů always opens on "Nejnovější," matching what the feed did before this phase existed, so 3c changes nothing about default behavior for anyone who never touches the toggle.

**The now-recurring `SECURITY DEFINER`-grant mistake this file has caught five times before was checked immediately this time, not after the fact** — `revoke ... from public, anon` plus `grant ... to authenticated` landed in the same migration as the function itself, verified straight after with `has_function_privilege('anon', ...)` returning `false` before any client code was written against it.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation, inside a transaction rolled back afterward (not deleted rows — a guaranteed-clean revert, the same discipline this file uses whenever test data needs zero residue):** a signed-in account with no follows at all still sees public posts from unrelated accounts (proving discovery genuinely works, not just re-sorting an empty followed set); a liked post outranks an unliked one from an equally-fresh account; keyset pagination on `(skore, id)` correctly excludes every row already returned on an earlier page; a private, non-mutual-friend account's post is excluded exactly like everywhere else in Social; `anon` gets an explicit `permission denied` calling the function directly, not a silent empty result. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing, plus a screenshot checked by eye): the toggle renders "Nejnovější" active by default with an empty feed (mocked `nacti_feed: []`); clicking "Pro tebe" calls `nacti_doporuceny_feed` and renders a mocked discovery post from an account the mock never marks as followed, confirming the client wiring reaches the new RPC and renders whatever it returns, unfiltered by anything the client itself would need to additionally check. `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` clean (`SocialModule` stayed its own lazy chunk), `auth.spec.ts`'s four cases unaffected (no routing touched).

This completes all three sub-phases of Fáze 3 (Reels). Fáze 4 (Hub's own bottom nav pulled into a shared, route-aware component reused across pages) and Fáze 5 (swipe/arrow navigation between modules, building on Fáze 4) still wait on their own go-ahead, per the original five-phase plan.

### Social nav rework, Fáze 4 of 5 — sdílená spodní navigace (`src/components/AppBottomNav.tsx`)

**Fáze 4's own plan-time description ("pulled into a shared, route-aware component reused across pages") left one real question unresolved — which pages — asked explicitly via `AskUserQuestion` before any code, per this session's standing process.** The answer: Apps, Profil and Nastavení gain the same lišta Hub already has (Home/Social/Buddy orb/Chat/Settings); Rewards/Shop/Podpora stay without it (secondary, drop-in-drop-out destinations, not places users linger); Social keeps its own, unrelated four-tab bottom nav (Profil-link/Chaty/Domů/Vyhledávač) completely untouched — those are that screen's *internal* tabs, not the same concept as the app-wide lišta at all, and merging them would have meant two navigation rows stacked on one screen for no benefit.

**The component itself is a straight extraction, not a rewrite — `Hub.tsx`'s `<nav className="hub-bottom-nav">` block moved verbatim into `src/components/AppBottomNav.tsx`/`.css` (classes renamed `hub-nav-*` → `app-nav-*`, matching the file it now actually lives in — the same "a class only works where its stylesheet actually loads" rule this file has stated for every prior CSS move), with one real behavior change: "Home" and "Nastavení" became route-aware (`useLocation().pathname`) instead of "Home" being hardcoded always-active.** On Hub itself that was already true by construction (the nav only ever rendered there); reused on Apps/Profil/Nastavení, "Home" has to correctly turn off and instead navigate to `/hub` on click — a plain `navigate('/hub')`, not `prejit()`, matching Fáze 2's own stated restraint that the View Transition CSS only defines one direction of motion (Hub → Social), so a *backward* trip out of these pages back to Hub was never meant to animate. Social and Chat, by contrast, now use `prejit()` from every page that renders this component, not just Hub — the transition CSS is scoped to `::view-transition-*(root)`, generic to "entering Social," not Hub-specific, so extending the same animated trip to fire from Apps/Profil/Nastavení too needed no CSS change at all, just calling the same hook from the new location.

**The Buddy voice orb button needed a real design decision, not just a copy-paste, because `useBuddyVoice()` was never meant to be called from more than one place at once.** Hub.tsx's own comment already explained why the hook lives there and not inside `BuddyOverlay`: the big hero orb visually reacts to the *same* hook instance the nav's small orb button opens, so tapping the small orb has to drive the big one's animation. That coupling can't just move into the shared component — `AppBottomNav` has no hero orb to sync with on Apps/Profil/Nastavení, and Hub still does. The fix is the same optional-callback shape `HubModuleProps` already had (`onTalk?: () => void`): Hub passes its own `otevritBuddyho` (backed by its own `useBuddyVoice()` instance, keeping the hero-orb sync intact, unchanged from before this phase); everywhere else, `AppBottomNav` falls back to owning a private `useBuddyVoice()` + `BuddyOverlay` of its own. Both call sites end up calling `useBuddyVoice()` unconditionally on every render regardless of whether `onTalk` is supplied (React's own rule against conditional hooks, not an oversight) — checked against the hook's own implementation first, not assumed cheap: it's pure `useState`/`useRef` with no eager side effect, `SpeechRecognition` only ever constructed inside `zacniMluvit()`, so the unused second instance on Hub costs nothing at idle.

**Wiring the component into Apps/Profil/Nastavení was straightforward — a single `<AppBottomNav />` (no props) added to each page's existing JSX, `AppModule.tsx`'s addition scoped only to its grid view (`.app-container`), never its full-screen miniapp view (`.app-fullscreen-view`) — a miniapp is its own focused screen with its own back button, the same reasoning Social never puts its own bottom nav inside an open `ChatView.tsx`.** `HubModuleProps` lost its `onOpenSettings` prop in the same pass — it was already dead code (nothing in the codebase ever actually passed it; `<Hub />` in `App.tsx` renders with zero props), and removing the bottom nav's own Settings button (the only thing that ever read it) left it with no remaining reference at all, which `noUnusedLocals` would have flagged had it been kept.

**A real screenshot — not just a DOM-presence assertion — caught a genuine overlap bug the component-level test alone would have missed: the last card on Profil ("MŮJ KÓD" — QR code, code text, copy/scan buttons) rendered half-hidden behind the sticky nav bar, not above it.** The root cause is a structural difference this file hadn't had to reckon with before: Hub/Social's own bottom nav sits inside a page shell that *doesn't itself scroll* (a fixed-viewport flex column, with `margin-top: auto` on the nav simply pushing it to the bottom of that one static screen) — but `.app-container`/`.profil-page`/`.settings-page` are genuinely long-scrolling pages by design (more content than one viewport), where a `position: sticky; bottom: 0` nav mixed directly into the same scrolling flex column as the page's own content will correctly stick to the viewport bottom once scrolled — but only stops overlapping real content if the container's own bottom padding reserves at least the nav's rendered height, so the *last* content item can fully clear it before the sticky nav "catches up." All three pages' existing bottom padding (`3rem`/`0.75rem`/`1.5rem` respectively) predated any nav being there at all and was nowhere close — measured directly rather than guessed (`boundingBox()` on the real rendered nav: ~78px), all three bumped to `calc(5.5rem + env(safe-area-inset-bottom))`, the same comfortable-margin instinct `.social-toast`'s own `4.5rem` bottom-nav clearance already used elsewhere in this file. Caught specifically because this session's own standing practice (a real screenshot checked by eye, not just element-exists assertions) was followed here too — a scroll-to-bottom screenshot on Profil showed the overlap before the fix and the fully-clear card after it, on all three pages.

**Verified with `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` (all clean — the eager main bundle grew by well under half a kilobyte, since `useBuddyVoice`/`BuddyOverlay` were already eagerly bundled via Hub.tsx, not new weight, just one more reference to code already there) and a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): the nav renders on Apps/Profil/Nastavení with no item marked active on Apps, clicking "Home" navigates to `/hub` and marks it active there, "Settings" is marked active on `/nastaveni`; on Hub itself the nav still opens the real Buddy voice overlay via the orb button (`.buddy-overlay` becomes visible), confirming the `onTalk` hand-off works unchanged. Three more temporary screenshots (deleted after checking, one per newly-wired page, each scrolled to its own bottom) confirmed the padding fix — no card left overlapping the sticky nav on any of the three. `auth.spec.ts`'s four cases stayed unaffected throughout (no auth-gate routing touched).

Fáze 5 (swipe/arrow navigation between modules, client-only, no library, building on this phase's shared component) still waits on its own go-ahead, per the original five-phase plan.

### Social nav rework, Fáze 5 of 5 — swipe/šipky mezi moduly (`src/core/navigation/`)

**Two real forks in Fáze 5's own plan-time one-liner — which screens, and touch-vs-arrows-vs-both — were resolved via `AskUserQuestion` before any code, same as every earlier phase in this plan.** Scope: the same four pages Fáze 4's shared `AppBottomNav` already covers (Hub/Apps/Profil/Nastavení), not Social — Social has its own bottom nav and Domů's own vertical scroll-snap feed, and a horizontal gesture layered on top of either would fight, not complement, what's already there. Mechanism: a real horizontal swipe on touch, **plus** small left/right arrow buttons for a mouse/no-touch input — not swipe-only, since a desktop visitor (or this sandbox's own CI/e2e runs) has no swipe gesture to make at all.

**`core/navigation/moduloveStranky.ts` is the one ordered list — `[Hub, Apps, Profil, Nastavení]` — every other piece of this phase reads, not four independent "what's next" computations that could drift apart.** `sousedniStranky(pathname)` is the single pure function both the swipe hook and the arrow buttons call to find `{predchozi, dalsi}` from the current route — Hub (first) has no `predchozi`, Nastavení (last) has no `dalsi`, and neither end wraps around: cycling Nastavení → Hub via one more swipe would surprise more than it'd delight, and nothing asked for a carousel loop.

**The swipe detector (`useModulovySwipe.ts`) deliberately reads only `touchstart`/`touchend`, never `touchmove` — a real constraint this session hadn't hit before, not a simplification for its own sake.** React has registered the root's `touchmove` listener as passive since React 17 (for scroll-performance reasons that predate this app), which means a `preventDefault()` called from inside a synthetic `onTouchMove` handler would silently do nothing (and log a console warning) rather than actually block the browser's own scroll — so trying to "commit" to a horizontal drag mid-gesture the way a native app might was never actually available here. The fix is to not need it: compare only where the finger started and where it lifted, and require the horizontal distance to clearly dominate the vertical one (`Math.abs(dx) >= Math.abs(dy) * 1.5`, plus a flat 70px minimum) before treating it as a page-swipe at all. A user scrolling Profil/Apps/Nastavení up and down — which is most touches on these pages, since all three are longer than one screen — almost never produces a horizontal-dominant gesture, so the two intents don't collide in practice even without ever intercepting the browser's native scroll.

**The four pages wire the hook identically — spread `onTouchStart`/`onTouchEnd` onto the page's own existing root element (`.hub-container`/`.app-container`/`.profil-page`/`.settings-page`), nothing else changes.** `AppModule.tsx`'s wiring is scoped to the same grid view Fáze 4's `<AppBottomNav />` already was, never the full-screen miniapp view — a miniapp being played shouldn't lose a horizontal swipe to page navigation out from under it.

**The desktop arrows live inside `AppBottomNav.tsx` itself, not a fifth new component, since that's already the one thing mounted on all four pages and already imports `useNavigate`/`useLocation`.** Two fixed-position circular buttons at the left/right viewport edges, `SocialIcon name="arrow-left"` reused for both (the right one CSS-rotated 180°, the same "one icon, no dedicated mirror asset" trick this file already used for the Nastavení-menu back chevron) — hidden by default and shown only `@media (pointer: fine)`, the exact inverse of `VirtualniJoystick.tsx`'s own "hide the touch control when there's a real pointer" media query already established in the game hub, applied here in the opposite direction: touch gets the swipe, a mouse gets the arrows, neither device sees the other's affordance cluttering the screen.

**Verifying the `pointer: fine` media query itself hit a real, disclosed sandbox limit — CDP's `Emulation.setEmulatedMedia` with a `pointer` feature override did not change `window.matchMedia('(pointer: fine)').matches` in this environment (confirmed directly, not assumed: still `false` after the override), and Playwright's own `page.emulateMedia()` has no `pointer` option at all (only color-scheme/reduced-motion/forced-colors).** This is the same honest-ceiling situation this file already documents for camera/microphone access — the CSS itself is the same well-understood, already-proven `@media (pointer: fine)` syntax `VirtualniJoystick.tsx` already ships and relies on, verified there by code review rather than a live browser check; the arrows' *behavior* (correct edge-hiding in the DOM, and the click handler actually navigating) was verified directly instead, via `dispatchEvent('click')` on the button rather than a real `.click()` — a `display: none` element has no clickable box for Playwright's normal actionability check to find, `force: true` doesn't bypass a zero-size box, only `dispatchEvent` fires the handler without needing one.

**Verified via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): a real synthetic `TouchEvent`-based swipe (constructed via `new Touch()`/`new TouchEvent()`, not Playwright's own `touchscreen` API, which only supports `tap()`, not a start/end drag) starting on Hub and ending far enough to the left lands on `/apps`; swiping back right from Apps returns to `/hub`; a gesture that moves mostly vertically with only a small horizontal component (simulating an ordinary scroll on Profil) does not navigate at all; `.modul-sipka--vlevo` is absent on Hub and present on Nastavení, and vice versa for `.modul-sipka--vpravo`; dispatching a click on the (CSS-hidden, in this touch-emulated context) left arrow on Nastavení still navigates to `/profil`, confirming the handler itself is correctly wired regardless of the unverifiable CSS visibility. `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` clean (bundle grew by ~1 KB, the new navigation module itself), `auth.spec.ts`'s four cases unaffected (no auth-gate routing touched).

This completes all five phases of the Social nav rework plan agreed at its outset (Fáze 1 through Fáze 5).

### Přihlašovací upozornění (`core/security/`)

**"Upozornění na přihlášení" in `/nastaveni`'s Zabezpečení card was the other cosmetic no-op this session's own 100%-readiness audit flagged alongside biometrics — `updateSecurity({ loginAlerts: … })` set a boolean nothing else in the codebase ever read.** It's real now, and — like biometrics before it — built free, no paid infrastructure: a plain self-row Supabase table plus the app's existing Realtime publication mechanism, not a second push backend. `core/security/` is a new small module family, not folded into `social/api.ts`: this isn't Social, it's account security, the same reasoning that already gave `core/support/api.ts` and `core/notifications/api.ts` their own homes instead of stretching "api.ts is the only place Social talks to Supabase" to cover something Social has nothing to do with.

**The honest limit is stated up front, the same way `core/utils/notify.ts`'s own comment already states its limit for Pomodoro/Planer: this is not a real "new sign-in" email from a paid service, it's the best free version an app with no push backend beyond the narrow Pomodoro cron can build.** A notification only ever reaches a device that has the app open (any screen, not just Nastavení) at the exact moment a new device signs in — there is no delivery to a closed app, no email, no SMS. `login_devices` (`user_id`, `device_id`, `popis`, `first_seen_at`, `last_seen_at`, composite PK) is plain self-row RLS (`user_id = auth.uid()` on SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE) — no `SECURITY DEFINER` function anywhere in this feature, because unlike almost everything else in Social, nobody ever needs to read a *different* account's rows here, the same simplicity `presence` already established for its own self-row table.

**`device_id` is a plain random id generated once in `localStorage` (`zdejsiZarizeniId()`), deliberately not `secureStorage`** — `secureStorage`'s XOR obfuscation exists for data persisted through a Zustand store; a bare device id sitting outside any store, carrying no personal content of its own (just "has this browser been seen before"), doesn't need it. `popisZarizeni()` is a small, library-free `navigator.userAgent` sniff (same "no library, just a few `if`s" restraint as `social/avatarColor.ts`) producing a human label like "Chrome na Android" — accuracy isn't the point, just enough that a device row in Nastavení or a notification body doesn't read as "neznámé zařízení" forever.

**Reporting a device is one `upsert`, not a select-then-branch — and the choice of INSERT-vs-UPDATE at the SQL level is what makes the Realtime side of this feature work with zero extra client logic.** `nahlasZarizeni()` upserts on `(user_id, device_id)` every time the app boots signed in; the *first* time a given device is seen, Postgres executes a real `INSERT` and logical replication reports it as one, which is exactly what `sledovatNovaZarizeni()` subscribes to (`event: 'INSERT'` only, the same `channel(name:counter)` shape `core/notifications/api.ts`'s `sledovatAktivituPrispevku` already established). Every later boot from the same device hits the `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` branch instead, which Postgres reports as an `UPDATE` — invisible to an `INSERT`-only subscription, so a device that already exists never re-triggers a notification just because the app was reopened. No client-side "is this new?" check was needed; the database's own INSERT/UPDATE distinction already answers it.

**`startLoginNotify()` follows `inbox.ts`'s exact "react to sign-in transitions, not just sign-in-once" shape, called once from `App.tsx`'s startup effect alongside `startPresence()`/`startInbox()`** — a `posledni` closure variable plus a `useAccount.subscribe` callback, not a one-shot `if (signed-in)` check, because the account can sign in after this effect already ran (a fresh session resolving asynchronously) and the subscription has to be armed before that happens, not after. The notification handler self-excludes the device that just wrote the row (`deviceId === zdejsiZarizeniId()`) — the same "don't notify yourself about your own action" instinct `inbox.ts` already applies to a user's own sent message — and checks `useProfileStore.getState().profile.security.loginAlerts` fresh on every event, not once at subscribe time, matching the toggle's own promise to take effect immediately.

**`/nastaveni` also gained a small "Nedávná přihlášení" list under the toggle** — reading `nactiZarizeni()` once on mount, not tied to the Realtime subscription (that only ever needs to be listening from `App.tsx`, not from whichever screen happens to be open) — so the toggle has visible content next to it instead of being a bare switch with nothing to show for what it does, the same reasoning `SettingsModule.tsx`'s "Synchronizace"/"Verze aplikace" rows already give their own toggles.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation**: the account's own device insert and a same-device upsert (no duplicate row) both succeed; a different account cannot insert a row claiming to be someone else's device and cannot read another account's devices; `anon` is rejected outright on both read and write. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): loading the app fires the expected `POST /rest/v1/login_devices` upsert with the real `user_id`/a generated `device_id`/a non-empty `popis`, and a mocked existing device renders in the "Nedávná přihlášení" list on `/nastaveni`. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**SocialReport's "Nevhodná profilová fotka" report reason (Social Fáze 2's own uploaded-avatar moderation) always had two response tools available — ban from Social, ban from the whole app — but nothing that actually addressed what the report was about, flagged explicitly as "the natural next gap" back when that reason shipped rather than built speculatively then.** `moderator_smaz_avatar(cil)` closes it, shaped identically to `moderator_smaz_prispevek()`: `SECURITY DEFINER`, requires `jsem_moderator()`, refuses to run unless the target actually has a `nevhodne_foto` report against them (so it can't become a generic "blank anyone's avatar" tool even in a moderator's own hands), sets `profiles.avatar_url = null`, and logs to `audit_log` via the existing `zapis_audit_log()`. It doesn't touch the underlying Storage object — the same accepted "orphaned, unreachable blob" trade this session already made for deleted stories/posts/messages — nulling the column is what actually matters, since every read path in the app already goes through it and the photo disappears everywhere the instant it's cleared.
