**A location on the map can now open a real 3D first-person world instead of only a bottom-sheet blurb, and walking into what's waiting there is what starts the fight — not a button in the sheet.** This is the first "location with actual content" the map's generic locations were always waiting for (see the "zatím nikam nevede" placeholder text above): `lokace.ts` gained a fourth `LokaceTyp`, `'explorace'`, and `MapaSveta.tsx` initially checked it the exact same way it already checked `NEPRATELE_PODLE_LOKACE` for a direct-to-combat location — a lookup against a new `SVETY_PODLE_LOKACE` map (`game/data/world.ts`), no new branch shape, just a different data table and a different sheet button ("🌲 Vstoupit do světa" instead of "⚔️ Vstoupit do boje"). `Emberfall` was the one location wired up at first, holding Season 1's first quest, "Ztracené štěně" (The Lost Pup) — the other 6 planned locations and 29 planned side quests are still deliberately not stubbed out; adding one is "a new `Lokace` + a new `SvetKonfigurace` + a new `Nepritel[]` entry + a new `Quest`," the same low-ceremony shape every other content table in this file already has, not a new system. (See **Fáze 7 (dungeons)** below for why that `typ === 'explorace'` check was later replaced with a presence check against `SVETY_PODLE_LOKACE` instead.)

**The loop is Map → Location → 3D world → exploration → encounter → the *existing* 2D card combat → reward → quest complete → XP → back to the map — deliberately not a 3D combat system.** `Explorace3D.tsx` (the screen) and `usePlayerWorld.ts` (the engine, see below) know nothing about cards, decks, or damage math; the only thing exploration produces is one event, "the player reached the monster," surfaced as `onSetkani`. `GameModule.tsx` reacts to it by setting the *same* `soubojLokaceId` state a direct arena/dungeon pin already sets, so from that point on it's the same `Souboj.tsx`/`useSouboj.ts` that every other fight in the game already uses — `combat/nepratele.ts` gained one more entry (`emberfall`, a Stínovlčák) rather than exploration growing a parallel enemy/reward system. Leaving the fight (win or lose) clears both `soubojLokaceId` *and* `exploraceLokaceId` in one place, so the player lands back on the map, never back inside the 3D world they just left.

**Quest completion is a new, deliberately tiny store (`useQuestStore.ts`), not a field bolted onto `useGameCharacter` or the gamification store.** A quest is account-wide progress like badges (finishing it isn't tied to which character happens to be selected this session), but it isn't XP/streak/badge data either, so it doesn't belong in `useGamificationStore` — its own `secureStorage`-backed persist, validated the same item-by-item way as `gameCharacterValidation.ts` (`questValidation.ts`, deriving valid quest/objective ids straight from `QUESTS` instead of keeping a second hardcoded id list that could drift out of sync). Registered in `BACKUP_STORES` like every other persisted key must be. `Souboj.tsx` still doesn't know quests exist: it grew one optional prop, `onVyhra`, called once alongside the existing `recordAction`/`credit`/`pridatXpPostave` effect on a real win, and only `GameModule.tsx` decides — via `BOJOVY_CIL_PODLE_LOKACE`, `game/data/quests.ts` — whether that fight happens to be the one that finishes a quest objective. A location with combat but no quest attached (the Arena, the existing dungeon) simply never passes `onVyhra`.

**A quest has an explicit life cycle — `nedostupny` → `aktivni` → `splneny` — and a list of `cile` (objectives), not one flat `cil` string and an implicit "walking in is accepting."** The vertical slice's `Souboj`-only wiring worked for exactly one quest with exactly one step; a real quest engine has to support quests that need more than one thing done, possibly of different kinds (`TypCile` is `'boj'` today, on purpose the only case built so far — a dialogue or item-collection quest later adds a new value here, not a new store). `MapaSveta.tsx`'s location sheet now gates on this: an `'explorace'` location with an unaccepted quest shows "📜 Přijmout quest" instead of the world-entry button, and only after `prijmoutQuest()` does the sheet switch to a checklist of `cile` plus "🌲 Vstoupit do světa." `BOJOVY_CIL_PODLE_LOKACE` maps a location to *which* quest and *which one objective* a win there satisfies (`{ questId, cilId }`, not just a quest id) — a quest whose objectives span more than one location, which will exist soon, couldn't be expressed by a flatter mapping. `useQuestStore.splnitCil(questId, cilId)` marks that one objective done and only promotes the quest into `dokoncene` once every objective in `Quest.cile` is done, computed against the quest's own data, not a hand-maintained counter.

**`Questy.tsx` is the quest log, opened from the map's floating menu — the second `.mapa-menu-dlazdice` tile, proving out the "adding a feature is one more tile" claim made when that menu shipped with only Hrdina in it.** Three sections (Aktivní/Dostupné/Splněné), each just `QUESTS.filter(...)` against the store's `aktivni`/`dokoncene` arrays — a "Dostupné" quest (visible on the map, not yet accepted) is deliberately listed here too, not hidden until accepted, so a player can see what's out there before committing to it.

**`stavQuestu`/`jeCilSplneny` are plain exported functions taking the store's raw arrays as arguments, not methods living on the store itself — a real bug this phase hit and fixed, not just a style choice.** The first version put them on the store (`stavQuestu: (id) => { ... get() ... }`), and every consumer subscribed to `useQuestStore((s) => s.stavQuestu)`. That never re-rendered after `prijmoutQuest()`: Zustand only re-renders a component when the *selected value* changes by `Object.is`, and a method defined once in the store creator is the same function reference for the store's entire lifetime — the underlying `aktivni`/`dokoncene` arrays it reads via `get()` had genuinely changed, but the selected value (the function itself) hadn't, so nothing told React to re-render. The sheet was stuck showing "Přijmout quest" forever, caught by the e2e test asserting the checklist actually appears after clicking it — the same category of mistake `leveling.ts`'s pure-function pattern already avoided elsewhere in this file, now enforced here too: compute derived state in the component (or a pure helper it calls) from data the component actually subscribes to, never from a store method that closes over `get()`.

**Story (Fáze 3) is a small dialogue-sequence engine, not text hand-written inside the components that happen to trigger it.** `game/data/story.ts` holds `STORY: Record<string, DialogSekvence>` — ordered `DialogRadek[]` (speaker, icon, text) keyed by an id — and `Quest` (`data/quests.ts`) gained two optional fields, `dialogPriPrijeti`/`dialogPriDokonceni`, that just name a sequence to play. `StoryDialog.tsx` is the one player: full-screen (not a translucent overlay — a short scene, matching how a visual novel dialogue box works, not a sheet over something else), one line at a time, tap-anywhere or the "Dál ▸" button to advance, "Přeskočit" to bail out early, a dot-per-line progress indicator at the top. It's reused as-is in two different places in the loop — `MapaSveta.tsx` renders it inline right after `prijmoutQuest()` (the accept beat, still on the map), `GameModule.tsx` renders it as its own screen tier after a win that completed a quest (the payoff beat, between combat and the map — literally the "Story" step in this feature's own loop diagram, Souboj → reward → quest complete → XP → **Story** → map). Neither caller hardcodes who's talking: a line's `mluvci` can be a literal NPC name from the data, or the sentinel `'__hrac__'`/`'__buddy__'`, which `StoryDialog` resolves at render time to the actually-selected `postava.jmeno`/`.ikona` (data has no way to know which of the five heroes is being played) or to a fixed Buddy identity — the same reasoning as `Postava.ikona` being resolved per-character everywhere else in this file.

**`useQuestStore.splnitCil` returns `true` only on the call that actually finished the quest, not on every partial objective — that boolean is what gates the completion dialogue.** Without it, a multi-objective quest would replay its epilogue after each objective, and `Souboj.tsx`'s `onVyhra` — which fires on every win at a quest-linked location, including a player re-fighting an already-cleared encounter — would replay it forever. `GameModule.tsx` only calls `setStoryId(...)` when `splnitCil(...)` returns `true`, so the dialogue plays exactly once, the moment the quest's last objective clears.

**`usePlayerWorld.ts` is plain Three.js, not React Three Fiber — matching this codebase's existing pattern instead of introducing a second one.** Social's ambient scene and Form Check's pose engine already both own a renderer/camera/render-loop entirely outside React (`useAmbientScene.ts`, `usePoseEngine.ts`) and clean it up on unmount; the exploration hook is a third instance of that exact shape, not a different paradigm bolted on next to it. It builds the world from plain geometry — a ground plane, cone-and-cylinder "trees," dodecahedron "rocks," an icosahedron enemy with a point light and a slowly-turning halo torus, and a two-sphere "core + glow" Buddy companion lifted straight from `useAmbientScene.ts`'s koule technique — because there is no real 3D asset pipeline in this project and downloading one from a third-party source would break the "runs completely without the cloud, self-hosted assets only" rule that already governs Form Check's models and the map/character art. Fog (`scene.fog`, colour from `SvetKonfigurace.barvaMlhy`) does double duty as both atmosphere and a cheap draw-distance cutoff.

**Input is unified through the Pointer Events API so the same code drives mouse-drag-to-look on desktop and touch-drag-to-look on mobile** — a pointerdown/pointermove/pointerup triplet on the canvas container updates yaw/pitch directly, clamped so the player can't flip past vertical. Movement is separate: WASD/arrow keys on desktop, or `VirtualniJoystick.tsx` on mobile — a small DOM control living *outside* the canvas container's own DOM subtree, which is what makes a touch on the joystick never also register as a look-drag (the browser dispatches a pointer event to whichever element is actually under it; a sibling element with its own listener simply never sees a touch that landed on the joystick). The joystick hides itself under `@media (pointer: fine)` rather than a JS device check, so a touchscreen laptop still gets it while a mouse-only desktop doesn't have it sitting uselessly in the corner. The player's starting facing direction is computed from `SvetKonfigurace.start`/`poziceSetkani` (`Math.atan2`), not hardcoded — a location where the encounter isn't roughly "in front of" the spawn point would otherwise start the player facing away from the only thing there is to walk toward.

**`Explorace3D` is lazy-loaded from `GameModule.tsx` (`React.lazy`, `Suspense` with a shared `.game-lazy-fallback`), the same reasoning as `SocialModule`/`GameModule` itself in `App.tsx`:** the three.js weight should be paid only by a player who actually opens an `'explorace'` location, not by everyone who visits `/hra`. Unlike the map image and character portraits (lazily *fetched* — real bytes over the network, cached after), this is lazily *bundled* — a separate JS chunk Vite only requests when the dynamic `import()` actually runs.

**Fáze 7 (dungeons) gave the existing dungeon (`dungeon-stinne-jeskyne`, "Molten Core," `typ: 'dungeon'`) the same 3D entrance Emberfall has, without turning it into an `'explorace'` location.** The dungeon already had real content before any of this — three sequential enemies in `combat/nepratele.ts` with HP that deliberately never restores between them (see the dungeon paragraph under **Game hub** above) — it just still opened straight into `Souboj.tsx` from the map sheet, the pre-3D flow every combat pin used before Emberfall's vertical slice. Relabelling it to `typ: 'explorace'` would have worked but would have also renamed its display type from "Dungeon" to "Průzkum" on the map sheet for no real reason — the type describes *what the place is*, not *whether it happens to have a 3D entrance*, and those turned out to be independent questions the first time a second location needed 3D. So `MapaSveta.tsx`'s `jeExplorace` flag changed from `detail?.typ === 'explorace'` to `!!SVETY_PODLE_LOKACE[detail?.id ?? '']` — a data-presence check, same shape as the pre-existing `NEPRATELE_PODLE_LOKACE` lookup right next to it — and the dungeon kept its own `typ`/label/colour while gaining "🌲 Vstoupit do světa." `world.ts` gained a `'jeskyne'` (cave) `TerenTyp` alongside `'pole'`/`'les'`: `usePlayerWorld.ts`'s decoration loop now forces cave terrain to place only rocks, never trees (a `DodecahedronGeometry` scatter, no `jeStrom` roll), and the cave's `SvetKonfigurace` uses a smaller `polomerSveta` (16 vs. Emberfall's 26) and near-black sky/fog/ground colours for a deliberately claustrophobic feel instead of another open field with a different palette.

**The dungeon has no quest attached (same as before this phase — see the dungeon paragraph under Game hub), so its 3D entrance needed its own HUD, not the quest banner Emberfall shows.** `Explorace3D.tsx`'s HUD block only ever handled the quest case; a location with `NEPRATELE_PODLE_LOKACE` entries but no `QUESTS` entry would have shown nothing at all walking in, which reads as a bug on a screen that already has a HUD pattern established. It now also checks `NEPRATELE_PODLE_LOKACE[lokace.id]` when there's no quest, and shows a distinctly-styled warning (`.explorace-quest-hud--varovani`, red instead of the quest banner's gold) naming the enemy count and the no-healing rule up front, before the player commits to walking further in. `MapaSveta.tsx`'s sheet text got the same nepratele-aware branch inside the `jeExplorace` case, for the same reason — the sheet already distinguished a single-enemy fight from a multi-enemy one for direct-combat locations (`nepratele.length > 1`), and a 3D-entrance location deserves the same distinction instead of a generic "new place to explore" line that would undersell what's actually waiting inside.

The loop itself needed no changes: `GameModule.tsx` already treats every `'explorace'`-flagged 3D world identically regardless of what's on the other side of the encounter (see the loop paragraph above) — the dungeon's `onSetkani` sets the same `soubojLokaceId` state Emberfall's does, and from there it's the pre-existing multi-enemy `Souboj.tsx`/`useSouboj.ts` flow, unmodified, that already shows the "Soupeř *n* / *total*" pill and carries HP across all three fights. Proving the loop generalizes to a second, quest-less, multi-enemy location without touching `GameModule.tsx`'s screen-tier logic at all is exactly what this phase set out to show.

**Fáze 8 (bossové) turned the dungeon's existing final enemy, "Strážce jeskyně" (already the strongest of its three, already literally named "Guardian"), into a real boss — not a new content system, three optional fields on the existing `Nepritel` (`jeBoss`, `zuriPodHp`, `zuriNasobicPoskozeni` in `combat/types.ts`).** A location doesn't need a new `Lokace`/`SvetKonfigurace`/quest to get a boss; any entry in `NEPRATELE_PODLE_LOKACE` can become one by adding those three fields, the same low-ceremony shape as everything else in that file. The Arena's and Emberfall's enemies stay plain — a boss is something a fight *can* have, not something every fight needs.

**The one real mechanic a boss adds is an enrage phase, resolved entirely inside the existing shared `vyhodnotAkci` pipeline (Fáze 4) rather than a parallel combat path.** Once a boss's HP crosses `zuriPodHp` (a fraction of its max, e.g. 0.4), `zuriTeto` — a value computed fresh on every `vyhodnotAkci` call — turns permanently true for the rest of that specific fight (carried forward via `SoubojStav.nepritelZuri`, reset to `false` when the fight moves to the next enemy in a dungeon or restarts via `zkusitZnovu`) and its counter-attack damage is multiplied by `zuriNasobicPoskozeni` from that point on. This is deliberately a one-way, single-condition switch — not a general status-effect/phase engine — because nothing yet needs more than "the boss hits harder once badly hurt," the same restraint already applied to `specialniSchopnost`'s three effect shapes when a fourth wasn't yet needed. The transition logs its own line (`"😡 {jméno} se rozzuřil!"`) exactly once, the turn it happens, computed via a `zuriPoprve` flag (`zuriTeto && !s.nepritelZuri`) so the message can't repeat on every subsequent turn the boss stays enraged.

**A boss's badge and win screen are additive reads of `Nepritel.jeBoss`, not a second reward system running alongside the existing one.** `Souboj.tsx`'s win effect still calls `recordAction('battle', odmenaXp)` exactly as before for the XP/credits/counter — a boss fight *is* a battle — and only adds one extra call, `recordAction('boss', 0)`, when the just-defeated enemy (still readable off `nepritel` at the moment `faze` becomes `'vyhra'`, since `indexNepritele` isn't advanced on the final kill) has `jeBoss` set; the `0` XP argument is deliberate, the real XP was already awarded by the `'battle'` call on the line above, this call exists only to bump the `'boss'` counter and let `COUNT_BADGES` unlock `boss_slayer` at `needed: 1`. `ActivityKind` gained `'boss'` alongside `'battle'` for exactly that — the counter/badge pairing pattern already established for every other `ActivityKind`, not a bespoke one-off `unlockBadge` call. The win screen swaps its trophy for a crown and its heading for "Boss poražen!" when `nepritel.jeBoss` is true (checked before the existing dungeon-vs-arena heading logic, since a boss fight finishing a dungeon deserves the more specific message); mid-fight, the enemy card gets a "👑 BOSS" pill and, once enraged, a pulsing red glow and "😡 Rozzuřen" tag (`.souboj-bojovnik--zuri`, an `@keyframes` alternating box-shadow colour, disabled under `prefers-reduced-motion` like every other looping animation in this file) — all of it reading `nepritelZuri`, which `useSouboj` now exposes alongside the rest of its state. The sheet text and 3D-entrance warning HUD from Fáze 7 (both already nepratele-aware for the dungeon's quest-less multi-enemy case) gained one more conditional line — "Na konci čeká boss." — when any enemy in that location's array carries `jeBoss`, so a player commits to the dungeon already knowing what's actually waiting at the end.

**Fáze 9 (character progression) added equipment — `Vybaveni` (`game/data/equipment.ts`), a deliberately third mechanic next to the shop's permanent `Predmet` and the inventory's consumable `Lup`, not a reuse of either.** The shop's `ownedItems` is boolean and account-wide with no choice involved (own it, it's always active); loot is consumed the instant it's used. Equipment is owned once (like the shop) but *equipped per character* (like level/skill points in `leveling.ts`) and can be swapped or removed at will — none of the three existing shapes fit, so it gets its own store, `useVybaveniStore.ts`, with two separately-shaped fields: `vlastnene: string[]` (account-wide ownership, same shape as `ownedItems`) and `nasazene: Record<postavaId, vybaveniId>` (which character has what equipped, mirroring `useGameCharacter.progres`'s per-character keying). Registered in `BACKUP_STORES` and validated the same item-by-item way as every other store this session added (`vybaveniValidation.ts`, ids derived from `VYBAVENI` and postava ids from the existing `POSTAVA_IDS` in `gameCharacterValidation.ts` rather than a third copy of that list).

**Equipment drops are guaranteed, not rolled — the opposite design choice from batohový loot on purpose.** `Nepritel` gained `vybaveniId` (`combat/types.ts`) alongside the existing `lupId`/`sanceNaLup`: a boss's signature relic is always awarded on the win that defeats it (`Souboj.tsx`'s win effect calls `ziskatVybaveni` unconditionally when the field is present, no `Math.random()` involved, and the store's own `ziskatVybaveni` is idempotent so refighting an already-cleared boss can't grant duplicates), while a potion drop is still a chance. A boss fight is a one-shot, deliberately memorable moment — a coin-flip on whether it pays out its whole point would undercut that in a way a common enemy's potion chance doesn't. The win screen's existing loot line (Fáze 6) gained a sibling paragraph for this, pointing the player at the Hrdina screen to actually equip what they just earned.

**The equip bonus feeds into the exact same `vypocitejBojoveStatistiky` every other combat number already goes through, not a fourth parallel calculation.** The function gained one more optional argument (`nasazenaVybaveniId`) alongside `ownedItems`, folding the equipped item's `bonusVydrz`/`bonusPoskozeni`/`bonusKriticka` into the same three-source sum the doc comment above it already described (shop + level/skills, now + equipment) — both call sites (`useSouboj.ts`'s hook, which reads `useVybaveniStore.nasazene[postava.id]` itself, and `Hrdina.tsx`'s Statistiky section) pass it the same way, so an equipped relic can never show one number on the character card and a different one in an actual fight. `Hrdina.tsx` gained a "Vybavení" section between Vylepšení and the skill tree, reusing the skill tree's own `.hr-uzel`/`.hr-vylepsit` markup rather than inventing new card styling — a plain equip/unequip toggle per owned piece, with an empty-state hint pointing at bosses when nothing's owned yet. Only one relic exists today (Amulet strážce, from the dungeon's Strážce jeskyně) — the next one is, as with every other content table in this file, one more `Vybaveni` entry plus a `vybaveniId` on whichever future boss drops it.

**Fáze 10 (Season 1 content) is being built incrementally rather than in one pass — 7 locations, 30 side quests and 5 named bosses is real scope, not a single unit of work.** The actual illustrated map (`mapa-sveta.jpg`) already names its regions — Frosthold, Shadowveil, Elenwood ("The Eternal Forest," Greenhaven's own subtitle), Emberwaste, Dawnbreak Fields, Sandcrown, Verdant Vale — and those don't line up one-to-one with the original spec's location list (there's no separate "Old Forest" pin distinct from Greenhaven's forest on the actual art; a second pin for the same patch of trees would look wrong on the illustration). Rather than inventing pins the art doesn't support, each Season 1 story beat attaches to whichever existing city pin already carries the matching region theme — Greenhaven *is* "Old Forest" here, its 3D world representing the Eternal Forest surrounding the town, the same "the world is the land around the town, not the town itself" relationship Emberfall's fields already established to Emberfall.

**Greenhaven, Voidspire and Frostheim all keep their existing `typ: 'mesto'` on the map — this is the decoupling from Fáze 7 (dungeon) proving out on three differently-typed locations, not a one-off special case.** `MapaSveta.tsx`'s `jeExplorace` already reads `SVETY_PODLE_LOKACE` presence, not `Lokace.typ`, so giving any of these cities a 3D world needed *zero* code changes in `MapaSveta.tsx`, `Explorace3D.tsx`, `GameModule.tsx` or `useSouboj.ts` — only data, each time the same four-file recipe: a `SvetKonfigurace` in `world.ts` (Greenhaven's dark forest palette, Voidspire's corrupted-violet plain, Frostheim's icy navy — the latter two both reuse `teren: 'pole'`/`'jeskyne'` from earlier locations with an entirely different palette, proving terrain type and colour palette are independent knobs, not one setting), one quest in `quests.ts` (each a single `'boj'` objective — deliberately not yet the multi-location multi-objective shape the quest engine's comments already flagged as coming "soon," saved for a location that actually needs it rather than forced here for its own sake), two `STORY` sequences per quest, and one boss-only `Nepritel` entry (Strážce lesa at Greenhaven, Stínová královna at Voidspire, Zamrzlý strážce at Frostheim — the spec's "Forest Guardian," "Shadow Queen" and "Frozen Guardian" — each a single `'explorace'` encounter shaped like Emberfall's, not a `dungeon`-style multi-enemy chain).

**Four relics now exist, and they were deliberately built to spread across four different archetypes rather than four flavours of the same stat.** Amulet strážce (dungeon) is balanced-defensive (`bonusVydrz`/`bonusKriticka` together), Trnová koruna (Greenhaven) leans offensive (`bonusPoskozeni`), Stínový plášť (Voidspire) leans risky (`bonusKriticka` dominant, little else), Ledový štít (Frostheim) is pure tank (`bonusVydrz` alone, nothing else) — with only `useVybaveniStore`'s single equip slot, relics that actually pull in different directions is what turns "which one is equipped" into a real build choice instead of "whichever was found most recently."

**Each story location's `dialogPriDokonceni` line references the ones before it, turning independent side content into a felt through-line without any code coordinating it.** Emberfall's ending hints at something waking up beyond the town; Greenhaven's guard explicitly says "Napřed Emberfall, teď Věčný les"; Voidspire's guard goes one further — "Emberfall, Greenhaven, teď my... kdosi budí staré strážce jednoho po druhém"; Frostheim's guard closes the loop so far — "Emberfall, Greenhaven, Voidspire, teď my... a blíží se to k srdci království." Nothing in `useQuestStore`/`GameModule.tsx` enforces this ordering or cross-references — it's purely the `STORY` dialogue text itself accumulating a sentence per location, the cheapest possible way to make independent boss fights read as one unfolding season rather than unrelated errands.

**Solace closes the main storyline, and it's the one location in this chain that reuses the dungeon's multi-enemy shape instead of Emberfall's single-encounter one — on purpose, to make the finale feel heavier than the four story bosses before it, and it needed zero new mechanism to do that.** `NEPRATELE_PODLE_LOKACE.solace` is an array of two (Probuzený strážný, then První strážce as the boss — the spec's "The First Guardian"), so the existing "HP never restores between enemies at one location" rule that has applied to the dungeon since before this session automatically makes Solace the hardest fight in the game, no new state or flag required. `MapaSveta.tsx`'s long-standing "hlavní město je vyhrazené pro dějovou linku, ta zatím nevznikla" placeholder — reserved since the map rewrite, long before this phase existed — is superseded automatically the moment `SVETY_PODLE_LOKACE.solace` exists, because `jeExplorace` is checked before the capital-specific fallback in the sheet-text ternary; nothing about that branch order had to change to retire the placeholder now that the story it was reserved for finally shipped.

**Tuning Solace's two fights taught a real balance lesson, not just theming, and it's recorded as a comment on the boss entry rather than a number picked by feel and forgotten.** The first pass gave the First Guardian noticeably higher stats than every other boss ("it's the finale, it should hit harder") and the same kind of Playwright verification pass used to confirm every phase in this session couldn't clear it in six retries with a fresh, unlevelled level-1 character — every other Fáze-10 location was implicitly beatable from a brand-new character (that's what the identical verification pattern had already confirmed location after location), so a finale that wasn't wasn't a deliberate difficulty spike, it was an inconsistency the test caught. The fix wasn't to make the fight easy — it's still the second enemy in a no-heal-between sequence, so the *combination* stays the hardest fight in the game — it was to stop *also* stacking harder per-enemy numbers on top of that already-real structural difficulty. The boss's own stats now sit in the same range as the other four bosses; the two-fight, no-healing shape alone is what makes Solace the climax.

**Five relics now exist**, the fifth (Koruna prvního strážce, from Solace) deliberately breaking the "one archetype, one relic" pattern the first four established — it carries all three bonuses at once, and is the strongest single number in each category the equip slot has seen, because a season finale's reward should read as strictly better than what led up to it, not just differently specialized.

Still deliberately deferred, exactly as declined at the start of this session and reconfirmed here rather than forgotten: the remaining 25 side quests across all six locations. Each follows this same four-file recipe against whichever existing pin's region theme fits, added incrementally rather than fabricated wholesale in a single pass.

### Souboj — the second game (`src/fighting/`)

**A real Mortal-Kombat-style fighting game, original to Buddy, is being built as the second card in the Games hub menu (see above) — a genuinely different game from Buddyheim's RPG, not a reskin of it, and the user explicitly asked to discuss the plan phase-by-phase before any code, per a new standing process this session adopted for large redesigns.** Three product decisions were settled up front via `AskUserQuestion` before any code was written: (1) **how the phone reaches the TV** — a device sitting at the TV opens the app itself in "TV mode" and phones connect to it as controllers over the network, Jackbox-style, rather than true OS-level screen casting (Chromecast/AirPlay), which was explicitly rejected as unreliable cross-device, near-unusable on iPhone, and impossible to verify in this sandbox anyway; (2) **the roster is brand-new characters made just for this game**, not a trimmed reuse of the RPG's five heroes — Buddyheim's cast stays Buddyheim's; (3) **visual style is 2D/2.5D stylized**, matching the app's existing flat-art pipeline (world map, character portraits), not full 3D — there is no existing 3D-character pipeline in this project to build a fighter on, only `explorace/`'s primitive-geometry environments, which don't transfer to a fighting game's up-close character rendering.

**The build is planned as five phases, agreed with the user before starting: Fáze 0 (networking skeleton, done first because it's the riskiest/least-verifiable part), Fáze 1 (core fight engine as pure functions), Fáze 2 (4 characters with movesets matching the 4 reference action buttons), Fáze 3 (TV-side visual rendering — arena, health bars, character sprites), Fáze 4 (wire the finished game into the real Games hub menu card, replacing its current `BRZY` placeholder), Fáze 5 (AI opponent/balance, later).** This section documents Fáze 0 only — the rest hasn't been built yet.

**Fáze 0 is a phone-as-controller / TV-as-display pairing layer with zero new backend, built by copying an already-proven pattern rather than inventing one: Supabase Realtime *broadcast* channels, the exact idiom `social/api.ts`'s `sledovatPritomnost()` already uses for chat presence/typing.** A broadcast channel needs no database table and no RLS — it's ephemeral pub/sub scoped to a channel name, and the app is already signed-in-gated so Realtime auth is already satisfied by the existing session. `src/fighting/network.ts`'s `hostujMistnost(kod, handlery)` (TV side) opens a channel named `souboj-mistnost-<KOD>` and listens for `pripojit`/`vstup` broadcast events; `pripojSeJakoOvladac(kod, hracId, jmeno, handlery)` (phone side) joins the same channel, and — only once `SUBSCRIBED` actually fires, never before, or the first message would go nowhere — broadcasts its own `pripojit` event once. The TV assigns each connecting player a slot (1 or 2) and acks with a `pripojeno` event carrying that slot; a phone that broadcasts `pripojit` again with the same `hracId` (a brief reconnect) is re-slotted onto its *existing* slot rather than stealing the other one. `vygenerujKodMistnosti()` reuses the same no-`I`/`O`/`0`/`1` alphabet `friend_code` already established, since a 4-character room code is exactly the kind of thing that gets read aloud across a room. `poradiKanalu`, a module-level counter appended to every channel name, is the same "every channel needs a unique name" fix this file already documents for the inbox/presence collision — without it, leaving and re-entering the screen would try to resubscribe an already-subscribed channel and throw.

**Because this game cannot function offline at all — unlike almost everything else in this app, where cloud is an optional bonus — `network.ts` says so up front in its own header comment, and both `FightingModule.tsx` entry points fail closed with a plain-language message when `isSupabaseConfigured` is false, rather than the silent no-op the rest of the codebase uses for a missing cloud config.** There is no local-only fallback for "the phone talks to the TV" — that link only exists as a Realtime channel.

**The controller screen (`src/fighting/components/Ovladac.tsx`) is a code-entry form that transitions to a full-bleed gamepad UI once the TV acks a slot — a discrete d-pad (4 separate direction buttons, not an analog stick, since a fighting game's own input model is closer to a real arcade d-pad than the RPG's 3D-exploration joystick) plus the 4 colored circular action buttons from the reference image (👊 red punch, 🦵 blue kick, 🛡️ green block, ✨ gold special), all driven by `onPointerDown`/`onPointerUp`/`onPointerCancel` + `setPointerCapture`, not `onClick` — the game needs to know how long a button is *held*, not just that it was tapped once, matching the reasoning `VirtualniJoystick.tsx` already established for the RPG's own touch input.** `.souboj-ovladac` is deliberately not constrained to the app's usual 480px card shell (`touch-action: none; user-select: none`) — it's meant to be used as a real physical controller filling the whole screen, not a scrollable page.

**The TV screen (`src/fighting/components/TvHost.tsx`) shows the room code plus one panel per player slot, each rendering a live, wordless confirmation that its inputs are actually arriving** — a small dot that translates toward whichever direction is currently held, and each action-button icon lighting up while its matching phone button is held — deliberately no game rendering yet, since that's Fáze 3's job; Fáze 0 only had to prove the pairing and input-relay mechanism works end to end.

**Fáze 0's own verification hit a hard, precisely-diagnosed sandbox limitation: a live phone↔TV Realtime round-trip cannot be exercised inside this environment at all.** A first attempt wrote a genuine two-`BrowserContext` Playwright spec (TV context + controller context) against the real Supabase Realtime WebSocket, mirroring how Secret Chat's crypto round-trip was verified earlier in this session — it failed, the controller stuck forever on "Připojuji…". Diagnosed by temporarily logging the `.subscribe()` status/error in both `hostujMistnost`/`pripojSeJakoOvladac` and watching both browser contexts' console output via a throwaway diagnostic spec (deleted after use): `WebSocket connection to 'wss://…supabase.co/realtime/v1/websocket?…' failed: Establishing a tunnel via proxy server failed.` Cross-checked against the sandbox's own proxy status endpoint, which showed repeated `connect_rejected`/403 entries for the Supabase host specifically — and confirmed definitively by `/root/.ccr/README.md`, which lists **WebSocket upgrades** explicitly under "Not supported through the proxy (report, do not work around)." This isn't specific to this feature or a bug to keep chasing — it's the same reason `tests/e2e/helpers.ts`'s `mockSupabase()` has only ever intercepted HTTP, never WebSocket, documented elsewhere in this file for Secret Chat/Stories' own Realtime-dependent checks; Souboj is simply the first feature whose *entire* purpose is that WebSocket link, so it's the first to hit the wall head-on rather than working around it with a `page.reload()`.

**What Fáze 0 actually verifies, given that constraint: each side's own UI behavior in isolation, via a temporary Playwright spec (deleted after passing) — not a real pairing.** The TV side generates a room code matching the expected 4-character alphabet, shows both player panels in a "waiting" state, and its back button returns to the game-selection menu; the controller side's submit button starts disabled, enables once 4 characters are entered (auto-uppercased), and — this is the one genuinely load-bearing assertion — visibly enters and *stays* in a "Připojuji…" state with no gamepad UI ever appearing, since no TV-side acknowledgment can ever arrive in this sandbox. **Real phone-to-TV pairing has not been verified anywhere and can only be verified by the user on two real devices** — this is stated plainly rather than implied, the same honesty this file already applies to Form Check's on-device ML (verified by code review, no camera in the sandbox) and the biometrics escape hatch's WebAuthn quirks.

**Routing**: `App.tsx` gained a lazy `FightingModule` import (`React.lazy(() => import('@/fighting/FightingModule'))`, matching `GameModule`/`SocialModule`'s existing lazy pattern — confirmed via the build output producing its own separate `FightingModule-*.js`/`.css` chunks, not merged into the eager main bundle) and a new route, `/hra/souboj`, gated the same as every other route on `dovnitr` (signed in). **It is deliberately not yet reachable from the Games hub menu** — `GamesHubModule.tsx`'s second card is still the generic `BRZY` placeholder from the menu's own first pass, and wiring a real card to this route is explicitly reserved for Fáze 4, once there's an actual game behind it worth advertising rather than a bare pairing screen.

**Fáze 1 (`src/fighting/combat/`) is the fight simulation itself, and it's built exactly the way this codebase already knows how to build a testable game system — pure functions and constants with no React, no store, no network, matching `game/leveling.ts`'s own "the whole system as pure functions/constants" shape.** `krokSouboje(stav, vstupy, deltaMs)` is the one entry point: given the current two-fighter state, a fresh `HracVstup` for each side (held movement direction, held block, and an `akce` that's a one-shot press-edge event, not a held state — the caller decides when "held" becomes "just pressed," the engine only ever sees the edge) and how much time passed, it returns the next state. Nothing about *how* input reaches the engine is decided here — a real controller's broadcast stream turning into per-tick edges is explicitly Fáze 3's job, same "engine doesn't know where input comes from" boundary `useSouboj.ts` already draws between itself and `Souboj.tsx` in the RPG's own combat system.

**Damage is a fixed number per action, not a random range, and that's a deliberate testability choice, not a balance one — `Math.random()` anywhere in the tick function would make every test either flaky or need to mock the RNG, and this engine's whole point in Fáze 1 is provably deterministic replay given the same inputs, which local multiplayer (two controllers, one shared TV) needs anyway to stay in sync.** `AKCE_DATA` holds one entry per `UtocnaAkce` (`udar`/`kop`/`specialni` — not `blok`, which is a held defensive state checked at the moment of an incoming hit, never an action of its own) with a flat `poskozeni`/`dosah`/`trvaniMs`/`cenaMany`; Fáze 2's four real characters will differentiate movesets by swapping which of these numbers each character's version of an action carries, not by reintroducing randomness.

**A bojovník is "busy" — ignoring all input — for exactly two reasons, and both use the same mechanism: a countdown timer decremented every tick, checked once at the top of that fighter's own per-tick resolution.** Landing a *non-blocked* hit sets the defender's `zranitelnostKonci` to `HITSTUN_MS`; starting *any* action (whether it ends up connecting or not) sets the attacker's own `utokKonci` to that action's `trvaniMs` — a whiffed kick still costs its full recovery time, the same "committing to a move has a cost even on a miss" rule real fighting games use. While either timer is nonzero, movement, blocking and new actions are all ignored for that fighter that tick, and `blokuje` is forced `false` regardless of what was true before — a stunned or mid-swing fighter can't also be defending, which matters because block state is read at the *exact* moment a hit resolves, not cached from an earlier tick.

**Mana is deliberately not free to use from the start — a fighter opens a round at 0, not full, so "spam the special" isn't even possible turn one.** It regenerates passively every tick (even while busy — recovering from your own attack still earns meter) and gets a flat bonus for every hit that actually lands, win or lose, blocked or not — `MANA_ZA_ZASAH` only ever applies to a *connecting* attacker, a whiff earns nothing beyond the passive trickle. A special attempted without enough mana isn't a wasted turn in the harsh sense — it costs no cooldown at all, the press just does nothing, the same "double-tap on an already-liked post is a no-op, not an error" leniency this file already applies to over-eager input elsewhere.

**Hit resolution happens in one fixed order — player 0's action, then player 1's — specifically so a genuinely simultaneous exchange (both fighters throw a hit in the same tick) always resolves the same way given the same inputs, which is what makes replaying a recorded input sequence produce an identical fight.** Distance is measured *after* both fighters have already moved that tick, not before — so a step forward that closes into range and an attack thrown the same frame can connect, matching how a real fighting game reads "did the hit land" against where everyone actually ended up, not where they started. A round that ends (either fighter's `hp` hits 0) freezes: `krokSouboje` returns the exact same object reference on every subsequent call rather than recomputing a no-op state — cheap to check (`stav === novyStav` is true) and matches `useQuestStore`'s own established Zustand-selector lesson that returning a genuinely new object when nothing changed is what causes silent re-render bugs downstream, here applied to a plain function instead of a store.

**Verified with `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` (all clean) — no Playwright spec needed for this phase, unlike Fáze 0.** Unlike the networking skeleton, nothing here touches a browser API, Supabase, or even React — the whole engine is exercised by `tests/unit/fighting-combat.test.ts` (11 new cases: movement and its arena-boundary clamps, an out-of-range whiff still costing its own cooldown, a landed hit's damage/hitstun/mana-gain, a blocked hit's reduced damage and hitstun-free result, a special refused for lack of mana vs. one that lands and spends it, a busy fighter's inputs being ignored until its timer clears, and a KO freezing the state to the same object reference on the next call) — the same "pure functions, no rendering, no browser needed" tier `xp.test.ts`/`combat.test.ts`/`finance.test.ts` already established for exactly this class of logic. `krokSouboje`, `vytvorBojovnika`, `vytvorSoubojStav`, `AKCE_DATA` and every tuning constant are exported; the per-tick internals (`tikBojovnika`, hit resolution) stay private, so the tests exercise the same public contract a future caller (Fáze 3's render loop) will actually use, not implementation details that could drift out of sync with it.

**Fáze 2 (`src/fighting/combat/postavy.ts`) is four brand-new characters — Pyra, Bulwark, Volt, Onyx, explicitly not the RPG's five heroes (per the `AskUserQuestion` decision recorded above) — built as data-only multipliers over Fáze 1's engine, not a second combat system or new mechanics.** Each `Postava` carries six multipliers (`maxHpNasobic`/`rychlostNasobic`/`poskozeniNasobic`/`obranaNasobic`/`dosahNasobic`/`cenaManyNasobic`) applied to the engine's existing `AKCE_DATA` numbers — a character makes udar/kop/specialni hit harder, cost less mana, or reach further, but never adds a fifth action or a new state machine. This mirrors the RPG's own "one shared card pool, a character's bonus only multiplies matching cards" restraint (`game/combat/karty.ts`) — the moveset genuinely differs per character without the engine growing new shapes to test.

**The four are deliberately spread across distinct archetypes, the same "four different archetypes, not four flavours of one stat" reasoning already recorded for the RPG's own relics (Fáze 10 above) — a glass-cannon striker (Pyra: +damage/+speed, −HP, −defense), a slow tank (Bulwark: +HP/+defense/+reach, −speed, pricier special), a cheap-special speedster (Volt: fastest moves and movement, cheapest special, −damage/−reach), and a reach-focused all-rounder (Onyx: every multiplier neutral except a long dosah) — so which one a player picks is a real style choice, not a strictly-better-or-worse pick.** Onyx is also the engine's default (`VYCHOZI_POSTAVA`, used when `vytvorBojovnika`/`vytvorSoubojStav` are called with no character argument) precisely *because* its multipliers are neutral everywhere except reach — and reach only ever changes a hit/miss distance threshold, never an exact damage/cooldown/mana number, so every one of Fáze 1's own already-shipped unit tests (which assert exact `poskozeni`/`trvaniMs`/`cenaMany` values, never an exact `dosah`) kept passing completely unmodified once characters existed. Confirmed directly, not assumed: all 11 Fáze 1 tests ran again with zero changes after this phase's wiring landed, and still passed.

**`efektivniAkceData(postavaId, akce)` is the one place the engine's generic action formula and a character's personal style combine into one concrete number — `tikBojovnika` and `vyhodnotZasahPokudZahajen` both call it exclusively now, neither ever reads `AKCE_DATA` directly for a live fighter's own numbers any more (only `efektivniAkceData`'s own internals do, as the neutral base to multiply from).** Movement speed gets the same treatment inline (`RYCHLOST_POHYBU * POSTAVY[b.postavaId].rychlostNasobic`) rather than being folded into `efektivniAkceData`, since movement isn't itself an `UtocnaAkce` — it has no `AkceData` shape to return. Defense is deliberately evaluated as the *target's* trait, not the attacker's, and applied in a fixed order: distance/hit-or-miss first (the attacker's own `dosahNasobic`), then the defender's `obranaNasobic` against the base damage, then block's flat reduction on top of that — obrana is "this character is naturally tougher," block is "this specific defender chose to guard this specific hit," and the two stack rather than either overriding the other.

**Verified with `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` (all clean), again no Playwright needed — this phase is still pure data and pure functions, nothing touches React, a browser API, or Supabase.** `tests/unit/fighting-postavy.test.ts` (11 new cases, 79 total across both fighting-engine test files) checks the roster shape itself (exactly four ids, none matching an RPG hero name), Onyx's neutral-except-reach multiplier set, `efektivniAkceData`'s scaling math for a non-default character (Volt's shortened cooldown and cheapened special), each character's scaled starting HP, and — the load-bearing cases — full `krokSouboje` runs proving the *actual, wired-together* behavior differs by character: Bulwark measurably takes less damage than Onyx from the identical hit, Pyra measurably deals more, obrana and block combine multiplicatively rather than one silently overriding the other, and Volt measurably out-moves and out-recovers Onyx given the identical input and delta. **A bug in the test file itself, not the engine, was caught and fixed before trusting the suite**: an early draft computed "damage dealt" as the flat `MAX_HP` constant minus a defender's post-hit HP, which is wrong the moment the defender isn't the default character — Bulwark's actual starting HP is `MAX_HP * 1.25`, not `MAX_HP`, so the arithmetic produced a nonsensical negative "damage" figure; fixed by reading the defender's own `.maxHp` off the freshly-created fighter instead of assuming the flat constant, the same class of mistake this file's own "test the fix, don't assume the number" discipline exists to catch even in a test that never touches Supabase or a browser.

**Character selection itself — an actual picker UI on the controller, TV-side portraits — is deliberately not built in this phase.** The reference-image visual style for Souboj's characters (2D/2.5D stylized, matching the app's existing flat-art pipeline per the `AskUserQuestion` decision) means a real character-select screen needs actual character art to show, which belongs in Fáze 3 alongside the rest of the TV-side visual rendering it was already scoped for — building a selection UI now would mean either placeholder art nobody asked for, or building the same screen twice.

**Fáze 3 (TV-side visual rendering) reordered one thing from the original plan before writing any of it: character selection moved from "something the controller does after connecting" to a genuinely local step it does *before* ever calling `pripojSeJakoOvladac`.** The practical reason is the same WebSocket wall Fáze 0 already hit and documented — nothing that depends on a live phone↔TV round trip is verifiable in this sandbox at all, so any part of Fáze 3 that could be built as pure local React state instead was, specifically so it stays inside what a Playwright spec here can actually prove. `types.ts`'s `PripojitPayload` gained a required `postavaId`, sent with the very first `pripojit` broadcast rather than as a second message after connecting — one round trip, not two, and it also means the TV never has to render a "waiting for them to pick a character" sub-state on top of "waiting for them to connect."

**`src/fighting/combat/loop.ts` is the bridge between "what the controller is currently holding" and "one tick of Fáze 1's engine," and between "one `SoubojStav`" and "what a health bar's width should be" — pure functions again, same `game/leveling.ts`-style discipline as Fáze 1/2, deliberately kept out of `TvHost.tsx` itself so it stays unit-testable without a browser.** `detekujAkci`/`sestavVstup` do the edge detection `HracVstup.akce` requires (see Fáze 1: the engine only ever sees a fresh press, never "still held") — the controller broadcasts *held* button state (`stisknuto: true/false` on press/release, unchanged from Fáze 0), so something has to diff "held now" against "held last tick" before the engine can consume it, and that something is `sestavVstup`, called once per player per `requestAnimationFrame` tick in `TvHost.tsx`, never inside the engine itself. `PORADI_AKCI` (`udar` > `kop` > `specialni`) makes the diff deterministic even in the vanishingly rare case two action buttons land as newly-pressed in the same tick — consistent with Fáze 1's own "same inputs, same outcome" requirement for future replay/sync. `hpProcenta`/`manaProcenta`/`poziceProcenta`/`vizualniStavBojovnika`/`maNaSpecial` are the read side — clamped 0–100 percentages and a small `'ko' | 'hitstun' | 'blok' | 'utok' | 'idle'` enum a CSS class can key off directly, with an explicit, commented precedence order (KO beats everything, hitstun beats blok — a stunned fighter can't also be shown blocking, matching `tikBojovnika`'s own `blokuje` override during hitstun) rather than a chain of independent booleans a caller could combine incorrectly.

**`Bojiste.tsx` is the actual arena render, and it's deliberately as inert as `game/combat/Souboj.tsx`'s own presentational half — one `SoubojStav` and the two players' names in as props, JSX out, no hook that touches `requestAnimationFrame`, Supabase, or even local component state.** `TvHost.tsx` alone owns the game loop and hands `Bojiste` a finished, immutable snapshot every tick; `Bojiste` just reads `loop.ts`'s helpers to turn that snapshot into two health/mana bars, two positioned fighter tokens, and (once `stav.stavKola === 'konec'`) a winner banner. Keeping it this thin is what makes the arena screen paintable and checkable independent of ever proving a live match actually reaches it.

**`TvHost.tsx` owns the actual match: once both connected players carry a `postavaId`, one `useEffect` keyed on a derived `pripraveno` boolean (not on the raw `hraci` array, which gets a new reference on *every single button press*) starts a `vytvorSoubojStav` + `requestAnimationFrame` loop exactly once, rather than tearing down and restarting on every input broadcast.** That distinction mattered in practice, not just in theory: an early version of this effect depended on `hraci` directly, and — because `setHraci` produces a new array reference on every incoming `vstup` broadcast — the effect's own cleanup (`cancelAnimationFrame`) fired and the guard clause (`if (soubojStavRef.current) return`) then skipped rescheduling, silently killing the whole loop the instant the very first button was pressed after the match started. Caught by reasoning through the dependency array before it shipped, not by a failing test — this sandbox can't drive a live match to observe it directly (see below), so this is a case where understanding *why* a hook re-runs mattered more than any test could catch it here. The loop itself reads the freshest held input off `hraciRef` (a plain ref mirrored from `hraci` on every render), not off a value captured in the effect's own closure, so the per-tick `sestavVstup`/`krokSouboje` calls never see stale button state even though the effect that started the loop only ran once.

**Character portraits are not real art — Souboj still has no art pipeline (see the phase-0 product decisions above), so `VyberPostavy.tsx`/`Bojiste.tsx` both render each `Postava.ikona` emoji at a larger size inside a styled card/token instead, the same honest placeholder-not-invented-detail restraint this file's own `BRZY` sections already apply elsewhere.** A real illustrated roster is scope for whenever the visual-style phase gets its own dedicated pass, not something to fake with unrelated stock art now.

**What Fáze 3 could actually verify in this sandbox, and what it honestly couldn't, following the exact same disclosure Fáze 0 already established: nothing that requires two browser contexts talking over a live Supabase Realtime WebSocket is provable here, because the proxy still doesn't support the upgrade at all (unchanged since Fáze 0's diagnosis).** What moved character selection to a pre-network step bought back is real, though — a temporary Playwright spec (deleted after passing) confirmed the controller's local-only path end to end: entering a room code lands on "Vyber si bojovníka," not directly on a connection attempt; the confirm button stays disabled until a card is tapped, and tapping one visibly marks it selected; confirming enters "Připojuji…" and correctly never reaches the d-pad/action-button gamepad UI (since no TV acknowledgment can ever arrive here); the back button from character-select returns to the code-entry form; and the TV side generates a 4-character room code and shows both player panels waiting, with no `.souboj-bojiste` rendered before anyone connects. **A genuine phone-to-TV match — both sides pairing, a real fight actually playing out on the TV screen — has not been verified anywhere and can only be verified by the user on two real devices**, stated plainly rather than implied, same as Fáze 0's own pairing claim and Form Check's on-device ML.

**Verified with `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` (all clean — `FightingModule` stayed its own lazy chunk, confirmed from the actual build output, not merged into the eager main bundle) plus `tests/unit/fighting-loop.test.ts`** (17 new cases — edge detection with and without simultaneous presses, `sestavVstup`'s assembly, percentage clamping at both ends, position-to-percentage at the arena's start/middle/end, every branch of `vizualniStavBojovnika`'s precedence order, and `maNaSpecial`'s boundary at exactly-enough mana — 96 unit tests total in the project now) and a temporary Playwright spec covering exactly the locally-reachable path described above, deleted after passing. `auth.spec.ts`'s four cases stayed unaffected (this phase touched no auth-gate routing).

**Fáze 4 replaced the Games hub's generic "Další hra / BRZY" placeholder tile (`GamesHubModule.tsx`, dating back to when that menu first shipped — see **Games hub** above) with a real, working Souboj card — the last piece of plumbing needed before the game is reachable the normal way, through Play, rather than only by typing `/hra/souboj` directly.** `Hra.nahled` became `string | null` rather than always a URL, with a new optional `emoji` field as the fallback — Souboj still has no illustrated art at all (see the phase-0 product decisions above: no 3D-character pipeline, no art generation done yet for this game specifically), and inventing a stock image or a fake screenshot to fill the tile would have been the exact "don't fake it" mistake this file's own placeholder discipline (Library's `BRZY` tile, the admin panel's `BRZY` toggles, Souboj's own emoji-only character cards from Fáze 3) exists to avoid. `.games-card-nahled--prazdny` (already in the CSS, previously only ever used by the now-deleted generic placeholder) grew a centered, drop-shadowed emoji instead of staying empty — the same box, a real icon instead of nothing.

**The placeholder tile itself is gone outright, not left behind as a third "more coming" slot** — with the second real game now filling the spot the placeholder used to occupy, keeping a separate "there will be a third" tile with nothing behind it would have been speculative filler this session's own restraint already argues against building (`GamesHubModule.tsx`'s own header comment already called it "obsah/název přibude, jakmile bude jasné, co to vlastně bude" — that condition is what changed). `.games-card--soon`/`.games-badge-soon` stayed in the CSS file unused for now rather than deleted, the same "leave a rule in place for a plausible near-future consumer" call this file already made for `ProfilGoals.css`'s orphaned classes — a third game reintroducing the same "coming soon" tile shape is a likelier outcome here than not.

**Verified with `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` (all clean — `GamesHubModule` stayed in the eager main bundle exactly as before, `FightingModule` still its own separate lazy chunk, so wiring the card cost no bundle-weight regression on either side) and a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): `/hra` renders both Buddyheim and Souboj, with no "BRZY" text left anywhere on the page; tapping the Souboj card navigates to `/hra/souboj` and lands on the existing role-choice screen ("Hostovat na TV" / "Připojit se jako ovladač"). `auth.spec.ts`'s four cases stayed unaffected (no auth-gate routing touched).

**Fáze 5 was only ever scoped as "AI opponent/balance, later" with no detail — before writing anything, the two open questions (what the AI is *for*, and how sophisticated it should be) went back through `AskUserQuestion`, the same discipline the three phase-0 product decisions used, rather than guessing.** The answer settled the scope precisely: a solo mode against the computer (not a balance-only pass, not both), and a simple reactive bot (not tiered difficulty). That's a real, load-bearing product decision, not an implementation detail — a "difficulty selector" or a "training dummy that never attacks" would have been just as plausible reads of "AI opponent" without asking.

**`src/fighting/combat/ai.ts` is `pripravAkciAi(ja, souper, nahodne?)` — one more pure function in the same `game/leveling.ts`-style family as everything else in `combat/`, and deliberately stateless: no memory of what it did last tick, only a fresh read of both fighters' current `BojovnikStav` every call.** That "reactive, not planning" restraint is what "simple reactive bot" from the `AskUserQuestion` answer actually cashes out to in code — no target selection, no combo sequencing, no lookahead, just three rules evaluated in order: block reactively if the opponent is mid-attack and in range of that specific action's `dosah`; move toward the opponent if out of `kop`'s range; attack (mostly `kop`, occasionally `specialni` if mana allows) with a per-tick probability if in range. Being stateless is also what keeps the bot honest about not being able to spam: a naive version that returned `akce: 'kop'` on every tick the opponent stayed in range would auto-fire the instant each `utokKonci` recovery ended, something a real player's held-button input can't do (`sestavVstup`'s edge detection in `loop.ts` exists specifically to prevent that) — so the bot's own attack decision is gated behind `AI_SANCE_UTOKU`, a per-tick coin flip, which naturally throttles it to occasional attempts instead of continuous pressure, without needing any held-vs-pressed state of its own.

**`Math.random()` inside `pripravAkciAi` doesn't violate Fáze 1's own "no randomness" rule for the engine — that rule was about `krokSouboje` needing to replay identically given the same recorded inputs, for a future networked two-controller match to stay in sync; the AI's *decisions* are themselves just one more `HracVstup` fed into that same deterministic engine, computed entirely locally on the TV with nothing to keep in sync with.** `nahodne` is still injectable (defaulting to `Math.random`) purely for test determinism, the same reasoning `avatarColor.ts`/`shareLink.ts` never needed but this file's own testing discipline still calls for whenever a pure function's output depends on chance — `tests/unit/fighting-ai.test.ts` feeds it a small queue of fixed values instead of mocking the global.

**`nahodnaPostava()` picks the computer's character uniformly from the same four-character roster a human would pick from (`VSECHNY_POSTAVY`) — no easier or harder character reserved for the AI, since nothing about "simple reactive bot" implied the opponent should be mechanically weaker, only that its *decision-making* should be simple.**

**The trigger lives entirely on the TV side, not the controller — `TvHost.tsx`'s waiting screen gains a "🤖 Hrát proti počítači" button, shown only once a real Hráč 1 has connected and slot 2 is still empty.** That gating is deliberate, not incidental: solo mode is what a person does when a *second* phone isn't available, not a mode reachable before anyone has connected at all — offering it earlier would mean nothing for it to actually play against. Clicking it is a plain `setHraci` call assigning a synthetic `HracStav` to slot 2 under a fixed sentinel id (`AI_HRAC_ID = 'pocitac-ai'`), never touching `network.ts` at all — no broadcast, no `hostujMistnost` involvement, because there's no second device to coordinate with. That one `setHraci` call is enough to satisfy the existing `pripraveno` gate (`hraci[0]?.postavaId && hraci[1]?.postavaId`) unchanged from Fáze 3, so the match-start effect fires exactly the same way it already does for two real controllers — solo mode needed no changes to *how* a match starts, only to *what* can fill the second slot.

**Inside the per-tick game loop, the AI slot's input comes from `pripravAkciAi(soubojStavRef.current.hraci[1], soubojStavRef.current.hraci[0])` instead of `sestavVstup`, decided by a plain `a1.hracId === AI_HRAC_ID` check — and the edge-detection bookkeeping (`vstupPredchoziRef`) is skipped for that slot entirely, since the bot has no held-button state to diff against a previous tick in the first place.** Everything downstream — `krokSouboje`, `Bojiste.tsx`'s rendering, the win/KO screen, "Nový zápas" — is completely unaware a slot might be AI-controlled; a `HracVstup` is a `HracVstup` regardless of which function produced it, the same "engine doesn't know where input comes from" boundary this file has stated since Fáze 1.

**Verification followed the exact same honest split Fáze 0/3 already established, and for the same underlying reason: the bot's own decision logic is pure and fully testable, but the button that starts a solo match only ever appears after a real controller has connected, which this sandbox's WebSocket limitation still makes unreachable.** `tests/unit/fighting-ai.test.ts` (11 new cases — moving toward the opponent in both directions, attacking under the probability threshold and correctly doing nothing above it (proving no auto-spam), falling back from an unaffordable `specialni` to `kop`, blocking only when the opponent is both mid-attack and within that action's own reach, *not* blocking when out of range or when the opponent isn't attacking at all, and `nahodnaPostava`'s boundary behavior at `nahodne() → 0` and `→ 1`) brings the project to 107 unit tests. A temporary Playwright spec (deleted after passing) confirmed the one thing genuinely checkable without a live socket — the "Hrát proti počítači" button never renders prematurely, before any real player has connected — the same restraint as Fáze 0/3's own "verify what's locally reachable, disclose what isn't" posture; **a real solo match, on a real TV against a real phone, has not been played end-to-end anywhere and still needs the user to confirm it on real devices**, exactly the caveat this section has carried since Fáze 0. `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` all clean (`FightingModule` still its own lazy chunk), `auth.spec.ts`'s four cases unaffected.

**This completes all five originally agreed phases.** Souboj is reachable from the Hub through Play like every other piece of content, playable solo against a simple bot without needing a second phone, and playable with a friend once real two-device pairing is confirmed by the user — the one thing that was never verifiable inside this sandbox at any phase and still isn't.

**Fáze 6, a follow-up pass after all five original phases already shipped, closed the two gaps the roster/arena had explicitly deferred — real character graphics instead of emoji, and visual/animation polish for the TV arena — both confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` (multiSelect) before any code, per this session's own standing process.** Both had been named out loud in Fáze 2/3's own write-up as deliberately not built yet ("Souboj still has no art pipeline," "there is no real 3D asset pipeline in this project"), not oversights.

**`PostavaGrafika.tsx` is hand-authored inline SVG, not a raster pipeline like the RPG's own `public/postavy/<id>.jpg` — a real, disclosed constraint, not a style preference.** The RPG's character portraits are crops taken directly out of a reference art sheet the user supplied, run through a Pillow/JPEG pipeline (see **Game hub** above); no equivalent sheet exists for this brand-new four-character roster, and this session has no image-generation tool available to make one. Vector SVG is the one path to "real graphics, not emoji" that the session can actually produce itself, and it fits the phase-0 product decision (2D/2.5D stylized, no 3D pipeline) rather than fighting it — it also scales cleanly between the small selection card and the larger arena token with one file instead of needing two resolutions the way a raster crop would.

**Each of the four is built from one shared "chibi fighter" base shape (head circle, torso capsule, four limbs) recolored per character (`PALETY`, keyed by `PostavaId`), plus one signature accessory that's the only genuinely bespoke path per character** — a flame crown for Pyra, a shield-shaped helmet for Bulwark, a lightning-bolt hairline for Volt, a cape-plus-sword silhouette for Onyx — rather than four fully independent illustrations. This keeps the four visually consistent as one roster (same proportions, same line language) while still reading as distinct at a glance, and keeps the component small enough to stay a single file. A soft radial "aura" gradient behind each figure, colored per character, doubles as both a bit of atmosphere and a cheap way to keep a character legible against the arena backdrop behind it.

**`animovana` is a prop, not a property of the component's own state, because the same graphic is used in two places that need different motion — or none.** `VyberPostavy.tsx`'s selection cards pass `animovana` for a slow idle breathing bob (`souboj-postava-dychani`), giving the picker screen some life; `Bojiste.tsx`'s arena tokens deliberately don't, because there the *wrapper* `<div>` around the graphic already owns motion tied to `vizualniStavBojovnika` (idle/attack/hitstun/block/KO, see below) — animating both the SVG and its wrapper at once would mean two `transform`s fighting each other on the same visual element.

**The arena itself (`Bojiste.tsx`/`FightingModule.css`) moved from a flat card with two circular emoji badges to something that actually reads as a fighting stage, driven entirely by the `vizualniStavBojovnika` states `combat/loop.ts` already exposed — no new engine surface needed, this was purely a rendering-side pass.** A floor gradient (`.souboj-arena-podlaha`) plus an inset vignette on the arena container gives it depth instead of a flat glass panel; each fighter token dropped its circular badge shape (redundant now that there's a real humanoid silhouette to look at) in favor of a plain positioned box with a blurred contact-shadow ellipse under its feet, so the figure reads as standing on the floor rather than floating over it. Player 2's graphic is mirrored (`scaleX(-1)`) so the two fighters visually face each other, reusing the same SVG with no second asset.

**Every one of the five visual states gets its own distinct motion, all driven by CSS classes the component already computed per frame — `idle` bobs gently, `utok` lunges toward the opponent (a `--smer-vypadu` custom property flips sign per side so one keyframe serves both fighters instead of two near-duplicates), `hitstun` shakes and flashes a bright yellow `drop-shadow`, `blok` glows green with no movement (a blocking fighter isn't flung around), and `ko` plays a one-shot fall-and-fade (`animation-fill-mode: forwards`) that stays at its end pose because `krokSouboje` freezes and keeps returning the same state object once a round ends (see Fáze 1) — the class never gets removed to re-trigger it.** None of this needed a `prefers-reduced-motion` override of its own — the existing sitewide kill-switch in `global.css` (`*, *::before, *::after { animation-duration: 0.001ms !important }`) already neutralizes every one of these `@keyframes` for a reduced-motion viewer, the same free coverage this file already notes for the RPG boss's enrage glow.

**The one genuinely new piece of state Bojiste owns — a brief red flash around a fighter's HP bar the instant it drops — exists because a shrinking bar alone is easy to miss on a TV a few meters away, and it's deliberately the smallest possible addition, not a scope creep into "Bojiste has real state now."** A `useRef` holds the previous frame's HP per fighter, one `useEffect` compares it against the current props each render and, on a decrease, flips a `zasazen: [boolean, boolean]` flag for `350ms` via `setTimeout`. This stays honest to the component's own "no engine logic, no network, no `requestAnimationFrame`" boundary — it derives a purely cosmetic signal from what the props already say happened, the same class of thing `FeedPrispevek.tsx`'s double-tap-like burst or `ChatView.tsx`'s edited-message tag already are elsewhere in this codebase: a small local reaction to data the component receives, not a new source of truth.

**Verification split cleanly along a line this phase made newly visible: `VyberPostavy.tsx` is reachable by Playwright (it renders before any network call, same as every earlier Souboj phase's locally-testable surface), but `Bojiste.tsx` is not reachable at all — not even the "locally reachable subset" earlier phases relied on, because it only ever mounts inside `TvHost.tsx` after a live match starts, which needs the same WebSocket pairing this sandbox's proxy has never been able to make (see Fáze 0).** A temporary Playwright spec (deleted after passing, plus a screenshot checked by eye) confirmed the character-select screen: all four cards render real, non-zero-sized SVGs instead of emoji text, each reading distinctly (flame crown, shield helmet, lightning hair, cape-and-sword), and selecting one still enables "Pokračovat" exactly as before. For `Bojiste.tsx`, this phase used a genuinely different verification method for the first time in Souboj's history — a temporary Vitest + React Testing Library component test (`@testing-library/react` was already a project dependency, just unused until now — `tests/components/` itself stays "still-unbuilt" per this file, the test directory was created, exercised, and deleted, not left behind as a new permanent tier), mounting `Bojiste` directly with a fabricated `SoubojStav` built from the engine's own `vytvorSoubojStav()`: confirmed real `<svg>` elements replace the old emoji text nodes, confirmed the correct state class lands per fighter (hitstun/block/KO, checked by directly setting `zranitelnostKonci`/`blokuje`/`hp` on a fabricated state), confirmed the win banner still renders correctly, and — using fake timers plus `act()` — confirmed the HP-bar flash class appears the instant HP drops between two renders and clears again after its own timeout. `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` all clean (`FightingModule` still its own lazy chunk, 18 KB JS / 12.4 KB CSS, no regression in either), `auth.spec.ts`'s four cases unaffected (no routing touched). **A real rendered TV arena, mid-fight, on an actual second screen has still not been seen anywhere — this phase improved what it draws, not what it's finally confirmed to look like end-to-end, which remains the one thing only the user can verify on real devices.**

**A second follow-up pass, confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` (multiSelect: character graphics, arena visuals/animation, HP/mana bars — all three picked), went one layer deeper on the same three surfaces rather than opening new ones.** Nothing here changes the engine, the network layer, or `combat/loop.ts`'s public contract — every change is either inside `PostavaGrafika.tsx`/its CSS or inside `Bojiste.tsx`/`FightingModule.css`, the same "rendering-side pass, no new engine surface" restraint the first Fáze 6 pass already stated for the arena work.

**`PostavaGrafika.tsx`'s four fighters gained body shading, a consistent dark outline, and a small "boj postoj" (fighting stance) instead of a perfectly flat, symmetric figure — three cheap wins that read as a real jump in polish without needing a second art pipeline.** A shared `linearGradient` per character (top-lit `telo` fading to a new `teloTmave` palette field) replaces the flat torso/leg/arm fills, giving the same silhouette actual volume; a uniform `OBRYS` stroke (a single dark slate, not a per-character color — simpler, and dark enough to read against every palette) outlines the main body shapes the same way a hand-inked illustration would; the arms shifted to a slightly asymmetric guard (left raised, right lower) instead of a mirror-image T-pose, and small "glove" circles in the character's own `akcent` color sit at each hand — tying the figure back to its accessory color the same way the RPG's own portraits keep a consistent visual language per hero. A small white highlight ellipse on the head and two boot shapes at the foot of each leg round out the "actually shaded, not flat-colored" read. None of this touches the `animovana`/wrapper-animation boundary the first pass already established — the gradient/stroke/stance are static geometry, not new motion.

**`Bojiste.tsx`'s HP bar gained a "ghost" afterimage layer, and it's implemented with zero new JavaScript — a second bar element bound to the exact same `hpProcenta(b)` value as the existing front bar, just with a slower, delayed CSS `transition` (`0.6s ease-out 0.15s` vs. the front bar's `0.12s linear`).** Because both bars receive the identical width from props on every render, the browser's own transition interpolation does the "afterimage catches up after a hit" effect for free — the front bar snaps to the new, lower value instantly, and the darker ghost bar visibly lags behind for a beat before draining down to match. This is a deliberately different approach from the trailing-bar design considered (and set aside for a simpler flash) in the first Fáze 6 pass — that earlier version needed a `useRef`/`setTimeout` pair with real risk of stale-closure bugs (the same class of bug this file has flagged elsewhere, e.g. `TvHost.tsx`'s own dependency-array lesson); driving both bars off the same prop with different transition speeds needed none of that. The existing hit-flash (`souboj-hp-bar--zasazen`) and the new ghost bar are complementary, not redundant — the flash draws the eye to the *moment* of the hit, the ghost bar shows *how much* was just lost.

**The mana bar gained one honest, narrow signal — a pulsing glow when a fighter's mana is genuinely at its maximum (`b.mana >= b.maxMana`), not an approximation of "probably enough for a special."** Computing real special-readiness would mean threading `efektivniAkceData`/a specific character's special cost into `Bojiste.tsx`, a presentational component that currently knows nothing about `combat/engine.ts`'s cost tables — deliberately not done, since a full-bar pulse is both simpler and something the component can state as flatly true from data it already has, rather than an inference that could be wrong for a character whose special costs less than a full bar.

**The arena itself gained two small additions on top of the first pass's floor/vignette: a burst of six short "impact spark" lines at the hit fighter's position, and colored corner "stage light" glows matching each player's existing accent (red for player 1, cyan for player 2, the same colors `.souboj-hrac-panel--1`/`--2` already use on the TV's waiting screen).** The sparks reuse the exact same `zasazen` state the HP-bar flash already tracks — no second hit-detection mechanism, just a second visual reading the same boolean — and are positioned with six fixed rotation angles (`0deg` through `300deg` in steps of `60`) rather than CSS trigonometric functions, for the same "wider browser support, no extra runtime computation" reasoning as everywhere else motion is kept simple in this file. A faint vertical divider line down the arena's center is purely decorative (fighters cross it freely) — a small nod to "this is a stage with two sides," not a functional boundary.
