**Verified the same honest way as the first Fáze 6 pass, along the identical reachability line: `VyberPostavy.tsx`'s new gradients are Playwright-checkable (a temporary spec, deleted after passing, confirmed all four cards render a `<linearGradient>` inside their SVG, plus a screenshot checked by eye showing visible shading/gloves/stance on all four), and `Bojiste.tsx`'s new markup needed the same temporary Vitest + React Testing Library approach as before (component directory created, exercised, deleted — `tests/components/` stays "still-unbuilt" as a committed tier).** Three new component-test cases confirmed: both the ghost (`souboj-hp-bar-duch`) and front (`souboj-hp-bar-vypln`) HP layers render with the correct width from props; `souboj-mana-bar--plna` appears only on the fighter whose mana is genuinely at max, not the one just short of it; and the six impact-spark spans appear the instant HP drops between two renders and are gone again once the same flash window elapses (fake timers + `act()`, mirroring the first pass's HP-flash test). `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` all clean, `auth.spec.ts`'s four cases unaffected (no routing touched). **The same caveat as every earlier Souboj phase still holds: none of this has been seen on a real TV screen during an actual phone-to-TV match, only through fabricated props and a screenshot of the locally-reachable character-select screen.**

**A third pass, confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` (4 questions, all answered with the recommended option), replaced the flat 2D arena with a real 3D world and the controller's d-pad with a landscape joystick — a genuine reversal of Fáze 0's "2D/2.5D stylized, no 3D" product decision, not an incremental polish.** The four confirmed forks: a real 3D environment (grass/trees/water, same primitive-geometry technique as the RPG's `usePlayerWorld.ts`, not photorealism); a split screen with an independent camera per player (top = Hráč 1, bottom = Hráč 2), not just one shared scene visually divided; fighters staying flat SVG "billboard" sprites inside the 3D world rather than becoming 3D models (the project still has no 3D character pipeline); and the controller rotating to landscape with a joystick replacing the d-pad, action buttons unchanged.

**`useSoubojScene.ts` (`src/fighting/arena/`) is a new Three.js hook following the exact ownership shape `usePlayerWorld.ts`/`useAmbientScene.ts` already established — renderer/scene/cameras/loop owned entirely outside React, cleaned up on unmount, plain Three.js rather than React Three Fiber (a second, parallel way of working with a scene next to the codebase's already-established pattern would add nothing).** The split screen is deliberately one renderer and one scene, not two — two `THREE.PerspectiveCamera`s, two `renderer.render()` calls per frame into two halves of one canvas via `setViewport`/`setScissor`, the standard Three.js split-screen technique and the cheaper one (no duplicated renderer/geometry/texture memory), matching the explicit "ať je to rychlé" requirement. Both cameras always `lookAt()` the midpoint between the two fighters (never purely their own fighter) so neither view can lose the opponent off-screen, but each camera's own *position* is biased 55% toward its own fighter (`ZAOSTRENI_NA_VLASTNIHO`) and lerped toward that target every frame — a deliberately simpler "each player's own zoom/angle on the same duel" framing rather than a literal over-the-shoulder first-person camera, chosen because the latter risks the opponent clipping out of frame or scenery blocking the shot for a 1D fight lane, and the simpler version still genuinely satisfies "vlastní kamera pro každého" — the two views are visibly, not just nominally, different.

**Fighters are not 3D objects in the scene at all — the world only ever holds their numeric X position, and the DOM-rendered `PostavaGrafika` SVG (unchanged from the first two passes) gets positioned by projecting that 3D point onto each camera's screen space every frame.** This is the one place this pass genuinely expanded Bojiste's own architectural promise ("no hook, no `requestAnimationFrame`") — a real split-screen 3D scene fundamentally needs an owning render loop, which now lives in `useSoubojScene.ts`/`SoubojArena3D.tsx`, not in `Bojiste.tsx` itself (which still stays a thin orchestrator: header/HP-mana bars, and a choice between the 3D arena and its 2D fallback). Positioning and visual state deliberately never touch the same CSS property: the hook writes `left`/`top`/`opacity` directly onto an outer `.souboj-3d-sprite` wrapper div (imperative, every frame, via a stable cached ref-callback per camera/fighter pair — see below for why that caching mattered), while the *inner* `.souboj-bojovnik` div keeps exactly the same React-rendered, className-driven `transform`-based state animations (idle bob, attack lunge, hitstun shake, KO fall) the flat 2D arena already had, completely unmodified — the two update mechanisms never fight over the same property, so nothing about the existing state-animation system needed to change for 3D to work.

**`registrujSprite`'s ref-callback caching is load-bearing, not a micro-optimization — without it, the sprite refs would churn on every single frame.** `stav` changes ~60 times a second (driven by `TvHost.tsx`'s own game loop), so an inline `(el) => {...}` ref callback recreated on every render would make React call the *old* callback with `null` and the *new* one with the element on every re-render — the hook's own `useMemo`-cached function-per-key avoids that entirely, keeping the same stable reference across renders so React never has to unregister/re-register a sprite it already has.

**A fighter's screen position comes from `Vector3.project(camera)`, converted from NDC (-1..1) to a CSS percentage — but the Y conversion has to account for which HALF of the container that camera actually owns, since NDC is relative to the camera's own viewport, not the full split-screen canvas.** Camera 0's NDC y=1 (top of its view) maps to 0% of the *whole* container; camera 1's maps to 50%. A fighter projecting outside the camera's near/far depth range (`promitanyBod.z` outside -1..1) hides that sprite via opacity rather than placing it somewhere nonsensical — a defensive case that shouldn't occur in normal play given the lerped, midpoint-locked cameras, but costs nothing to guard against.

**Water/trees/rocks reuse the RPG's exact geometry recipe (`CylinderGeometry`+`ConeGeometry` trees, `DodecahedronGeometry` rocks) verbatim rather than inventing new primitives — the same "no downloaded assets, no third-party models" constraint `usePlayerWorld.ts` already states applies here too.** Decorations are scattered only in a band *behind* the fight lane (negative Z, away from both cameras' shared line of sight down the lane), never beside or in front of it, so nothing can visually block a fighter regardless of how the cameras pan — a deliberate simplification over the RPG's own `volnaZonaKolem` exclusion-radius math, justified because Souboj's "world" is a single straight lane, not an open field a player can walk into scenery from any angle.

**A working 2D fallback was kept, not just a bare error screen the way `Explorace3D.tsx`'s own `selhalo` case shows** — Souboj already had a fully-functional flat arena before this pass (the entire first two rounds of Fáze 6 polish), so a device where WebGL creation genuinely fails should still get a playable match, not a dead end. `SoubojArena2D.tsx` is that exact prior rendering, extracted verbatim into its own file (no behavior change); `SoubojArena3D.tsx` reports failure via an `onSelhalo` callback, and `Bojiste.tsx` — now a real switch, not a single renderer — flips to the 2D fallback the moment that fires. The shared "impact spark" markup (`Jiskry.tsx`) was pulled out into its own tiny component in the same pass specifically because both arenas now need it identically, rather than staying duplicated inline as it was when only one arena existed.

**The controller's landscape switch is a CSS-only guarantee, not a JS one — `screen.orientation.lock('landscape')` is attempted best-effort from `Ovladac.tsx` (wrapped in a local cast, since the DOM lib's `ScreenOrientation` type doesn't include the still-experimental `lock` method) but is expected to usually fail silently: it requires Fullscreen mode first in most browsers, which this app deliberately doesn't force, and doesn't exist on iOS Safari at all.** The real mechanism is `@media (orientation: portrait)` in `FightingModule.css`: it shows a "🔄 Otoč telefon na šířku" prompt and hides both the joystick and the action buttons, so a player physically holding the phone in portrait is never shown half-usable controls. This surfaced a genuine CSS cascade bug that a same-specificity-existence check alone wouldn't have caught: the `@media` block was originally written *before* `.souboj-tlacitka`'s own unconditional `display: grid` rule in the file — at equal specificity, the *later* source-order rule wins even while a matching `@media` condition holds, so the portrait override was silently losing to the base rule. Caught only by a stronger verification (below), not by confirming the rule merely existed; fixed by moving the whole `@media` block to after every base rule it overrides, the same "override has to out-rank the base rule in source order, not just exist" lesson CSS cascade bugs always teach.

**The joystick itself is `VirtualniJoystick.tsx`, reused byte-for-byte from the RPG's 3D exploration — no changes needed, because it already just reports a continuous x/z (-1..1) and lets the caller decide what that means.** Souboj's engine has no concept of analog movement (`combat/engine.ts`'s `tikBojovnika` only ever compares `vstup.smer` against the exact strings `'vlevo'`/`'vpravo'`), so the joystick's continuous X gets thresholded into the *same* discrete `Smer` type the old d-pad already sent, over the *same* `VstupPayload` shape — zero changes needed anywhere in `types.ts`, `network.ts`, or the engine itself, confirming the "engine doesn't know where input comes from" boundary this file has stated since Fáze 1 held up exactly as designed for a real hardware-input change, not just a network-source change. A `posledniSmerRef` dedupes outgoing broadcasts to only fire on an actual `Smer` change, not on every pixel of joystick movement (which fires far more often than the derived direction actually changes) — the same "don't spam the network for a value that didn't really change" discipline this codebase applies to chat typing indicators and elsewhere.

**Verification split three ways, matching what's actually reachable in this sandbox.** The 3D arena itself — genuinely unreachable through the live app (still gated behind the same WebSocket pairing wall documented since Fáze 0) — was verified visually the same way Fáze 0's own diagnostic worked around that wall: a temporary, fully-reverted debug branch in `FightingModule.tsx` (`?debug3d=1` query param rendering `<Bojiste>` directly with a fabricated `SoubojStav`, deleted immediately after — confirmed via `git checkout` that nothing debug-shaped survived) let a real Playwright spec (also deleted after passing) confirm the canvas actually renders at a real size with four correctly-positioned sprites, and a screenshot checked by eye showed a genuinely convincing split-screen duel — grass, a dirt path, trees and a water strip in the backdrop, both fighters facing each other, distinct per-half framing. This also incidentally confirmed something new: Chromium in this sandbox *can* render real WebGL content (unlike the still-true "no camera/microphone hardware" limitation) — the wall here has only ever been the phone↔TV signaling channel, never the rendering itself. The 3D→2D fallback path was verified differently and more directly than anything in this app before: jsdom has no real WebGL context at all (`HTMLCanvasElement.getContext` genuinely returns nothing without the optional `canvas` npm package, which this project doesn't install), so a temporary Vitest + React Testing Library test mounting `<Bojiste>` exercises the *real* failure path — not a mock of it — and confirms `Bojiste` correctly lands on `SoubojArena2D` with both fighters still rendered. The landscape/portrait CSS was verified two ways: a temporary spec confirming the real, compiled stylesheet contains the expected `@media` rule (which is what first caught the cascade-order bug above), and a second spec injecting that same compiled CSS against bare divs with the right class names at both a landscape-shaped and portrait-shaped viewport — `orientation` is a purely dimension-derived media feature (unlike `pointer`, which CDP in this sandbox has already been documented as unable to emulate for Fáze 5's arrow buttons), so this check is fully trustworthy, not a best-effort approximation. `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` all clean (`three.module`'s shared chunk unchanged in size — confirms `useSoubojScene.ts` correctly reuses the existing dependency graph rather than duplicating it — `FightingModule` grew to ~24 KB JS / ~15 KB CSS for its own chunk), `auth.spec.ts`'s four cases unaffected. **What still hasn't been seen anywhere: a real second device actually pairing over the network and playing a live match through this new 3D, split-screen, landscape-controlled version — that gap is unchanged from every earlier Souboj phase, and only the user can close it.**

**A third follow-up pass, asked as an open "how else could this be improved" and answered with a multiSelect `AskUserQuestion` picking all four offered directions at once, closed the gap a codebase-wide grep had surfaced: Souboj had zero gamification integration and zero sound/haptic feedback, unlike literally every other game and miniapp in this file.** Unique specials, best-of-N scoring, and sound/vibration are still their own separate passes (this section covers unique specials and match XP/rewards only); the four-way answer set the order, not a single combined implementation.

**Unique special abilities replace what was, until now, every character's `specialni` action doing the exact same thing — a bigger hit — with `poskozeniNasobic`/`specialniSila` as the only lever telling them apart.** `TypSpecialu` (`combat/postavy.ts`) is a small closed set — `'poskozeni' | 'stit' | 'dvojity-zasah' | 'vysati'` — one archetype per character, the same "four different archetypes, not four flavours of one stat" restraint this file already states for the RPG's own relics: Pyra keeps the straightforward `'poskozeni'` (a flat `specialniSila` multiplier, `1.5×`, applied only to the `specialni` action in `efektivniAkceData`, never to `udar`/`kop`), Bulwark gets `'stit'` (the next incoming unblocked hit is fully absorbed, dealing no damage and no hitstun), Volt gets `'dvojity-zasah'` (the special resolves as two separate hits, each independently subject to block/shield), and Onyx gets `'vysati'` (heals for `specialniSila` — `0.5` — of whatever damage the special actually deals, capped at `maxHp`).

**`BojovnikStav` gained one field, `stitAktivni: boolean`, and it is deliberately consumed by an actual hit, not by a timer** — unlike `zranitelnostKonci`/`utokKonci`, which count down every tick regardless of what else happens, a shield has to survive its owner's own subsequent actions (moving, throwing another card) and only go away the moment it actually does its job. `tikBojovnika` sets it `true` the instant a `'stit'`-type special action begins (threaded through the same mutable `let` pattern the rest of the function already uses for per-tick state); the consumption happens inside a new `aplikujJedenZasah` helper extracted from what used to be the single, inline hit-resolution block in `vyhodnotZasahPokudZahajen` — it checks `cil.stitAktivni` first, and if true, fully absorbs the hit (zero damage, zero hitstun, shield cleared) before any of the normal block/damage/mana math runs at all.

**`aplikujJedenZasah` exists specifically so Volt's double-hit doesn't need a second, copy-pasted resolution path — it's called once for an ordinary hit, and called *twice in sequence* for `'dvojity-zasah'`, with the defender's state correctly threaded from the first call's result into the second's input.** This is what makes a Bulwark shield block *only* the first of Volt's two hits, not both — the exact behavior a real player would expect, verified directly rather than assumed. `vyhodnotZasahPokudZahajen` computes the base damage once via `efektivniAkceData`, calls the helper the appropriate number of times, sums the actual damage dealt across both calls (post-block, post-shield — not the theoretical base number), and only then applies `'vysati'`'s healing to whichever attacker has that effect, off the real total.

**Verified by `tests/unit/fighting-specialy.test.ts` (10 new cases, 117 total in the project) rather than anything requiring a browser or a live match — the engine is still the same pure, deterministic, no-`Math.random()` system Fáze 1 established, so every one of these is a direct `krokSouboje`/`efektivniAkceData` assertion.** Pyra's damage bonus is checked both directly (comparing `efektivniAkceData` output for `specialni` vs. `udar`/`kop`) and through a full simulated exchange; Bulwark's shield is confirmed granted regardless of range (a `'stit'` cast doesn't need to connect with anything to activate), confirmed to fully absorb the very next hit, and confirmed consumed (a second unblocked hit afterward lands normally). **A real test-authoring bug was caught and fixed while writing the Onyx-lifesteal-vs-Bulwark case** — it first computed "damage dealt" as a hardcoded `100` minus the defender's post-hit HP, silently wrong the moment the defender is Bulwark (`maxHpNasobic: 1.25`, so a real max HP of 125, not 100) — caught by the assertion itself failing (48.8 vs. an expected 36.3), fixed by reading `stav.hraci[1].maxHp` directly before the attack instead of assuming the flat constant, the exact same class of mistake CLAUDE.md already documents catching once before on the RPG's own Fáze 2 character tests.

**Bulwark's shield needed a visible sign on the TV, or a defender's "why did that hit deal zero damage" would read as a bug, not a mechanic — a small pulsing 🛡️ badge (`.souboj-stit-znacka`) renders on whichever fighter currently has `stitAktivni: true`, in both `SoubojArena2D.tsx` and `SoubojArena3D.tsx`.** It's positioned as a plain absolutely-positioned sibling inside `.souboj-bojovnik` (already `position: absolute` for its own arena placement, so the badge needed no new positioning context) — the same "engine state drives a CSS class, the component itself computes nothing" boundary `vizualniStavBojovnika` already established for idle/attack/hitstun/block/KO.

**XP and credits for playing a match are awarded per-phone, not from the TV — a direct consequence of `useGamificationStore`/`useWalletStore` being `secureStorage`-backed, per-device state with no cross-device sync for this game.** The TV cannot credit anyone (it has no player's own browser storage to write into), so `TvHost.tsx` instead broadcasts *only the outcome* — a new `KonecZapasuPayload` (`{ vitezSlot: 1 | 2 | null }`) sent once via a new `oznamKonecZapasu` call on `network.ts`'s existing `hostujMistnost` — the moment its own tick loop observes the round transition from `'probiha'` to `'konec'` (captured via a `stavPredTikem` comparison so the broadcast fires exactly once, not on every subsequent tick the frozen end-state is re-read). Each connected phone's own `Ovladac.tsx` receives that broadcast (a new `konecZapasu` handler on `pripojSeJakoOvladac`) and decides for itself whether it was the winner, by comparing `vitezSlot` against its *own* `slot` — never trusting the TV to know or care which specific device that slot belongs to.

**The `slot` read inside that handler comes from a ref, not the `slot` state variable, because the handler closure is created once, at connection time, while `slot` itself only actually becomes non-null asynchronously afterward, when the TV's own `pripojeno` acknowledgment arrives.** `slotRef.current` is set in the same `pripojeno` callback that already calls `setSlot(p.slot)` — the same "ref for what a long-lived closure needs to read fresh, state for what triggers a re-render" split this codebase already applies elsewhere (`hracIdRef`, `posledniSmerRef` in this same file).

**A win goes through `recordAction('souboj', XP_VYHRA)` (25 XP) plus `useWalletStore().credit(KREDITY_VYHRA)` (15 credits); a loss or a draw goes through bare `addXp(XP_UCAST)` (8 XP) only — deliberately not `recordAction`, so a loss/draw never bumps the `souboj` counter `ring_mistr` is keyed on.** `ring_mistr` ("Vyhraj 5 zápasů v Souboji") is a new `DEFAULT_BADGES`/`COUNT_BADGES` entry using a new `ActivityKind`, `'souboj'` — deliberately its own kind, not a reuse of the RPG's existing `'battle'`, the same "two different games, two different counters" split this file's own comment on `COUNT_BADGES` already draws for Pomodoro's `completedSessions` vs. `counters.pomodoro`. A draw counts as a loss for crediting purposes (participation XP only) — there's no draw-specific reward tier, since nothing about a draw represents more accomplishment than a clean loss.

**The phone shows a transient result banner (`.souboj-ovladac-vysledek`) naming what it just earned — "Vyhrál jsi! +25 XP, +15 kreditů" / "Prohrál jsi — +8 XP" / "Remíza — +8 XP" — self-clearing after 4 seconds via a plain `window.setTimeout`, the same "the component owns *how it looks*, not *when it disappears* beyond that one timer" shape Ovladac's own result-banner logic keeps intentionally simple**, matching how little state this screen otherwise carries (no persisted history of past matches, no "results" screen — a single line, then gone).

**Verified by a temporary Vitest + React Testing Library test (deleted after passing, following the exact precedent Fáze 6's `Bojiste.tsx` component test already set for the same underlying sandbox limitation — a live phone↔TV WebSocket round-trip is unreachable here, so the network layer was mocked and the handler exercised directly instead of through a real broadcast).** `pripojSeJakoOvladac` was mocked to capture the `pripojeno`/`konecZapasu` handlers `Ovladac.tsx` registers, then called directly with fabricated payloads: a win (own slot matches `vitezSlot`) correctly calls `recordAction('souboj', 25)` and `credit(15)` and shows the win banner; a loss (own slot doesn't match) calls only `addXp(8)`, never `recordAction`/`credit`, and shows the loss banner; a draw (`vitezSlot: null`) calls only `addXp(8)` and shows the draw banner. A separate pair of cases confirmed the shield badge renders for exactly the fighter with `stitAktivni: true` and renders nothing when neither fighter has it. **One test-environment-only wrinkle surfaced and was fixed in the test, not the app**: Testing Library's own `screen` export (a DOM query object) collided by name with `window.screen` — the app's best-effort `screen.orientation.lock()` call (see the landscape section above) reads the real global, which jsdom exposes with no usable `orientation` property at all, so the test had to explicitly stub `window.screen.orientation` before rendering, an environment gap unrelated to any app code. `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` all clean (117 unit tests, `FightingModule` chunk size essentially unchanged), `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**Best-of-N match scoring is the third of the four items, and it lives entirely in `TvHost.tsx` — no engine change — matching the engine's own documented boundary that a multi-round match is "věc obrazovky (Fáze 3), engine sám umí simulovat jen jedno kolo od začátku do KO."** `VITEZSTVI_NA_ZAPAS = 2` ("first to 2 wins," i.e. best of 3) is a plain constant; `skore: [number, number]` is new `TvHost` state (plus a `skoreRef` mirror for the same reason `soubojStavRef`/`hraciRef` already exist — the `requestAnimationFrame` loop needs to read the freshest score synchronously within one tick, not a value frozen in the effect's own closure).

**A round win (`stavKola` transitions `'probiha'` → `'konec'` with a non-null `vitez`) increments the winning slot's score; a drawn round (`vitez === null`, both fighters hit zero HP in the same tick) increments nobody's — the match simply continues, since a draw represents no accomplishment either way to bank.** The whole-match outcome — and with it, the `oznamKonecZapasu` broadcast that drives each phone's XP/credits (see above) — now fires only once `skoreRef.current` shows either slot reaching `VITEZSTVI_NA_ZAPAS`, not on every individual round's end as it did before this pass. This is a real, deliberate behavior change from the XP/rewards feature as first shipped: crediting a win the instant the *first* round ended would have paid out a whole match's reward for a match that was still ongoing. The broadcast's `vitezSlot` is computed from the final score comparison, not from `vitez` on the deciding round — with `VITEZSTVI_NA_ZAPAS = 2` a tied final score is structurally impossible (only one slot's count can ever reach 2 first), but the comparison stays defensive rather than assuming that invariant silently forever.

**Two reset functions share one helper (`zacniKolo`) rather than duplicating the "build a fresh `SoubojStav` and reset the per-round refs" logic — they differ only in whether `skore` also resets.** `dalsiKolo()` (round ended, match not yet decided — "Další kolo (`skore[0]` : `skore[1]`)" button) calls it alone; `novyZapas()` (match decided, or the player wants to restart) zeroes `skoreRef.current`/`skore` first. Which button renders is decided by `zapasSkoncil` (`skore[0] >= VITEZSTVI_NA_ZAPAS || skore[1] >= VITEZSTVI_NA_ZAPAS`), computed directly in the render body from the `skore` state — no separate "is the match over" state to keep in sync with the score that already answers the question. A running score bar (`.souboj-skore-pruh`, player names either side of a centered "N : M") sits above `<Bojiste>` for the whole time a round is in progress, not only between rounds, so the running tally is always visible, not just revealed at the end.

**Verified by a temporary Vitest + React Testing Library test (deleted after passing) — the same underlying constraint as every other Souboj phase (no live phone↔TV socket reachable in this sandbox) resolved the same way as the XP/rewards test above, plus one more mock this test specifically needed: `combat/engine`'s own `krokSouboje` export, replaced with a stateful call-counter (`vi.mock` with `importOriginal`, keeping every other real export — `vytvorSoubojStav`, `ARENA_SIRKA` — untouched) that returns `'probiha'` for a fixed number of ticks, then a `'konec'` with a test-controlled `vitez`, then resets its own counter — letting the *real*, unmocked `requestAnimationFrame` polyfill drive the actual `TvHost` game loop at its own pace while the test just `waitFor`s the resulting DOM text, rather than needing to intercept and manually pump RAF frames.** Player 1 connects for real (the mocked `hostujMistnost`'s captured `pripojilSe` handler, called directly); player 2 is the existing solo-mode AI opponent (`🤖 Hrát proti počítač`í), the fastest path to `pripraveno` without also needing to simulate a second controller's character pick. The test confirmed: after round 1's win, the score bar reads "1 : 0," `oznamKonecZapasu` has **not** been called yet, and "Další kolo" (not "Nový zápas") is the only button shown; after clicking through to and winning round 2 (same winner, now at 2), `oznamKonecZapasu` fires **exactly once**, with `{ vitezSlot: 1 }` — confirming the broadcast genuinely waits for the whole match, not the first round — and "Nový zápas" replaces "Další kolo." A second case confirmed clicking "Nový zápas" resets the visible score back to "0 : 0." `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` all clean (119 tests while the temporary spec existed, back to 117 after deletion — confirming it left nothing behind), `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**Sound effects and phone vibration are the fourth and final item, and they deliberately split across the two physical devices by which one actually has the matching hardware — the TV has speakers, the phone has a vibration motor — rather than both features living on both screens.** `sound.ts` (TV) and `haptika.ts` (phone) are two small, independent modules for exactly that reason: nothing about them shares code, because nothing about "play a tone through TV speakers" and "buzz a phone's haptic motor" actually overlaps beyond both being a reaction to the same game events.

**`sound.ts` follows Pomodoro's own already-proven idiom (`usePomodoro.ts`'s `playChime`) verbatim — Web Audio, short synthesized oscillator tones, no sound file at all**, so nothing new enters the PWA precache or needs downloading offline. `odemkniZvuk()` creates/resumes the single shared `AudioContext`, and it has to be called from inside a real user gesture or the browser silently refuses it — the same constraint Pomodoro's own `ensureAudio`/`resume()` call already states. The gesture Souboj actually has is `FightingModule.tsx`'s "Hostovat na TV" button click, the furthest-downstream point that still knows a TV session is about to start — `TvHost.tsx` itself has no "start the match" button of its own (a match begins automatically the instant both players connect), so there is no later gesture to hook into. Once unlocked there, every later sound call — including from inside `TvHost`'s own `requestAnimationFrame` loop, which has no gesture of its own — plays normally; browsers only gate the *first* unlock of a context, not each individual sound.

**Where each sound triggers reuses `Bojiste.tsx`'s existing "purely a reaction to prop changes" role rather than adding new logic to the game loop itself** — the same boundary this file already states for the HP-flash/spark effect ("still not game logic, just a visual reaction to what the number in props just did"), extended to audio. A hit-landed tone (`zahrajZasah`) reuses the *exact same* `zasazen` HP-decrease derivation that already drives the spark/flash effect, in the same `useEffect` — no separate detection needed, since "did HP just go down" is already computed there. A special-cast whoosh (`zahrajSpecial`) is new detection, though: `BojovnikStav.posledniAkce` is deliberately "sticky" (engine.ts: `zahajenaAkce ?? b.posledniAkce`, never reset to `null`), so naively checking "is `posledniAkce === 'specialni'`" every frame would replay the whoosh on every single tick a character's special stays their most recent action, not just the tick it was cast — a new `predchoziAkce` ref, compared the same way `predchoziHp` already is, catches only the genuine transition *into* `'specialni'`, firing once per cast (once for Bulwark's shield going up even though it may never connect with anything, once each for Volt's two chained hits sharing the one cast that started them, since they're one action, not two).

**A round/match-ending fanfare deliberately does not distinguish "player 1 won" from "player 2 won" — `Bojiste.tsx` is a shared TV screen with no individual stake in either slot, unlike a phone which genuinely has "my own outcome."** `zahrajVyhra()` plays for any decisive round end regardless of which slot won; `zahrajRemizu()` plays for a draw; there is no `zahrajProhra` — a "loss" isn't a concept the room as a whole experiences, only an individual player does, and that's exactly what the phone's own vibration (below) is for instead. A `konecOznamen` ref (mirroring `TvHost.tsx`'s own `stavPredTikem`-transition discipline for `oznamKonecZapasu`) makes sure the fanfare plays exactly once per round end, not on every subsequent render of the same frozen `stavKola: 'konec'` state, and resets the moment a new round actually starts (`stavKola` back to `'probiha'`).

**`haptika.ts` follows the same "feature-detect, degrade silently" shape this codebase already applies to `BarcodeDetector`/`MediaRecorder`/WebAuthn — the Vibration API has zero support on iOS Safari, so nothing here treats it as anything but a bonus.** `PODPORUJE_VIBRACE` is checked once at module load; every exported function is a thin, try/caught wrapper around `navigator.vibrate()` that quietly no-ops if the API is missing or throws. Two triggers: `zavibrujTlacitko()` (a short 15ms pulse) fires from `posliTlacitko` on every button *press* specifically, not release — the only tactile confirmation this physical-feeling controller has that a touch actually registered, since the phone otherwise shows no visual press-state of its own. The other three (`zavibrujVyhru`/`zavibrujProhru`/`zavibrujRemizu`, each a distinct pulse pattern) fire from the exact same `konecZapasu` handler that already decides win/loss/draw for XP crediting (see above) — reusing that same three-way branch rather than a second one, so the vibration and the XP/credit outcome can never disagree about what just happened.

**Verified by a temporary Vitest + React Testing Library test (deleted after passing), split the same way the modules themselves are — `Bojiste.tsx`'s sound triggers tested via mocked `@/fighting/sound` and controlled `rerender()` calls simulating successive game ticks, `Ovladac.tsx`'s vibration triggers tested via mocked `@/fighting/haptika` plus the same mocked-network-handler-capture approach the XP/rewards test above already established.** Confirmed: a genuine HP decrease between two renders plays the hit sound exactly once; `posledniAkce` transitioning into `'specialni'` plays the whoosh once, and staying at `'specialni'` on a further render does not replay it; a round ending with a winner plays the win fanfare exactly once, not again on a subsequent render with the same frozen state; a draw plays the draw tone, never the win fanfare; a real button press fires the button-vibration exactly once, and releasing it fires nothing more; a win outcome fires only `zavibrujVyhru`, never the loss/draw variants. **Two more test-environment-only gaps surfaced and were fixed in the test, not the app, joining the `window.screen.orientation` stub the XP/rewards test already needed**: jsdom has no Pointer Capture API at all (`setPointerCapture`/`releasePointerCapture` are simply missing), which the action buttons already call on `pointerdown` — the test polyfills both as no-ops before rendering. `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` all clean (123 tests while the temporary spec existed, back to 117 after deletion), `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**This closes all four items from the multiSelect answer that started this pass of Souboj improvements** — unique special abilities, XP/rewards for playing matches, best-of-N match scoring, and sound effects + phone vibration — each verified the same way every Souboj phase before it has been: real unit/component tests where the logic is reachable, temporary specs deleted after passing, and an honest acknowledgment of what still can't be verified in this sandbox (a real two-device match, unchanged since Fáze 0).

**A follow-up "how else could this be improved" pass, answered via `AskUserQuestion` (multiSelect, picking three of four offered directions — different arenas/scenes, match statistics, a round timer; a single-device local mode was offered but not picked), closed a real design gap alongside two content/polish additions.**

**A round timer (`CAS_LIMIT_MS = 60000`, `combat/engine.ts`) closes a genuine hole the pure engine had carried since Fáze 1: nothing stopped a round from running forever if both fighters just circled and blocked, since a round only ever ended on an actual KO.** `krokSouboje` now checks the elapsed `stav.cas` after the existing KO check (KO still wins if both happen the same tick — verified directly, not assumed) and, once the limit is reached with nobody KO'd, decides the round by whoever has more HP, a draw on an exact tie — the same two-value "vítěz-nebo-remíza" shape the KO branch right above it already uses, just fed from HP instead of "who's at zero." This stays inside Fáze 1's own deterministic, no-`Math.random()` contract — nothing about a fixed time limit needed randomness. `combat/loop.ts` gained `zbyvaSekund(stav)`, a pure derivation from `stav.cas`/`CAS_LIMIT_MS` (clamped so it can never read negative, even a tick or two after the real expiry) that `Bojiste.tsx` renders as a small pill above the arena — plain, most of the round, then a red pulsing state once `zbyvaSekund(stav) <= 10`, the same "only draw the eye once it actually matters" restraint the mana bar's own full-mana pulse already established. Four new `tests/unit/fighting-combat.test.ts` cases (124 unit tests in the project now, up from 117) cover: a round still running before the limit; HP deciding the winner at the limit; an exact-HP tie reading as a draw; and a genuine KO landing on the exact same tick the limit is reached still taking priority over the HP comparison. Three more `tests/unit/fighting-loop.test.ts` cases cover `zbyvaSekund` itself (full limit at `cas: 0`, a mid-round value, and the post-expiry floor at zero).

**Arena/scene variety (`src/fighting/arena/areny.ts`) is deliberately a pure-data addition on top of `useSoubojScene.ts`, not a second 3D pipeline — three `Arena` configs (Zelená pláň, the original louka palette moved here unchanged; Pouštní kaňon, a warm sandy palette with rocks and no water; Půlnoční aréna, a near-black palette with no decorations at all, deliberately not "the same forest with the lights off") replace what used to be hardcoded sky/fog/ground/water/decoration colors inside the hook.** `useSoubojScene`'s own decoration loop already distinguished trees from rocks by a coin flip; it now reads `arena.dekorace` (`'les' | 'kameny' | 'zadne'`) to decide whether that coin flip even happens, and pulls every color (sky, fog, ground, path, water — nullable, since a desert/night arena has none — tree trunk/crown, rock) from the selected `Arena` instead of a literal hex per mesh.

**Which arena a match uses is decided entirely on the TV, before the match starts, and deliberately never touches the network at all — a real simplification this session almost didn't take.** The obvious-looking alternative (broadcast the founder's pick alongside `postavaId` in `PripojitPayload`, the way character selection already works) would have needed a real answer to "what if the second phone tries to pick a different arena," a problem `postavaId` never has since each phone only ever picks its own character. Since the 3D scene is purely a TV-rendered visual with no effect on `SoubojStav`/the engine at all, the far simpler fix was to make it purely TV-local state (`arenaId`, `useState<ArenaId>`) selected from a small pill row on the same pre-match waiting screen the "🤖 Hrát proti počítači" button already lives on, threaded straight down through `Bojiste`'s new `arenaId` prop to `SoubojArena3D` — zero changes to `types.ts`, `network.ts`, or the engine, the same "engine doesn't know where input comes from, and visuals don't need to know where the network is" boundary this session keeps re-deriving for genuinely different reasons each time.

**Match statistics (`useSoubojStatistikyStore.ts`) are per-device, per-character win/loss/draw counters — the same `secureStorage`-backed, per-device architecture as XP/credits, and for the identical reason: gamification-shaped state here has no server, no cross-device sync, only whichever phone the account happens to be playing from.** `zaznamenejVysledek(postavaId, 'vyhra' | 'prohra' | 'remiza')` is called from the exact same three-way branch inside `Ovladac.tsx`'s `konecZapasu` handler that already decides XP/credits/vibration — one shared decision point, not a fourth independent branch that could someday disagree with the other three about what just happened. Validated the same item-by-item way as every other persisted store this session has added (`core/utils/soubojStatistikyValidation.ts`, valid character ids derived straight from `POSTAVY` rather than a second hardcoded list, an unknown id or a corrupt count silently dropped/zeroed rather than failing the whole store) and registered in `BACKUP_STORES` — the standing rule for any new persisted key, restated once more here: miss it and a user's Souboj record silently drops out of every backup they ever make.

**A small "📊 Statistiky" screen, reachable from the room-code entry screen (the one place every controller session starts, whether connecting fresh or having just left a match), lists all four characters with their record — or "Zatím žádný zápas" for one never played.** It's a plain local boolean (`zobrazStatistiky`), not a fifth `StavSpojeni` step, since it never participates in the connection flow at all — the same reasoning that already kept the XP/rewards result banner a simple piece of local state rather than a network-driven one.

**Verified by a temporary Vitest + React Testing Library test (deleted after passing), split along the same lines the features themselves are — `Bojiste.tsx`'s countdown checked via `rerender()` with different `stav.cas` values (confirms the seconds shown and the `--dochazi` class threshold, and that the pill disappears once `stavKola` is `'konec'`); `TvHost.tsx`'s arena picker checked by clicking between options and confirming `is-vybrana` moves with the click; `Ovladac.tsx`'s statistics screen checked both empty (four "Zatím žádný zápas" rows) and after a real win recorded through the same mocked `konecZapasu` handler-capture approach this session's earlier XP/rewards and sound/vibration tests already established, confirming the store itself ends up holding `{ vyhry: 1, prohry: 0, remizy: 0 }` for the character actually played.** `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` all clean (129 tests while the temporary spec existed, back to 124 after deletion — 117 before this pass, confirming exactly 7 new permanent unit cases and nothing else left behind), `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched), `FightingModule`'s own lazy chunk grew to ~32 KB JS (arena data, the statistics store/screen) with no change to the eager main bundle's size worth noting.

**A fifth pass — asked as an open "make the joystick+TV game as good as it can be," narrowed via `AskUserQuestion` to "visual juice (hit-stop/screen shake/KO zoom)" and "deeper combat (combo chains + knockback)," both picked at once — added real weight to the fight itself, on top of what was until now a flat, un-punctuated exchange of fixed-damage hits.** Neither piece touches the network layer or `types.ts`'s wire shapes at all — both live entirely inside the already-established "pure engine, TV decides what a tick looks like, Bojiste only reacts to props" boundary this file has restated at every earlier Souboj phase, extended here rather than bent.

**Combo is a genuinely new piece of `BojovnikStav` — `komboPocet`/`komboKonci` — built by consecutive unblocked landed hits and decaying on a real window, not a session-long counter.** `KOMBO_OKNO_MS = 1200` is refreshed to full on every additional landed hit and ticks down unconditionally inside `tikBojovnika` every frame — including while the attacker is busy recovering from their own last swing or the defender is in hitstun, the same "the clock doesn't care what else is happening" discipline `zranitelnostKonci`/`utokKonci` already established — so a combo genuinely lapses if the follow-up hit doesn't land inside that window, not merely if the player stops attacking. `KOMBO_BONUS_ZA_UDER = 0.08` (8% extra damage per stacked hit) and `KOMBO_MAX_STUPNU = 5` (a hard cap at +40%) keep it a real but bounded incentive to actually land consecutive hits rather than a runaway snowball; a blocked hit neither extends nor benefits from a combo — it's specifically a reward for landing clean, matching how a shield/block already exists to neutralize an attacker's own advantage. The bonus is computed from the attacker's own combo state *before* that hit resolves, inside `aplikujJedenZasah` (not the calling `vyhodnotZasahPokudZahajen`) — the same place knockback is computed, and for the identical reason: Volt's `dvojity-zasah` calls this helper twice in one tick, and both the combo bonus and the knockback distance the *second* hit sees have to reflect what the *first* hit of that same special just did, which only holds if the computation lives inside the per-hit helper, not the once-per-action caller. `combat/loop.ts` gained `komboAktivni(b)`, the same "read a finished field, no side effects" shape as `hpProcenta`/`zbyvaSekund` — `0` once `komboKonci` has actually lapsed, even if `komboPocet` is still sitting at a stale nonzero value, so `Bojiste.tsx`'s new 🔥×N badge (shown only from combo count ≥2, next to the attacking fighter's name) can never show a promlčené combo just because nobody bothered to reset the count field itself.

**Knockback is a new `AkceData.knockback` field (udar 18, kop 32, specialni 55 — roughly proportional to each action's own damage, not a separately-tuned number), applied as horizontal position displacement inside the same `aplikujJedenZasah` helper, clamped to `[0, ARENA_SIRKA]` so a fighter can be pushed to the wall but never off the playable lane.** Direction is derived from the two fighters' relative position at the moment of the hit (attacker pushes the defender further away, never toward themselves) — a blocked hit still knocks back, just reduced by the same `BLOK_REDUKCE` fraction the damage reduction already uses, rather than being nullified outright, since a real block softens a hit's momentum without stopping it dead. Deliberately **not** scaled by any character multiplier the way damage/reach/mana-cost already are — knockback stays a property of the *action*, the same for every character who throws it, specifically to avoid quietly opening a fifth balance axis nobody asked to tune when only "combo chains and knockback" was confirmed.

**Hit-stop is TV-side only, living in `TvHost.tsx`'s own tick loop, deliberately not an engine change at all — the same boundary this file draws between "what a fight *is*" (the pure, deterministic `combat/engine.ts`) and "how a TV chooses to show it."** `HIT_STOP_MS = 90` is a brief, genuine simulation freeze: while `hitStopMsRef.current` is nonzero, the RAF loop's own `tik()` skips calling `krokSouboje` entirely for that frame (not merely skipping the re-render — the fight itself doesn't advance), decrementing the ref instead. It's armed by comparing each fighter's HP immediately before and after a real `krokSouboje` call; if either dropped, the freeze engages for the next `HIT_STOP_MS` of wall-clock time before ticks resume — the classic "a landed hit gets a beat of stillness before the fight continues" feel real fighting games use, built with zero new engine state because it only ever needs to compare two already-available HP numbers frame-to-frame.

**Screen shake and KO zoom are both pure CSS-class toggles computed in `Bojiste.tsx` from data it already tracked, not new state driving new logic — a fresh `otres` boolean set in the exact same effect and with the exact same timer as the pre-existing `zasazen` HP-flash (they're two presentational reactions to the identical "HP just dropped" signal, one on the HP bar, one on the whole arena), and a zoom class derived directly from `stav.stavKola === 'konec' && stav.vitez !== null` — deliberately excluding a draw, since nothing about a draw is a triumphant moment worth zooming into.** Both wrap the existing arena render (3D or its 2D fallback, whichever `Bojiste` is currently using) in one new `.souboj-arena-obal` div rather than touching either arena component — `souboj-arena-obal--otres` drives a short `@keyframes` horizontal jitter, `souboj-arena-obal--zoom` a plain CSS `transition` scaling the whole arena up slightly once a round is won. Both are automatically neutralized for a `prefers-reduced-motion` viewer by the same sitewide `*, *::before, *::after { animation-duration: 0.001ms !important; transition-duration: 0.001ms !important; }` kill-switch this file already documents covering every other looping/one-shot animation in the app — confirmed by reading that rule again, not assumed to still apply.

**Verified by a temporary Vitest + React Testing Library test (deleted after passing) plus 8 new permanent unit tests in `tests/unit/fighting-combat.test.ts` (132 unit tests in the project now, up from 124) — the pure engine mechanics (knockback direction/clamping/block-reduction, combo count/bonus/decay/cap) go through the same "no browser needed" tier every other `combat/engine.ts` addition has used since Fáze 1, while hit-stop/shake/zoom needed the component tier since they only exist in `TvHost.tsx`/`Bojiste.tsx`.** The component test confirmed: the combo badge shows only from count ≥2 and stays hidden once `komboKonci` reaches 0 even with a stale nonzero `komboPocet`; the arena shakes the instant HP drops between two renders; the KO zoom class appears on a real win and stays absent on a draw; and — the one case needing an actual running `TvHost`, verified with a mocked `krokSouboje` call-counter standing in for the real engine — that hit-stop genuinely arms without permanently freezing a match, which still goes on to reach "Další kolo" normally. **A real, previously-passing test needed a genuine fix, not a weakened assertion, once combo shipped**: Volt's `dvojity-zasah` special test in `fighting-specialy.test.ts` expected exactly double a single hit's damage, but the second of the two chained hits now legitimately lands with an +8% combo bonus from the first hit landing in the same tick — the expected math was corrected to `jedenZasah * (2 + KOMBO_BONUS_ZA_UDER)` and confirmed against the actual observed numbers, not adjusted until the test merely passed. `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` all clean (137 tests while the temporary spec existed, back to 132 after deletion), `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched), `FightingModule`'s own lazy chunk grew only marginally (new CSS rules, a few new fields threaded through existing props) — no new dependency, no new network surface, no engine determinism lost.

**A sixth pass — asked as an open "what else could this game use," answered via `AskUserQuestion` (multiSelect) picking KO finishers + an intro screen, parry/comeback, and a local single-device mode, plus a free-text request to "graphically polish it to the best level and optimize it to run smoothly" — landed all four together, since none of them touched the same code and building them in one pass was cheaper than four separate round trips.**

**Parry (perfektní blok) is the deepest addition — a real, testable mechanic in `combat/engine.ts`, not a cosmetic — and it's timing-based, not resource-based like Bulwark's shield.** `BojovnikStav` gained `blokDrzenMs` (how long the current block has been held *continuously*, growing every tick it's held and resetting to 0 the instant it isn't — including while busy, since a stunned or mid-swing fighter isn't blocking regardless of what the input says) and `parryZablesk` (a purely cosmetic countdown for the UI, decaying unconditionally like `komboKonci` already does). A hit landing while the defender's `blokDrzenMs <= PARRY_OKNO_MS` (150ms) is a "perfect block": zero damage, zero knockback, no hitstun for the defender — and the attacker is punished with `PARRY_TREST_MS` (500ms) of stun, deliberately longer than an ordinary `HITSTUN_MS` (200ms), so landing into a perfect parry is a worse outcome than just missing. This rewards *reactive* blocking — pressing block right as a hit arrives — over passive turtling: a block held from a distance for a long time (many ticks, `blokDrzenMs` well past the window before contact) is just an ordinary block by the time anything actually lands. **This is a real design tradeoff, not free** — it made several already-passing tests that used to combine "start blocking" and "attack lands" in the same zero-delta tick (a natural way to write a block test) now describe a *perfect* parry instead of an ordinary block, since a block that starts on the very tick it's needed is, by this feature's own definition, perfectly timed. Five such pre-existing tests across three files (`fighting-combat.test.ts`, `fighting-postavy.test.ts`, `fighting-specialy.test.ts`) were fixed — not weakened — by having the defender raise block a tick *earlier*, past the parry window, before the attack lands, the same "test the fix, don't assume the old number" discipline this file has applied to every prior test-breaking mechanic (combo's effect on Volt's double-hit test, before it).

**Comeback is the second engine addition, deliberately simple and orthogonal to parry — a flat damage multiplier for an attacker fighting from low HP, no timing involved at all.** `COMEBACK_PRAH` (30% of max HP) and `COMEBACK_NASOBIC` (1.2×) live next to parry's constants; the bonus is computed from the *attacker's own* HP fraction at the moment of the hit and applies to blocked and unblocked damage alike (unlike combo, which blocked hits get none of) — comeback is about the attacker's own desperation, not about the specific exchange landing clean. Both bonuses stack multiplicatively with combo when they co-occur, verified directly rather than assumed. `combat/loop.ts` gained the read-side pair, `jeParry`/`jeComeback`, the same "read a finished field, no side effects" shape as `komboAktivni`/`hpProcenta` before them.

**The intro "VS" screen and per-character KO finishers are the presentational half, both confined to `TvHost.tsx`/`Bojiste.tsx`/CSS — no engine involvement, matching this file's own standing boundary.** Once both slots are `pripraveno`, `TvHost.tsx` now shows a brief `INTRO_MS` (2.2s) "VS" screen — both fighters' portraits sliding in from opposite sides, a pulsing "VS" between them — and **genuinely delays** starting the match loop until it finishes (a second `useEffect` gated on a new `introAktivni` state, and the match-creation effect's own dependency array grew to `[pripraveno, introAktivni]`) rather than layering the intro as an overlay on top of a fight that's already quietly running underneath — the round timer, hit-stop and sound effects would otherwise all start ticking before the players ever see who they're facing. Per-character KO finishers replace the one shared fall-and-fade animation with four distinct ones, selected by combining `.souboj-bojovnik--ko` with a new `.souboj-bojovnik--postava-<id>` class (added to both `SoubojArena2D.tsx`/`SoubojArena3D.tsx`'s existing class-string construction) — Pyra flares and burns away, Bulwark sinks straight down under its own weight instead of falling backward like the others, Volt flickers out in stepped (not eased) motion matching its own "fastest of all" character trait, Onyx dissolves and shrinks into shadow rather than hitting the ground at all. All four are still hand-authored CSS `@keyframes` over the existing flat SVG figure (`PostavaGrafika.tsx`) — the project still has no 3D/animation pipeline for Souboj's cast, the same disclosed constraint as every earlier art pass for this game.

**Parry and comeback also needed their own visual language, reusing exactly the mechanisms this session already established rather than inventing new ones.** A perfect parry flashes a cyan "✋ PARRY!" badge next to the defender's name (same inline-badge shape as the combo indicator) and a matching cyan glow on their arena token's SVG, plus a new, distinct "cink" tone (`zahrajParry()`, a sharp triangle-wave ping — audibly different from the blunt square-wave `zahrajZasah()`) detected the same "catch the transition into a truthy state" way `zahrajSpecial()` already catches `posledniAkce` flipping to `'specialni'`. Comeback pulses a soft red ring around the low-HP fighter's header **and** around their arena token — but the token-level version needed a real fix mid-build: a first draft set `animation` directly on `.souboj-bojovnik` (the same element `--idle`/`--utok`/`--hitstun` already animate for their own state-driven motion), and since the `animation` shorthand fully replaces rather than merges with another `animation` declaration on the same element, comeback's own rule — declared later in the stylesheet — would have silently *cancelled* whichever movement animation the fighter's current combat state was already playing. Fixed by moving the comeback pulse onto the wrapper's own `::before` pseudo-element instead (a separate box, its own independent `animation`), the general lesson being the same one this file's CSS-cascade write-ups keep re-teaching: two states sharing one element can only coexist if they don't fight over the same shorthand property.

**A local, single-device mode (`LocalniZapas.tsx`) is the biggest addition of this pass — the one the user had originally declined earlier this session, now explicitly requested — and it needed zero new engine code, since `combat/engine.ts`/`combat/loop.ts` never cared where input came from in the first place.** Both players share one screen: sequential character selection (the exact same `VyberPostavy.tsx` reused twice, once per player, with no changes at all — it was already a controlled, prop-driven screen), an arena picker reusing the same pill row `TvHost.tsx`'s waiting screen already has, then a match screen that renders the *same* `Bojiste.tsx` every other Souboj mode uses (so hit-stop, parry, comeback, combo, screen shake, sound — all of it — work identically, free, with zero duplicated logic) plus two small on-screen control clusters, one per player, positioned at opposite corners of the same device. Movement uses a plain ◀/▶ button pair per player rather than `VirtualniJoystick.tsx` — a joystick sharing a half-screen with four more buttons for a second player has no room to be useful, and the engine only ever consumed discrete `'vlevo'/'vpravo'` anyway, never analog input. `network.ts` is never imported at all — this mode is genuinely offline, unlike everything else in Souboj, which this session's own `network.ts` header comment already flags as the one part of the game that *can't* work without the cloud.

**XP, credits and account-wide badges are deliberately NOT awarded for a local match, on purpose, while per-character match statistics still are.** A local match has no way to know which of the two people at the keyboard is "the account" — `secureStorage` is one shared per-device store, not per-player — so crediting XP/credits the same way a real two-device match does (`Ovladac.tsx`'s `konecZapasu` handler) would make "play yourself forever on one phone" a trivial, unlimited way to farm rewards meant to represent real matches against another connected player. `useSoubojStatistikyStore`'s win/loss/draw counters, by contrast, are purely cosmetic flavor with no reward value riding on them, so local mode still records those — the same account-wide, XP-bearing systems this file already treats as sensitive to grinding (`FEATURE_GATING_ENABLED`'s reasoning, the shop's "no purchase completes today" restraint) drew the actual line, not an arbitrary one.

**Extracting `HIT_STOP_MS` out of `TvHost.tsx` and into `combat/loop.ts` is this pass's one small architectural move, made the moment a second owner of a game loop (`LocalniZapas.tsx`) needed the identical constant.** `combat/loop.ts` already holds every other "sits between the pure engine and whichever screen owns the render loop" helper (`sestavVstup`, `vizualniStavBojovnika`, `zbyvaSekund`) — hit-stop's own timing constant belongs there for the same reason, not duplicated a second time or exported awkwardly from one page component into another. This is the same "promote to a shared module the moment a second caller needs it" rule this file already states for `core/utils/notify.ts` (Pomodoro → Planer) and for state/hooks generally, just at a much smaller scale.

**The graphics/performance half of the ask landed as real instancing in `useSoubojScene.ts`'s 3D arena, not a cosmetic tweak.** The scene's decorative trees/rocks used to be 26 fully independent `THREE.Mesh` objects, each with its own freshly-allocated geometry *and* material even though every tree in one arena shares the same colors and every rock the same base shape — 26 separate draw calls, doubled to 52 because the split-screen scene already renders twice per frame (once per camera). They're now built as a small, fixed number of `THREE.InstancedMesh` objects (one for tree trunks, one for tree crowns, one for rocks, per arena that actually has decorations) sharing one geometry and one material each, with per-instance position/rotation/scale baked into a transform matrix — the same real, measurable "fewer draw calls, same visual result" win this file's own RPG sections have never needed to make (Buddyheim's world has no comparable decoration density), verified by reading the resulting scene graph rather than guessed at. Cleanup needed no changes at all: `InstancedMesh` is a `Mesh` subclass, so the existing `scene.traverse` disposal loop (`geometry.dispose()`/`material.dispose()`) already covers it correctly. A second, smaller change disables MSAA (`antialias`) whenever `devicePixelRatio` is above 1 — a high-DPR phone's own supersampling already smooths edges most of the way there, so explicit antialiasing on top is a close-to-redundant cost paid twice per frame for the same split-screen reason as the draw-call count above.

**Local mode turned out to be the one part of Souboj this session could finally verify with a real, live Playwright screenshot of the actual 3D arena — every earlier phase's own "real phone-to-TV pairing has not been verified" caveat was specifically about the WebSocket wall this sandbox's proxy has never supported, and local mode is the first Souboj surface with no WebSocket in it at all.** A temporary e2e spec (deleted after passing) drove the real built app end-to-end — pick both characters, pick an arena, start the match — and screenshotted the live, running split-screen 3D scene: both cameras rendering correctly, the instanced trees/rocks/path/water all present and coherent, both fighters visible from each camera's own framing, the round timer and HP bars ticking, both control clusters rendered. This is the first time this session has had eyes-on confirmation of the 3D arena actually working end-to-end through the real app, rather than through a fabricated `SoubojStav` and a debug query param.

**Verified by 9 new permanent unit tests (141 in the project now, up from 132 — 5 for parry, 4 for comeback) in `tests/unit/fighting-combat.test.ts`, plus fixes to 3 pre-existing tests across two more files that parry's own timing rules reinterpreted, all in the same "no browser needed" tier every `combat/engine.ts` addition has used since Fáze 1.** Two temporary component tests (deleted after passing) covered what only exists in components: the comeback header class appearing/disappearing with HP, the parry badge rendering only while `parryZablesk` is live, the per-character KO class combination actually reaching the DOM (through the real 3D→2D fallback path, same as every earlier component test in this game), and — the one genuinely new verification shape this pass used — a full local-mode run (`FightingModule` → pick "Hrát lokálně" → both character picks → arena pick → a real running match with two control clusters) exercised with **zero mocking of the network layer**, since local mode has none to mock. A temporary e2e spec (deleted after passing) confirmed the same flow through the real production build with a real screenshot, described above. `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` all clean; `FightingModule`'s own lazy chunk grew to ~40 KB JS / ~13 KB gzip (local mode's UI plus the intro screen), `three.module`'s shared chunk unchanged in size (confirming the instancing change reused the existing dependency graph rather than adding to it); `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**A seventh pass answered "any more improvements for the 2-player game?" with eight ideas offered in plain text (not `AskUserQuestion`, since it was an open brainstorm, not a fork to choose between) — the reply was "yeah, do all of it," so all eight shipped in one pass: training mode, selectable match length, sudden death on a tied timeout, environmental arena hazards, a mana-regen handicap, unlockable cosmetic character variants, a post-match recap, and a quick emote/taunt button.** Four of the eight (training, sudden death, hazard, handicap) needed real `combat/engine.ts` changes and therefore a new match-wide settings object; the other four (best-of-N, recap, cosmetic variants, emotes) are screen-level or presentation-only.

**`SoubojMoznosti` (`combat/types.ts`) is the one new shape that made training/handicap/hazard possible without four independent ad-hoc mechanisms — a small object chosen once on the pre-match screen (the same screen arena selection already lives on) and carried unchanged inside `SoubojStav` for the whole match, read by `krokSouboje` every tick.** `treninkovyRezim: boolean`, `handicapManaRegen: [number, number]`, and `hazardOkraju: boolean` are the three fields; `VYCHOZI_MOZNOSTI` (all off, `[1,1]`) is what every pre-existing `vytvorSoubojStav()` call (including all 141 already-shipped unit tests) gets when the new fifth argument is omitted — confirmed by running the whole existing suite unmodified before writing a single new test, the same "prove the addition is additive" discipline this file already applied when characters were bolted onto Fáze 1's tests.

**Training mode is the simplest of the four and needed no new state at all, only a short-circuit inside `krokSouboje` — HP is clamped to a floor of 1 (never 0, so no KO can ever fire) and the entire time-limit/sudden-death branch is skipped outright, so a training round can literally never reach `stavKola: 'konec'`.** Leaving is therefore always the back button, never a "match over" screen — the same reasoning `Bojiste.tsx`'s new `souboj-casovac--trenink` badge (🎯 TRÉNINK, replacing the countdown pill in the exact same slot) exists for: there's no countdown to show when nothing is counting down. Both `TvHost.tsx`'s and `LocalniZapas.tsx`'s pre-match screens hide the best-of-N and handicap pickers entirely once training is toggled on — neither setting means anything if the round can't end.

**Best-of-N replaces what used to be a hardcoded `VITEZSTVI_NA_ZAPAS = 2` constant in `TvHost.tsx` with a chosen `pocetNaVyhru` (1/2/3, labelled "Na 1 kolo"/"Bo3"/"Bo5") — and, because `LocalniZapas.tsx` had never had multi-round scoring at all (only a single round + "Hrát znovu"), this pass gave it the identical `skore`/`skoreRef`/`zacniKolo`/`dalsiKolo`/`novyZapas` shape `TvHost.tsx` already established, not a second, different scoring scheme.** This also fixed a real inconsistency that best-of-N surfaced rather than caused: `LocalniZapas.tsx` used to call `useSoubojStatistikyStore`'s `zaznamenejVysledek()` once per individual round; now that a "match" can be more than one round, it records exactly once, when the whole match is decided — matching `Ovladac.tsx`'s own network-side convention (`oznamKonecZapasu` only ever fires once per match, never per round) that this file already documented when the RPG's own scoring shipped.

**Sudden death answers what used to happen on an exact HP tie at the time limit — used to be a flat draw, and is now a real escalation, not just a re-roll.** `krokSouboje`'s time-limit branch, on an exact tie, no longer ends the round: it sets `stav.suddenDeath = true` and `suddenDeathOd = stav.cas`, and the round keeps running under one new rule — the instant HP diverges by even one point (a blocked hit still lets some damage through), whoever has more HP wins immediately, no further time check needed. Damage during sudden death is multiplied by a genuinely escalating factor (`SUDDEN_DEATH_NASOBIC_ZACATEK` 1.5× at the start, growing `SUDDEN_DEATH_NASOBIC_NARUST_ZA_S` (0.15) per second sudden death has run, capped at `SUDDEN_DEATH_NASOBIC_STROP` (3×) — the same "cap it, don't let a long series compound forever" restraint `KOMBO_MAX_STUPNU` already established) — chosen specifically because two players who spent a whole round without anyone landing a decisive hit are the exact case a flat draw used to leave unsatisfying, and a fight neither side commits to could otherwise stall forever even with the multiplier, an accepted edge case flagged in the code rather than solved with a second timer nobody asked for. `Bojiste.tsx` swaps the countdown pill for a pulsing red "⚔️ NÁHLÁ SMRT" badge in the same slot training's own badge already claims — the three states (countdown/training/sudden death) are mutually exclusive by construction, so there's only ever one badge to show.

**Environmental hazards are a genuine per-arena property, not a global rule — only "Pouštní kaňon" (`pousty`) carries `Arena.nebezpeciOkraje: true`, because a canyon's edges are the one place in this game's three arenas where "falling off" has any real meaning; the grassy plain and the midnight arena stay hazard-free.** `HAZARD_OKRAJE_PRAH` (24 logical units) defines how close to either wall a knockback has to land to count as "at the edge," and `HAZARD_OKRAJE_POSKOZENI` (8, flat, not scaled by any character or combo multiplier — deliberately, per `efektivniAkceData`'s own standing "knockback is a property of the action, not the character" precedent) is added directly to the delivered damage inside `aplikujJedenZasah`, computed from where the hit's own knockback actually pushed the defender, not from the attack's strength. Both arena pickers (`TvHost.tsx`, `LocalniZapas.tsx`) show a small ⚠️ next to "Pouštní kaňon" so players know before picking it, and `sestavMoznosti()` on both screens reads `ARENY[arenaId].nebezpeciOkraje` directly — the arena choice and the hazard flag can never drift apart, since one is derived from the other every time a match starts.

**The handicap is deliberately a tempo advantage, not a power advantage — faster mana regeneration for a chosen player (`handicapManaRegen`, a per-player multiplier defaulting to 1, bumped to `HANDICAP_MANA_NASOBIC` (1.75) for whichever slot is picked), never a change to damage, HP, or defense.** This was a real design choice, not the only option available: reduced HP loss was considered and explicitly not built, because a damage-reduction handicap changes the outcome of every single exchange, while a mana-regen handicap only ever changes *when* the weaker player's special becomes available — a real help that still leaves every hit meaning the same thing for both players. Both pre-match screens offer "Bez handicapu"/"Hráč 1"/"Hráč 2" as three mutually exclusive pills, same shape as arena/best-of-N selection.

**Unlockable cosmetic variants are the one item this pass deliberately scoped down from what a literal reading might imply, and that restraint is documented in `kosmetika.ts` itself, not left implicit.** A variant choice is a **preview shown only inside `VyberPostavy.tsx`'s own character-select card** — it never reaches the match, the network payload, or either arena renderer. Threading it further would have meant changing `PripojitPayload`/`network.ts` (a wire-format change for a purely decorative choice) and re-touching `Bojiste.tsx`/`SoubojArena2D.tsx`/`SoubojArena3D.tsx`'s already-established prop chains for something with zero gameplay effect — a real cost for a preview-only payoff. What ships instead: `useSoubojStatistikyStore`'s existing win/loss/draw counters (already summed for the "📊 Statistiky" screen) answer "how many matches has this character been played" for free, no new counter needed; `ZAPASU_PRO_ODEMKNUTI_ZLATE` (5) is the threshold, and one shared black-and-gold "prestige" palette (`PALETA_ZLATA` in `PostavaGrafika.tsx`) replaces a character's normal colors when its preview toggle is on — one palette for all four characters, not four bespoke ones, since the accessory/silhouette per character (drawn from `postavaId`, unaffected by the palette swap) already carries the "which hero is this" signal; the recolor only needs to communicate "this one is dressed up." A locked card shows "🔒 Zlatá za *N* zápasů" computed from the same counter, counting down as more matches are played.

**The post-match recap is genuinely accumulated across the whole match, not just the final round — `combat/loop.ts`'s new `StatistikyZapasu`/`aktualizujStatistikyZapasu()` pair is a pure function taking the previous and current `SoubojStav` plus a running accumulator, called once per tick from both `TvHost.tsx` and `LocalniZapas.tsx`'s own game loops, the same "sits between the pure engine and whichever screen owns the loop" role `sestavVstup`/`vizualniStavBojovnika` already have in that file.** Three numbers per player: longest combo ever reached (`komboAktivni()`'s own max, not just its final value — a combo that peaked at ×4 and later dropped back to ×1 still shows ×4), total damage-dealing hits landed (derived the same "the other side's HP went down" signal `Bojiste.tsx`'s own hit-flash effect already uses), and perfect parries performed (the same `jeParry()` transition-into-true edge `Bojiste.tsx`'s parry sound/badge already detects). The accumulator resets on a brand-new match (`novyZapas()`) but *not* between rounds of the same match (`dalsiKolo()`) — a recap is meant to summarize the whole best-of-N, not just whichever round happened to end it. It renders as a small card above the "Nový zápas" button, shown only once the whole match — not just one round — is decided.

**The quick emote/taunt is the smallest of the eight, and it's the first genuinely new broadcast event `network.ts` has grown since the project's Fáze 0 — `EmotePayload` (`{hracId, emote}`), one-directional phone→TV exactly like `VstupPayload` already is, since the TV is the only screen that ever needs to show it.** `RYCHLE_EMOTE` (`types.ts`) is a fixed three-emoji set (👍😂😤) — the same "pevná sada, ne libovolný vstup" restraint this app already applies to message reactions and group icons elsewhere in the codebase, not a free-text taunt. `Ovladac.tsx` gained a small button row above the joystick; `TvHost.tsx`'s `hostujMistnost` handler resolves which slot sent it (via `hraciRef`, not stale `hraci` state — the same "handler needs the freshest ref, not a render-time closure" pattern the rest of this component already follows) and shows it as a small popping bubble under that player's name in `Bojiste.tsx` for 2.5 seconds, auto-clearing via a plain `setTimeout` (with the previous timeout explicitly cleared first, so two fast taunts from the same player don't fight over whose timer wins). `LocalniZapas.tsx` needed no network at all for the identical feature — each local cluster gets its own emote row, and pressing one just sets the shared `emoty` state directly, the same visual result with no broadcast round-trip since both "players" are already on the one screen that would show it.

**Verified with `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` all clean, following the same split this file has used at every earlier Souboj pass: real permanent unit tests for anything in `combat/engine.ts`/`combat/loop.ts`, temporary component tests (deleted after passing) for anything only reachable by rendering.** `tests/unit/fighting-combat.test.ts` gained one reinterpreted test (an exact-HP tie at the time limit now asserts `suddenDeath: true` and `stavKola: 'probiha'`, not the old flat draw — fixed the same "test the new intended behavior, don't weaken the assertion" way every earlier test-breaking mechanic in this file was handled) plus 7 new cases (sudden death resolving on any HP divergence, its damage multiplier's floor, a tied sudden death correctly *not* forcing a resolution with no hits landed, training's HP floor holding through 50 ticks of mutual specials, the mana handicap actually widening the mana gap, and hazard damage firing only with `hazardOkraju` true and only when the knockback actually lands at the wall). `tests/unit/fighting-loop.test.ts` gained 5 new cases for `aktualizujStatistikyZapasu` (the `predchozi === null` no-op returning the same object reference, hit attribution to the correct attacker, combo tracking the running maximum rather than the latest value, perfect-parry attribution, and confirming the function returns a new object rather than mutating its input) — one of these caught a real test-authoring mistake before trusting it: a too-short delta between two attacks left the attacker still in recovery, so the "second hit" never actually landed and the combo never reached 2 — fixed by giving the attacker enough time between hits to clear `AKCE_DATA.udar`'s own `trvaniMs`, not by loosening the assertion. A temporary component-test file (`tests/components/`, created, exercised, deleted — the tier stays "still-unbuilt" as a committed fixture, per this file's own standing description) covered what only exists in rendering: the training/sudden-death badges appearing in Bojiste's countdown slot, an emote bubble rendering for the correct player, `VyberPostavy.tsx`'s lock text vs. its unlockable toggle (seeded via `useSoubojStatistikyStore.setState`), a full `LocalniZapas.tsx` flow (both character picks → settings screen with the hazard warning visible → training hiding best-of-N/handicap → a running match with a score bar and a working local emote button), and `TvHost.tsx`'s pre-match settings screen with `network.ts` mocked the same way this file's own earlier component tests already mock it (capturing the handlers passed to `hostujMistnost` rather than opening a real channel). 153 permanent unit tests in the project (up from 141), `FightingModule`'s own lazy chunk grew to ~49 KB JS / ~15 KB gzip, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

### Form Check (`src/miniapps/form-check/`)

A rep counter that watches the camera and counts squats via on-device pose detection — `@mediapipe/tasks-vision` (PoseLandmarker, WASM), no server ever sees the video. It's the template for "heavy on-device ML asset" if another miniapp needs one:

**The model and WASM runtime are self-hosted under `public/mediapipe/`, not fetched from Google's CDN**, so the app never depends on an external host staying up — same reasoning as "runs completely without the cloud" elsewhere in this file. That's ~28 MB (a 5.5 MB model, plus a SIMD WASM build and a no-SIMD fallback for older browsers) and it must **never** reach the install precache — nobody who skips this miniapp should download it. `vite.config.ts`'s `globIgnores` excludes `mediapipe/**`, and a `CacheFirst` `runtimeCaching` route fetches it lazily the first time Form Check actually opens, then serves it from the browser's cache offline after. Before assuming a library needs every file it ships, check what it actually requests over the network — the package here ships a third WASM variant (`vision_wasm_module_internal.*`) that `FilesetResolver` never asks for, confirmed by watching requests; it never went into the repo, saving another ~12 MB for nothing.

The camera + ML pipeline lives entirely outside React, same ownership shape as Social's `useAmbientScene.ts`: `usePoseEngine.ts` owns the `<video>`, the MediaStream, the `PoseLandmarker` instance and a `requestAnimationFrame` loop, and only pushes rep count / status / feedback into React state. Cleanup on unmount stops every media track and calls `.close()` on the landmarker — skip that and the camera keeps recording in the background after the user navigates away.
